82/// Signals hermit takes from the guest's namespace, and what a guest loses.
83///
84/// ⚠️ ENUMERATED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY READING (2026-08-25). Every signal 1..64
85/// was run under two delivery paths -- self-directed `raise` and a sibling
86/// thread's `pthread_kill` -- with a native run as the control for each. Exactly
87/// TWO differ from native, and they fail in different ways at different points:
88///
89///   SIGSTKFLT (16)  `rt_sigaction` is NO-OPED below, so the handler is never
90///                   installed and the default disposition terminates the guest:
91///                   observed exit 144 (= 128 + 16).
92///   SIGTRAP    (5)  `rt_sigaction` passes through and the handler IS installed,
93///                   but ptrace consumes every SIGTRAP (syscall stops, seccomp
94///                   stops, breakpoints), so the handler never runs. ⚠️ THE GUEST
95///                   THEN EXITS 0 WITH NO DIAGNOSTIC AT ALL -- a clean pass that
96///                   behaved differently from native, which no cell can catch.
97///
98/// Everything else in 1..31 matches native exactly; 9/19 and 32/33 refuse
99/// `sigaction` natively too and are not hermit's. Realtime 34..64 are
100/// delivered and handled like any other signal under ptrace
101/// (<https://github.com/rrnewton/reverie/issues/207>); they are not
102/// appropriation.
103const APPROPRIATED_SIGNALS: [(i32, &str); 2] = [
104    (
105        libc::SIGTRAP,
106        "ptrace consumes every SIGTRAP (syscall/seccomp stops, breakpoints)",
107    ),
108    (
109        libc::SIGSTKFLT,
110        "reverie uses it as PERF_EVENT_SIGNAL, the PMU preemption timer",
111    ),
112];
113
114/// Say, once per installation, that a guest handler will never run.
115///
116/// ⚠️ WHY A DIAGNOSTIC AND NOT A REFUSAL. Returning `EINVAL` from `sigaction`
117/// was considered and deliberately rejected for SIGSTKFLT -- see the comment at
118/// the no-op below: the Go runtime registers that handler, and refusing would
119/// break every Go guest at startup. That reasoning generalises: installing a
120/// handler defensively is common, actually raising these signals is rare, so
121/// refusal breaks MORE programs than the current behaviour. The contract
122/// question of what a guest is owed here is genuinely open.
123///
124/// What is NOT open is that hermit currently says NOTHING. This line commits to
125/// no policy, breaks no conforming program, and turns a silent wrong answer into
126/// a visible one -- the same reasoning as the `HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE` marker.
127/// The decision, separated from the reporting so a test can exercise THIS and
128/// not a copy of it. A unit test that re-implements a predicate keeps passing
129/// when the real one is gutted -- measured in this project's own pipe work,
130/// where deleting the production wiring left every unit test green.
131fn appropriated_reason(signum: i32, handler: u64) -> Option<&'static str> {
132    // SIG_DFL (0) and SIG_IGN (1) lose nothing: the guest is not asking to be
133    // called back, so there is no expectation to disappoint.
134    if handler <= 1 {
135        return None;
136    }
137    APPROPRIATED_SIGNALS
138        .iter()
139        .find(|(s, _)| *s == signum)
140        .map(|(_, why)| *why)
141}
142
143fn warn_appropriated_signal(signum: i32, handler: u64) {
144    if let Some(why) = appropriated_reason(signum, handler) {
145        tracing::warn!(
146            "HERMIT_APPROPRIATED_SIGNAL signum={signum} effect=handler-installed-but-never-invoked reason={why}"
147        );
148    }
149}
150
151// NB: the kernel uses an eight-byte signal mask in its raw signal syscalls on
152// x86_64. `libc::sigset_t` is the 128-byte userspace wrapper type and must not
153// be used to access these buffers. See:
154// https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/latest/source/include/uapi/asm-generic/signal.h#L75
155fn validate_kernel_sigset_size(sigsetsize: usize) -> Result<(), Errno> {
156    if sigsetsize == KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE {
157        Ok(())
158    } else {
159        Err(Errno::EINVAL)
160    }
161}
162
163/// The mask Linux installs when a call names `mask` as the signal mask to sleep
164/// under (`rt_sigsuspend`, `ppoll`, `pselect6`): `set_current_blocked` drops
165/// `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`, which can never be blocked.
166pub(crate) fn kernel_installed_signal_mask(mask: KernelSigset) -> KernelSigset {
167    let unblockable = (1_u64 << (libc::SIGKILL - 1)) | (1_u64 << (libc::SIGSTOP - 1));
168    mask & !unblockable
169}
170
171fn without_perf_event_signal(mask: KernelSigset) -> KernelSigset {
172    let bit = (reverie::PERF_EVENT_SIGNAL as u32) - 1;
173    mask & !(1_u64 << bit)
174}
175
176/// The result of a kernel-access probe that is a call's first injection,
177/// made before anything of the call happens (`read_kernel_sigset`,
178/// `read_kernel_sigaction`, pselect6's wrapper validation), with the
179/// backend's not-run report, `-ERESTARTSYS` (which no probe returns
180/// itself), turned into `-ERESTARTNOINTR`. The report means a signal reached
181/// the thread at the call's entry and stopped the probe before its `syscall`
182/// instruction; the backend holds it. The call then restarts once the signal
183/// is delivered, as if the signal had come before the call: its handler runs
184/// under the thread's own mask, and the call runs. Ending the call with the
185/// not-run report instead returned EINTR, from a wait whose temporary mask
186/// the handler never saw and from calls that never fail with EINTR
187/// (`rt_sigprocmask`, `rt_sigaction`), which no Linux run produces
188/// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4526).
189///
190/// Only for a call's first injection. A probe after an earlier injection
191/// keeps the not-run report, since a restart would repeat that injection
192/// (pselect6's mask read after its wrapper validation and its observation of
193/// a pending real-time timer expiration, for example), and so does one after
194/// a scheduler request, which changes scheduler state (a record/replay
195/// zero-timeout ppoll). Where an earlier phase injects only sometimes, the
196/// caller says whether it did or can: epoll_pwait and epoll_pwait2's
197/// observation (`observe_under_temporary_mask`, which reports whether it
198/// injected), and a run-mode zero-timeout ppoll's publication of virtual
199/// timerfd expirations, which can inject only for a process holding an armed
200/// virtual timerfd. Record/replay pselect6's entry read is always the call's
201/// first injection (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4526).
202pub(crate) fn restart_if_first_probe_not_run<V>(result: Result<V, Error>) -> Result<V, Error> {
203    match result {
204        Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ERESTARTSYS)) => Err(Errno::ERESTARTNOINTR.into()),
205        other => other,
206    }
207}
208
209/// Read one raw kernel signal mask while preserving the kernel's user-access check.
210///
211/// `safeptrace::Stopped::read` deliberately uses `PTRACE_PEEKDATA` for reads of
212/// eight bytes or less. That operation can read a `PROT_NONE` page, unlike the
213/// kernel's `copy_from_user`, so using `MemoryAccess::read_value` alone would
214/// turn an `EFAULT` from a raw signal syscall into success. An invalid `how`
215/// value makes `rt_sigprocmask` copy exactly the kernel-sized input and then
216/// return `EINVAL` without changing the mask. Use that as a permission probe,
217/// then read the already-validated word while the guest is stopped.
218/// The red zone below the stack pointer, which a leaf function may use and an
219/// injected call therefore must not.
220pub(super) const STACK_RED_ZONE: usize = crate::arch::STACK_RED_ZONE;
221
222/// The two eight-byte scratch cells `handle_rt_sigsuspend` places below the
223/// red zone: the rt_sigpending result and the copy of the call's mask.
224const SCRATCH_CELLS_SIZE: usize = 2 * std::mem::size_of::<u64>();
225
226pub(super) async fn read_kernel_sigset<G, T>(
227    guest: &mut G,
228    address: Addr<'_, libc::sigset_t>,
229) -> Result<KernelSigset, Error>
230where
231    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
232    T: RecordOrReplay,
233{
234    let validation = syscalls::RtSigprocmask::new()
235        .with_how(-1)
236        .with_set(Some(address))
237        .with_oldset(None)
238        .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE);
239    match guest.inject(validation).await {
240        Err(Errno::EINVAL) => {}
241        Err(errno) => return Err(errno.into()),
242        Ok(_) => {
243            // Both Linux and the KVM syscall implementation reject an unknown
244            // operation. Success means the backend did not validate the probe.
245            return Err(Errno::EIO.into());
246        }
247    }
248    Ok(guest.memory().read_value(address.cast())?)
249}
250
251/// Whether a thread-directed SIGKILL (`tgkill` or `tkill`) names `tid`, a
252/// thread-group leader that has exited while a sibling runs on. Linux keeps
253/// such a leader as a zombie, and a SIGKILL that names it does not end its
254/// group: `complete_signal` finds that the exiting leader does not want the
255/// signal, and a thread-directed signal goes to no other thread. So the send
256/// is not reserved as a group kill; it takes the ordinary path, and the
257/// kernel answers it as Linux does, with no effect. Only a leader the
258/// scheduler retired at its own `exit` counts: it commits no other turn until
259/// the kernel shows such a leader a zombie, so by this turn it is one, in
260/// every run (`Scheduler::names_a_retired_exited_leader`). A leader that left
261/// the scheduler at a host-timed deregistration, as on DBT, does not
262/// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4445).
263pub(crate) async fn sigkill_names_an_exited_leader<G, T>(guest: &mut G, tid: DetTid) -> bool
264where
265    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
266    T: RecordOrReplay,
267{
268    names_a_retired_exited_leader(guest, tid).await
269}
270
271/// Signal phase 1: the guest's SIGALRM disposition (a guest handler or not)
272/// and SIGALRM blocked bit, as its runtime keeps them virtual, read back with
273/// a query `rt_sigaction` and `rt_sigprocmask` that the runtime answers from
274/// its virtual state.
275async fn virtual_sigalrm_state<G, T>(guest: &mut G) -> Result<(bool, bool), Error>
276where
277    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
278    T: RecordOrReplay,
279{
280    let state = read_sigalrm_sleep_state(guest).await?;
281    Ok((
282        state.disposition == SigalrmSleepDisposition::Handled,
283        state.blocked,
284    ))
285}
286
287/// Query through the runtime, which reports the guest's action and mask even
288/// when it owns a physical SIGALRM handler. These queries run in an owned turn;
289/// a scheduler-side read of the callback's physical mask is not a substitute.
290async fn read_sigalrm_sleep_state<G, T>(guest: &mut G) -> Result<InGuestSleepSigalrm, Error>
291where
292    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
293    T: RecordOrReplay,
294{
295    let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
296    let action = stack.reserve::<KernelSigaction>();
297    let mask = stack.reserve::<KernelSigset>();
298    let _stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
299    guest
300        .inject(
301            syscalls::RtSigaction::new()
302                .with_signum(libc::SIGALRM)
303                .with_action(None)
304                .with_old_action(Some(action.cast()))
305                .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE),
306        )
307        .await?;
308    guest
309        .inject(
310            syscalls::RtSigprocmask::new()
311                .with_how(libc::SIG_BLOCK)
312                .with_set(None)
313                .with_oldset(Some(mask.cast()))
314                .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE),
315        )
316        .await?;
317    let action: KernelSigaction = guest.memory().read_value(action)?;
318    let mask: KernelSigset = guest.memory().read_value(mask)?;
319    let disposition = if action.handler == libc::SIG_DFL as u64 {
320        SigalrmSleepDisposition::Default
321    } else if action.handler == libc::SIG_IGN as u64 {
322        SigalrmSleepDisposition::Ignored
323    } else {
324        SigalrmSleepDisposition::Handled
325    };
326    Ok(InGuestSleepSigalrm {
327        disposition,
328        blocked: mask & kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGALRM) != 0,
329    })
330}
331
332/// A5 step 3c.1d, design D37 rule (iii): before an `rt_sigaction` that moves
333/// `signum` away from ignored (its action now `SIG_IGN`, or `SIG_DFL` for a
334/// default-ignored signal with no handler; the new `handler` neither) runs,
335/// remove the pending ignored signals the guest's mask at this request
336/// (`M_req`, which governs next too) lets in, `I ∖ M_req`
337/// (`ignored_signals::removable`). Linux discarded each of them when it was
338/// sent, since an unblocked ignored signal is never queued (`sig_ignored`,
339/// kernel/signal.c:104-120 at v6.12), but the in-guest runtime's mask held it
340/// queued; once the new action is installed (`do_sigaction`, :4170-4233) it
341/// would be delivered at the return. A blocked one stays: Linux queued it
342/// too, and delivers it under the new action once unblocked. Under the gate
343/// only; one `/proc` read, and injections only for instances to remove.
344async fn remove_ignored_before_unignore<G, T>(
345    guest: &mut G,
346    signum: i32,
347    handler: u64,
348) -> Result<(), Error>
349where
350    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
351    T: RecordOrReplay,
352{
353    if !(1..=64).contains(&signum) || signum == libc::SIGKILL || signum == libc::SIGSTOP {
354        return Ok(());
355    }
356    let bit = kernel_sigset_bit(signum);
357    let stays_ignored = handler == libc::SIG_IGN as u64
358        || (handler == libc::SIG_DFL as u64
359            && crate::syscalls::threads::default_ignored_signals() & bit != 0);
360    if stays_ignored {
361        return Ok(());
362    }
363    let state = read_thread_wait_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())?;
364    if crate::syscalls::ignored_at_generation(state.ignored, state.caught) & bit == 0 {
365        return Ok(());
366    }
367    let own = crate::syscalls::helpers::gated_own_mask(guest, state.blocked);
368    let removed = crate::ignored_signals::removable(
369        &state,
370        own,
371        own,
372        guest.config().in_guest_reserved_signals,
373    );
374    crate::syscalls::helpers::remove_ignored_signals(guest, removed).await
375}
376
377/// Take one pending copy of `signo` from the calling thread's queues, its own
378/// first and then its process's shared one, as Linux dequeues it, with a
379/// zero-timeout `rt_sigtimedwait` injected while the thread holds its turn.
380/// Returns the signal taken, or the call's error (EAGAIN: none was pending).
381pub(crate) async fn take_pending_signal<G, T>(guest: &mut G, signo: i32) -> Result<i64, Error>
382where
383    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
384    T: RecordOrReplay,
385{
386    let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
387    let set = stack.push(kernel_sigset_bit(signo));
388    let timeout = stack.push(Timespec {
389        tv_sec: 0,
390        tv_nsec: 0,
391    });
392    let stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
393    // SAFETY: the kernel only reads the set; Reverie types the argument as
394    // mutable.
395    let set = unsafe { set.cast::<libc::sigset_t>().into_mut() };
396    let taken = guest
397        .inject(
398            syscalls::RtSigtimedwait::new()
399                .with_set(Some(set))
400                .with_info(None)
401                .with_timeout(Some(timeout))
402                .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE),
403        )
404        .await;
405    drop(stack_guard);
406    Ok(taken?)
407}
408
409/// Claim a pending `SIGCHLD` for the calling thread, in its own turn, so that
410/// it is delivered to this thread as it next resumes and no other thread can
411/// take it. Returns whether there was one.
412///
413/// A harmless `getpid` is injected. Reverie runs an injected call by stepping
414/// the thread through user mode, so while a signal is pending and unblocked the
415/// kernel dequeues it at that step, Reverie holds it for delivery as the thread
416/// resumes, and the injection returns a restart errno instead of running. The
417/// caller calls this only while SIGCHLD is the signal the kernel dequeues next,
418/// so that held signal is the copy: claimed. If an outside thread took the copy
419/// first, nothing is pending, the call runs, and nothing is claimed. The call
420/// consumes no signal itself, so a stop that ends it can lose nothing (the Codex
421/// review of a version that took the copy with `rt_sigtimedwait` and queued it
422/// again).
423pub(crate) async fn claim_pending_sigchld<G, T>(guest: &mut G) -> Result<bool, Error>
424where
425    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
426    T: RecordOrReplay,
427{
428    match guest.inject(syscalls::Getpid::new()).await {
429        Ok(_) => Ok(false),
430        Err(errno) if is_restart_errno(errno) => Ok(true),
431        Err(errno) => Err(errno.into()),
432    }
433}
434
435/// Test-only fault hook, like `HERMIT_TEST_TIMERFD_FAULT`, acting once per run
436/// at the first claim of a `SIGCHLD` (`claim_pending_sigchld`), for the orders
437/// of https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4346 that host timing alone
438/// gives too rarely to test:
439/// - `usr1-before-claim`: just before the claim, Detcore sends the claiming
440///   thread SIGUSR1 from its own process (an injected send would itself
441///   dequeue the pending SIGCHLD), so the thread's own queue holds a lower
442///   signal and the claim takes SIGUSR1 instead;
443/// - `usr1-after-claim`: just after the claim, Detcore sends the claiming
444///   thread SIGUSR1, which a guest that blocks it leaves pending, so the
445///   backend passes the claimed SIGCHLD on unreported;
446/// - `drop-claimed`: the claimed copy is dropped, as the ledger drops a
447///   duplicate (`Detcore::child_exit_sigchld_delivers`), at its delivery stop
448///   or where the backend passes it on unreported
449///   (`Tool::filter_unreported_signal`), so no handler runs and the sleep
450///   restarts: a relative `nanosleep` through the kernel's `restart_syscall`
451///   (`helpers::RestartCall::Nanosleep`).
452///
453/// Unset, nothing changes.
454const CLAIM_FAULT_ENV: &str = "HERMIT_TEST_SIGCHLD_CLAIM_FAULT";
455
456/// Whether the claim fault hook has fired in this process. It fires once.
457static CLAIM_FAULT_FIRED: std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool = std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool::new(false);
458
459/// A fault `HERMIT_TEST_SIGCHLD_CLAIM_FAULT` asks for.
460#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
461pub(crate) enum ClaimFault {
462    Usr1BeforeClaim,
463    Usr1AfterClaim,
464    DropClaimed,
465}
466
467/// Whether the hook asks for `fault` now: at most once per process.
468pub(crate) fn take_claim_fault(fault: ClaimFault) -> bool {
469    let asked = match std::env::var(CLAIM_FAULT_ENV).as_deref() {
470        Ok("usr1-before-claim") => ClaimFault::Usr1BeforeClaim,
471        Ok("usr1-after-claim") => ClaimFault::Usr1AfterClaim,
472        Ok("drop-claimed") => ClaimFault::DropClaimed,
473        _ => return false,
474    };
475    asked == fault && !CLAIM_FAULT_FIRED.swap(true, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst)
476}
477
478/// For `usr1-before-claim` or `usr1-after-claim` (`fault`,
479/// `take_claim_fault`): send the claiming thread SIGUSR1 from Detcore's
480/// process, which shares the guest's pid namespace.
481fn send_claim_fault_signal<G, T>(guest: &G, fault: ClaimFault)
482where
483    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
484    T: RecordOrReplay,
485{
486    if take_claim_fault(fault) {
487        // SAFETY: tgkill only sends a signal.
488        let sent = unsafe {
489            libc::syscall(
490                libc::SYS_tgkill,
491                guest.pid().as_raw(),
492                guest.tid().as_raw(),
493                libc::SIGUSR1,
494            )
495        };
496        if sent == 0 {
497            tracing::warn!("claim fault hook: sent SIGUSR1 {:?}", fault);
498        } else {
499            tracing::error!(
500                "claim fault hook: could not send SIGUSR1 {:?}: {}",
501                fault,
502                std::io::Error::last_os_error()
503            );
504        }
505    }
506}
507
508/// Whether `errno` is a restart errno, which an injection returns when a signal
509/// stop ended it before it ran (`claim_pending_sigchld`).
510fn is_restart_errno(errno: Errno) -> bool {
511    matches!(
512        errno,
513        Errno::ERESTARTSYS
514            | Errno::ERESTARTNOINTR
515            | Errno::ERESTARTNOHAND
516            | Errno::ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK
517    )
518}
519
520/// The guest's virtual SIGALRM blocked bit, read from the runtime (which
521/// answers the query from its virtual state).
522pub(crate) async fn virtual_sigalrm_blocked<G, T>(guest: &mut G) -> Result<bool, Error>
523where
524    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
525    T: RecordOrReplay,
526{
527    let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
528    let mask = stack.reserve::<KernelSigset>();
529    let _stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
530    guest
531        .inject(
532            syscalls::RtSigprocmask::new()
533                .with_how(libc::SIG_BLOCK)
534                .with_set(None)
535                .with_oldset(Some(mask.cast()))
536                .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE),
537        )
538        .await?;
539    let mask: KernelSigset = guest.memory().read_value(mask)?;
540    Ok(mask & kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGALRM) != 0)
541}
542
543/// Validate the entire action through the kernel before copying it privately.
544/// A partial `read_value` can fall back to ptrace for its final eight bytes,
545/// which would read through a protected page. SIGKILL accepts no new action,
546/// but both Linux and KVM copy the complete input before rejecting it.
547async fn read_kernel_sigaction<G, T>(
548    guest: &mut G,
549    address: Addr<'_, libc::sigaction>,
550) -> Result<KernelSigaction, Error>
551where
552    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
553    T: RecordOrReplay,
554{
555    let validation = syscalls::RtSigaction::new()
556        .with_signum(libc::SIGKILL)
557        .with_action(Some(address))
558        .with_old_action(None)
559        .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE);
560    match guest.inject(validation).await {
561        Err(Errno::EINVAL) => {}
562        Err(errno) => return Err(errno.into()),
563        Ok(_) => return Err(Errno::EIO.into()),
564    }
565    Ok(guest.memory().read_value(address.cast())?)
566}
567
568// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
569// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
570fn timeval_to_logical_time(value: libc::timeval) -> Result<LogicalTime, Errno> {
571    let seconds = u64::try_from(value.tv_sec).map_err(|_| Errno::EINVAL)?;
572    let micros = u64::try_from(value.tv_usec).map_err(|_| Errno::EINVAL)?;
573    if micros >= 1_000_000 {
574        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
575    }
576    let nanos = seconds
577        .checked_mul(1_000_000_000)
578        .and_then(|nanos| nanos.checked_add(micros * 1_000))
579        .ok_or(Errno::EINVAL)?;
580    Ok(LogicalTime::from_nanos(nanos))
581}
582
583// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
584// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
585fn logical_time_to_timeval(value: LogicalTime) -> libc::timeval {
586    libc::timeval {
587        tv_sec: value.as_secs() as libc::time_t,
588        tv_usec: value.subsec_micros() as libc::suseconds_t,
589    }
590}
591
592// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
593// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
594#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
595fn logical_time_to_alarm_seconds(value: LogicalTime) -> i64 {
596    value.as_nanos().div_ceil(1_000_000_000) as i64
597}
598
599/// `alarm()`'s return as Linux computes it (`alarm_setitimer`,
600/// `kernel/time/itimer.c`): the whole seconds left, plus one when the
601/// remainder is at least half a second, or when no whole second is left but
602/// the timer is still active. So an active timer, even one with less than a
603/// microsecond left, returns at least 1
604/// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4139).
605#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
606fn linux_alarm_seconds(value: LogicalTime) -> i64 {
607    let ns = value.as_nanos();
608    let (secs, rem) = (ns / 1_000_000_000, ns % 1_000_000_000);
609    let round_up = (secs == 0 && rem != 0) || rem >= 500_000_000;
610    (secs + u64::from(round_up)) as i64
611}
612
613// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
614// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
615/// A pid no task can have: above the largest `pid_max` Linux allows (2^22).
616/// `rt_sigqueueinfo` to it makes the kernel check the siginfo exactly as for
617/// any pid and then answer ESRCH, which is Linux's answer for a gone process
618/// (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4124>).
619pub(crate) const NO_SUCH_PID: libc::pid_t = libc::pid_t::MAX;
620
621/// The 128 siginfo bytes at `info`. A page the kernel may write a siginfo to
622/// can lack read permission (`PROT_WRITE` alone), which `process_vm_readv`
623/// refuses; the tracer then reads it through `/proc/<tid>/mem`, as ptrace
624/// access does, which needs no read permission on the mapping.
625/// The siginfo a guest's send `call` of `signal` queues, as the receiver
626/// sees it (4262 design N8-5): the first 48 bytes of the caller's
627/// `siginfo_t` for `rt_sigqueueinfo` and `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, or Linux's
628/// `SI_USER` (`kill`) or `SI_TKILL` (`tgkill`, `tkill`) record with the
629/// caller's process id and real uid; for `pidfd_send_signal`, the caller's
630/// siginfo or `SI_USER`. `None` when it cannot be read, or for a call
631/// Detcore does not decode, which the scheduler records as `SI_USER`.
632/// The process id is the container's, which is the receiver's unless either
633/// is in a nested PID namespace.
634fn sent_siginfo<T, G: Guest<Detcore<T>>>(
635    guest: &mut G,
636    call: &syscalls::Syscall,
637    signal: i32,
638) -> Option<KernelSiginfo>
639where
640    T: RecordOrReplay,
641{
642    if !crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config()) {
643        return None;
644    }
645    let code = match call {
646        syscalls::Syscall::RtSigqueueinfo(call) => {
647            return read_siginfo(guest, call.siginfo()?)
648                .map(|bytes| KernelSiginfo::from_user(&bytes));
649        }
650        syscalls::Syscall::RtTgsigqueueinfo(call) => {
651            return read_siginfo(guest, call.siginfo()?)
652                .map(|bytes| KernelSiginfo::from_user(&bytes));
653        }
654        // `pidfd_send_signal` queues the caller's siginfo when it passes one,
655        // and Linux's `SI_USER` record otherwise.
656        syscalls::Syscall::Other(sysno, args) if *sysno == Sysno::pidfd_send_signal => {
657            match AddrMut::<libc::siginfo_t>::from_raw(args.arg2) {
658                Some(info) => {
659                    return read_siginfo(guest, info).map(|bytes| KernelSiginfo::from_user(&bytes));
660                }
661                None => libc::SI_USER,
662            }
663        }
664        syscalls::Syscall::Kill(_) => libc::SI_USER,
665        syscalls::Syscall::Tgkill(_) | syscalls::Syscall::Tkill(_) => libc::SI_TKILL,
666        _ => return None,
667    };
668    let pid = guest.thread_state().detpid?.as_raw();
669    let uid = crate::syscalls::real_uid(guest.tid().as_raw())?;
670    Some(crate::scheduler::signal_lists::ordinary_siginfo(
671        signal, code, pid, uid,
672    ))
673}
674
675/// The 128 bytes of the guest's `siginfo_t` at `info`, `None` when they
676/// cannot be read. A range that would pass the end of the address space is
677/// unreadable, as it is to Linux's copy (EFAULT), and is never handed to the
678/// guest-memory reader, whose page walk would overflow on it.
679fn read_siginfo<T, G: Guest<Detcore<T>>>(
680    guest: &mut G,
681    info: AddrMut<libc::siginfo_t>,
682) -> Option<[u8; 128]>
683where
684    T: RecordOrReplay,
685{
686    info.as_raw().checked_add(128)?;
687    if let Ok(bytes) = guest.memory().read_value(info.cast::<[u8; 128]>()) {
688        return Some(bytes);
689    }
690    use std::os::unix::fs::FileExt;
691    let mem = std::fs::File::open(format!("/proc/{}/mem", guest.tid().as_raw())).ok()?;
692    let mut bytes = [0u8; 128];
693    mem.read_exact_at(&mut bytes, info.as_raw() as u64).ok()?;
694    Some(bytes)
695}
696
697/// The first bytes of the guest range at `address`, up to `len`, that are
698/// mapped: read a page at a time, through `/proc/<tid>/mem` where the page
699/// lacks read permission (`PROT_WRITE` alone, or `PROT_NONE`), and stopping at
700/// the first page that is not mapped. The kernel's copy to user mode stops at
701/// the first byte it cannot write, so every byte it can change in the range
702/// is in this prefix (the Codex re-check of
703/// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4351, P2-1).
704fn read_mapped_prefix<T, G: Guest<Detcore<T>>>(guest: &mut G, address: usize, len: usize) -> Vec<u8>
705where
706    T: RecordOrReplay,
707{
708    const PAGE: usize = 4096;
709    let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(len);
710    let mut mem = None;
711    while bytes.len() < len {
712        let at = address + bytes.len();
713        let take = (PAGE - at % PAGE).min(len - bytes.len());
714        let mut piece = vec![0u8; take];
715        let Some(remote) = AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw(at) else {
716            break;
717        };
718        if guest.memory().read_exact(remote, &mut piece).is_err() {
719            use std::os::unix::fs::FileExt;
720            if mem.is_none() {
721                mem = std::fs::File::open(format!("/proc/{}/mem", guest.tid().as_raw())).ok();
722            }
723            let Some(file) = mem.as_ref() else {
724                break;
725            };
726            if file.read_exact_at(&mut piece, at as u64).is_err() {
727                break;
728            }
729        }
730        bytes.extend_from_slice(&piece);
731    }
732    bytes
733}
734
735/// The most a signalfd read's records can need restored: one 128-byte record
736/// for each standard signal in each of the two queues it reads (the thread's
737/// and the process's). Real-time signals, which can queue more copies, are
738/// out of scope (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4319).
739const SIGNALFD_CAPTURED: usize = 2 * 64 * SIGNALFD_RECORD;
740
741/// What the guest's buffers `extents` (address, length) hold, in order, up to
742/// `limit` bytes and at most `SIGNALFD_CAPTURED`, read before a signalfd read
743/// writes them, so a record that read drops can leave them as they were
744/// (`apply_signalfd_answers`). The capture stops at the first byte that is not
745/// mapped, which the read cannot write either.
746pub(crate) fn capture_signalfd_buffer<T, G: Guest<Detcore<T>>>(
747    guest: &mut G,
748    extents: &[(usize, usize)],
749    limit: usize,
750) -> Vec<u8>
751where
752    T: RecordOrReplay,
753{
754    let total: usize = extents.iter().map(|(_, len)| *len).sum();
755    let len = total.min(limit).min(SIGNALFD_CAPTURED);
756    let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(len);
757    for (address, length) in signalfd_extent_pieces(extents, len) {
758        let piece = read_mapped_prefix(guest, address, length);
759        let short = piece.len() < length;
760        bytes.extend_from_slice(&piece);
761        if short {
762            break;
763        }
764    }
765    bytes
766}
767
768/// Put back, in the guest's siginfo buffer at `info`, the bytes that differ
769/// from `before`, the mapped prefix of its 128 bytes before a take whose copy
770/// is dropped (`read_mapped_prefix`). Only the bytes the kernel wrote differ,
771/// and it wrote only what user mode could, so no write reaches a page user
772/// mode could not write; a buffer that ends at an unmapped page keeps its
773/// prefix as it was (the Codex re-check of
774/// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4351, P2-1).
775fn restore_changed_bytes<T, G: Guest<Detcore<T>>>(
776    guest: &mut G,
777    info: AddrMut<libc::siginfo_t>,
778    before: &[u8],
779) where
780    T: RecordOrReplay,
781{
782    let now = read_mapped_prefix(guest, info.as_raw(), before.len());
783    let before = &before[..now.len()];
784    let mut at = 0;
785    while at < before.len() {
786        if now[at] == before[at] {
787            at += 1;
788            continue;
789        }
790        let start = at;
791        while at < before.len() && now[at] != before[at] {
792            at += 1;
793        }
794        if let Some(address) = AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw(info.as_raw() + start) {
795            let _ = guest.memory().write_exact(address, &before[start..at]);
796        }
797    }
798}
799
800/// The thread a process-directed signal goes to. `zombie` is the process's
801/// exit state (`Scheduler::exited_process_is_zombie`). `Ok(None)` is a zombie:
802/// no live threads, but its parent can still wait for it, so Linux still finds
803/// the process and the send succeeds (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4091).
804fn deterministic_kill_target(
805    targets: &[DetTid],
806    zombie: Option<bool>,
807    sig: libc::c_int,
808) -> Result<Option<DetTid>, Errno> {
809    match targets {
810        [] if zombie == Some(true) => Ok(None),
811        [] => Err(Errno::ESRCH),
812        [target] => Ok(Some(*target)),
813        [target, ..] if sig == 0 => Ok(Some(*target)),
814        _ => Err(Errno::ENOSYS),
815    }
816}
817
818// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
819// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1119): Review unmaskable process-group SIGKILL forwarding.
820fn can_forward_process_group_signal(
821    pid: libc::pid_t,
822    sig: libc::c_int,
823    backend_requires_pid_translation: bool,
824) -> bool {
825    pid < -1 && sig == libc::SIGKILL && !backend_requires_pid_translation
826}
827
828/// Whether one of Linux's three ordinary signal syscalls names the calling
829/// task exactly and asks for the one signal that cannot return successfully.
830///
831/// Keep process-group and broadcast spellings out of this predicate. Even when
832/// the caller belongs to the named group, KVM can refuse a group containing a
833/// second process; reserving an exit before that refusal would strand the
834/// scheduler's terminal barrier.
835fn self_sigkill_targets_current_task(
836    signal: libc::c_int,
837    target_process: Option<DetPid>,
838    target_thread: Option<DetTid>,
839    current_process: DetPid,
840    current_thread: DetTid,
841) -> bool {
842    signal == libc::SIGKILL
843        && (target_process.is_some() || target_thread.is_some())
844        && target_process.is_none_or(|target| target == current_process)
845        && target_thread.is_none_or(|target| target == current_thread)
846}
847
848impl<T: RecordOrReplay> Detcore<T> {
849    /// Bind a supported in-guest emulated sleep to its guest disposition/mask.
850    /// Ordinary admitted creation does not share signal dispositions between
851    /// processes, and each supported in-guest process has one guest thread.
852    /// The snapshot therefore stays current until this request resumes.
853    pub(crate) async fn in_guest_sleep_sigalrm_snapshot<G: Guest<Self>>(
854        &self,
855        guest: &mut G,
856    ) -> Option<InGuestSleepSigalrm> {
857        if !self.cfg.backend.virtualizes_guest_sigalrm {
858            return None;
859        }
860        match read_sigalrm_sleep_state(guest).await {
861            Ok(state) => Some(state),
862            Err(error) => {
863                self.stop_after_in_guest_sleep_signal_loss(
864                    guest,
865                    format!("could not query its SIGALRM action/mask before sleeping: {error}"),
866                )
867                .await
868            }
869        }
870    }
871
872    /// A typed wake means the scheduler generated a one-shot SIGALRM for this
873    /// exact sleep without sending it physically. Stage it through the frozen
874    /// runtime self-signal path, which runs signal and exit callbacks before
875    /// Linux kills the process. The caller must immediately return its handler:
876    /// it must neither resume guest code nor copy nanosleep's remainder out.
877    pub(crate) async fn stage_in_guest_fatal_alarm<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G) {
878        if !self.cfg.backend.virtualizes_guest_sigalrm {
879            self.stop_after_in_guest_sleep_signal_loss(
880                guest,
881                "received an in-guest fatal alarm on another backend".into(),
882            )
883            .await;
884        }
885        match read_sigalrm_sleep_state(guest).await {
886            Ok(InGuestSleepSigalrm {
887                disposition: SigalrmSleepDisposition::Default,
888                blocked: false,
889            }) => {}
890            other => {
891                self.stop_after_in_guest_sleep_signal_loss(
892                    guest,
893                    format!("its default/unblocked SIGALRM sleep snapshot changed: {other:?}"),
894                )
895                .await;
896            }
897        }
898        let call = syscalls::Tgkill::new()
899            .with_tgid(guest.pid().as_raw())
900            .with_tid(guest.tid().as_raw())
901            .with_sig(libc::SIGALRM);
902        if guest.inject(call).await != Ok(0) {
903            self.stop_after_in_guest_sleep_signal_loss(
904                guest,
905                "could not stage its default-fatal SIGALRM after a sleep wake".into(),
906            )
907            .await;
908        }
909    }
910
911    async fn stop_after_in_guest_sleep_signal_loss<G: Guest<Self>>(
912        &self,
913        guest: &mut G,
914        reason: String,
915    ) -> ! {
916        let reason = format!(
917            "thread {} cannot complete its in-guest SIGALRM sleep delivery: {reason}",
918            guest.thread_state().dettid,
919        );
920        crate::tool_global::report_determinism_loss(guest, reason).await;
921        crate::guest_log::flush_unsent();
922        match guest
923            .tail_inject(
924                syscalls::ExitGroup::new().with_status(detcore_model::HERMIT_POLICY_REFUSAL_EXIT),
925            )
926            .await {}
927    }
928
929    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
930    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
931    /// We send the alarms to the global scheduler to handle.
932    #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
933    pub async fn handle_alarm<G: Guest<Self>>(
934        &self,
935        guest: &mut G,
936        call: syscalls::Alarm,
937    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
938        if guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
939            let remaining = register_alarm(
940                guest,
941                LogicalTime::from_secs(call.seconds() as u64),
942                LogicalTime::ZERO,
943                Signal::SIGALRM,
944            )
945            .await;
946            // Record and replay keep the rounding they recorded with.
947            Ok(if self.cfg.recordreplay_modes {
948                logical_time_to_alarm_seconds(remaining.0)
949            } else {
950                linux_alarm_seconds(remaining.0)
951            })
952        } else {
953            info!(
954                "[dtid {}] Running without scheduler, so letting alarm call through...",
955                guest.thread_state().dettid
956            );
957            Ok(guest.inject(call).await?)
958        }
959    }
960
961    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
962    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
963    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#869)
964    /// Schedule a one-shot or periodic real-time interval timer on Detcore logical time.
965    pub async fn handle_setitimer<G: Guest<Self>>(
966        &self,
967        guest: &mut G,
968        call: syscalls::Setitimer,
969    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
970        if !guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
971            info!(
972                "[dtid {}] Running without scheduler, so letting setitimer call through...",
973                guest.thread_state().dettid
974            );
975            return Ok(guest.inject(call).await?);
976        }
977        if call.which() != libc::ITIMER_REAL {
978            return Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ENOSYS));
979        }
980
981        let value = call.value().ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
982        let timer: libc::itimerval = guest.memory().read_value(value)?;
983        let interval = timeval_to_logical_time(timer.it_interval)?;
984        let duration = timeval_to_logical_time(timer.it_value)?;
985        // A zero value disarms the timer, whatever its interval.
986        if duration.as_nanos() != 0 && interval.as_nanos() != 0 {
987            refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::ArmRecurringTimer).await?;
988        }
989        let (remaining, old_interval) =
990            register_alarm(guest, duration, interval, Signal::SIGALRM).await;
991        if let Some(old_value) = call.ovalue() {
992            let old_timer = libc::itimerval {
993                it_interval: logical_time_to_timeval(old_interval),
994                it_value: logical_time_to_timeval(remaining),
995            };
996            guest.memory().write_value(old_value, &old_timer)?;
997        }
998        Ok(0)
999    }
1000
1001    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1002    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-892)
1003    /// Return interval-timer state from Detcore's logical scheduler.
1004    pub async fn handle_getitimer<G: Guest<Self>>(
1005        &self,
1006        guest: &mut G,
1007        call: syscalls::Getitimer,
1008    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1009        if !guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
1010            info!(
1011                "[dtid {}] Running without scheduler, so letting getitimer call through...",
1012                guest.thread_state().dettid
1013            );
1014            return Ok(guest.inject(call).await?);
1015        }
1016
1017        let snapshot = match call.which() {
1018            libc::ITIMER_REAL => alarm_remaining(guest).await,
1019            libc::ITIMER_VIRTUAL | libc::ITIMER_PROF => {
1020                crate::scheduler::real_timer::ItimerSnapshot::default()
1021            }
1022            _ => return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into()),
1023        };
1024        let value = call.value().ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
1025        let timer = libc::itimerval {
1026            it_interval: logical_time_to_timeval(snapshot.interval),
1027            it_value: logical_time_to_timeval(snapshot.remaining),
1028        };
1029        guest.memory().write_value(value, &timer)?;
1030        Ok(0)
1031    }
1032
1033    /// A pause is really just an unbounded sleep.
1034    #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
1035    pub async fn handle_pause<G: Guest<Self>>(
1036        &self,
1037        guest: &mut G,
1038        call: syscalls::Pause,
1039    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1040        if guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
1041            // `pause` has no deadline: it returns only when a signal is delivered.
1042            // `LogicalTime::INDEFINITE` records that, and the scheduler refuses to
1043            // fast-forward virtual time onto it (see `step2d_handle_empty_queue`),
1044            // so the `Normal` arm below stays unreachable.
1045            loop {
1046                // A signal that already ended the call before it parked: one sent
1047                // while this thread was stopped short of filing the request, as
1048                // when its timeslice ended in the syscall's prehook.
1049                if sleep_signal(guest, SleepCheck::BeforePark, true).await? == SleepSignal::Pending
1050                {
1051                    return Err(Errno::ERESTARTNOHAND.into());
1052                }
1053                let mut req = Self::sleep_request_abs(guest, LogicalTime::INDEFINITE).await;
1054                req.fyi(crate::resources::PAUSE_FYI);
1055                req.in_guest_sleep_sigalrm = self.in_guest_sleep_sigalrm_snapshot(guest).await;
1056                match crate::tool_global::parked_wait_request(
1057                    guest,
1058                    req,
1059                    crate::scheduler::parked::ParkedWaitPolicy::PauseNoHandlerRestart,
1060                )
1061                .await
1062                {
1063                    ResumeStatus::InGuestFatalAlarm => {
1064                        self.stage_in_guest_fatal_alarm(guest).await;
1065                        return Err(Errno::EINTR.into());
1066                    }
1067                    // A pause never asks for `NothingElseCanRun`.
1068                    ResumeStatus::Normal | ResumeStatus::NothingElseCanRun => {
1069                        panic!(
1070                            "Internal violation: pause should never return from the scheduler except by interruption!"
1071                        )
1072                    }
1073                    ResumeStatus::Signaled(signals) => {
1074                        match sleep_signal(guest, SleepCheck::AfterWake(signals), true).await? {
1075                            SleepSignal::Pending | SleepSignal::Claimed => {
1076                                return Err(Errno::ERESTARTNOHAND.into());
1077                            }
1078                            SleepSignal::Held => return Err(Errno::EINTR.into()),
1079                            SleepSignal::None => {
1080                                info!(
1081                                    "[dtid {}] pause woken for a signal no longer pending for it; waiting again",
1082                                    guest.thread_state().dettid
1083                                );
1084                            }
1085                        }
1086                    }
1087                }
1088            }
1089        } else {
1090            info!(
1091                "[dtid {}] Running without scheduler, so letting pause call through...",
1092                guest.thread_state().dettid
1093            );
1094            Ok(guest.inject(call).await?)
1095        }
1096    }
1097
1098    /// Run rt_sigsuspend, without holding the deterministic scheduler turn where
1099    /// the call sleeps.
1100    ///
1101    /// The kernel must perform the temporary mask swap atomically and restore the
1102    /// original mask after signal delivery, so execute the real blocking syscall
1103    /// while marking this thread as blocked outside the runnable set.
1104    ///
1105    /// On a backend whose `rt_sigsuspend` never sleeps
1106    /// (`rt_sigsuspend_never_sleeps`: KVM), the call instead runs in the
1107    /// caller's own turn, and a refused wait yields the turn
1108    /// (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4360>).
1109    pub async fn handle_rt_sigsuspend<G: Guest<Self>>(
1110        &self,
1111        guest: &mut G,
1112        call: syscalls::RtSigsuspend,
1113    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1114        // D41 (A5 step 3c.1c): rt_sigsuspend is kernel-run on the in-guest
1115        // backends, so a default-fatal signal deliverable at its entry means
1116        // the call is not run, checked before anything else, the argument
1117        // checks and the real-time timer observation below included.
1118        if let Some(refused) = super::helpers::kernel_run_entry_refusal(guest)? {
1119            return Err(refused);
1120        }
1121        // Invalid arguments return immediately from the kernel and therefore are
1122        // not signal-only waits.
1123        validate_kernel_sigset_size(call.sigsetsize())?;
1124        let Some(mask_addr) = call.mask() else {
1125            return Err(Errno::EFAULT.into());
1126        };
1127
1128        let temporary_mask =
1129            restart_if_first_probe_not_run(read_kernel_sigset(guest, mask_addr).await)?;
1130        super::signal_shadow::note_wait_mask(guest, temporary_mask);
1131        // Under the in-guest delivery gate the call is parked in the
1132        // scheduler, never run by the kernel (A5 step 3c.1d, D39).
1133        if self.cfg.in_guest_signal_delivery {
1134            return Box::pin(self.parked_rt_sigsuspend(guest, temporary_mask)).await;
1135        }
1136        // The temporary mask lets the kernel take a pending real-time timer
1137        // expiration that every thread blocked when it expired
1138        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4089).
1139        observe_under_mask(guest, temporary_mask).await?;
1140        // The real call sleeps under a private copy of the mask read here, not
1141        // under the guest's buffer. The scheduler decides from this copy which
1142        // signals can end the call (`Resources::blocked_signal_mask`), and
1143        // another process sharing the buffer (MAP_SHARED) may rewrite it before
1144        // the call runs; Linux copies the mask when the call starts, and from
1145        // the guest's view the call has already started.
1146        //
1147        // The copy and the rt_sigpending result live in two cells just below
1148        // the stack's red zone, the scratch area an injected call may use, but
1149        // never on the caller's buffer: a guest may keep its mask there while
1150        // it blocks every signal, and a copy placed on that very buffer would
1151        // be the original again, while the pending cell would overwrite the
1152        // guest's mask. If the buffer overlaps the two cells, they move to just
1153        // below it. They are written directly rather than through a scratch
1154        // stack, whose commit writes one contiguous region down from the red
1155        // zone and so would cover a buffer inside it.
1156        let caller_mask = mask_addr.as_raw()..mask_addr.as_raw() + KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE;
1157        // A stack pointer, or a buffer, too close to address zero leaves no
1158        // room for the red zone and the cells below it. The call then fails
1159        // with EFAULT, as it does when a cell is not writable.
1160        let below_red_zone = (crate::arch::stack_pointer(&guest.regs().await) as usize)
1161            .checked_sub(STACK_RED_ZONE)
1162            .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
1163        let cells = below_red_zone
1164            .checked_sub(SCRATCH_CELLS_SIZE)
1165            .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?..below_red_zone;
1166        let cells_start = if cells.start < caller_mask.end && caller_mask.start < cells.end {
1167            // Checked for symmetry only: the mask was just read, and nothing
1168            // below address 16 is readable.
1169            caller_mask
1170                .start
1171                .checked_sub(SCRATCH_CELLS_SIZE)
1172                .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?
1173                & !(std::mem::align_of::<u64>() - 1)
1174        } else {
1175            cells.start
1176        };
1177        let pending_addr = Addr::<u64>::from_raw(cells_start).ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
1178        let mask_copy = AddrMut::<u64>::from_raw(cells_start + std::mem::size_of::<u64>())
1179            .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
1180        guest.memory().write_value(mask_copy, &temporary_mask)?;
1181        let pending_out = AddrMut::<libc::sigset_t>::from_raw(pending_addr.as_raw())
1182            .expect("stack address must be non-null");
1183        let pending_call = syscalls::RtSigpending::new()
1184            .with_set(Some(pending_out))
1185            .with_sigsetsize(KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE);
1186        guest.inject_with_retry(pending_call).await?;
1187        let pending: u64 = guest.memory().read_value(pending_addr)?;
1188        let call = call.with_mask(Some(Addr::from_raw(mask_copy.as_raw()).expect("non-null")));
1189
1190        // On a backend whose `rt_sigsuspend` never sleeps
1191        // (`rt_sigsuspend_never_sleeps`), the call returns at once, so it runs
1192        // in this thread's own turn, as any call that cannot block does.
1193        // Backgrounded as a sleeper, it left nothing holding the scheduler
1194        // between the background grant and its continuation, so the scheduler
1195        // reported a deadlock or skipped virtual time to a timed waiter, as
1196        // host timing decided (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4360).
1197        //
1198        // The backend refuses a wait for a signal not yet pending (ENOSYS), and
1199        // a guest retries it, so that refusal hands the turn on first with a
1200        // strong yield (`ResourceID::SchedYield`): the caller is not selected
1201        // again until another thread has had a turn, and under chaos not until
1202        // a thread that did not yield has (a yield epoch), so the threads it
1203        // waits on run before it retries, in the order the scheduler gives. In
1204        // its own turn alone, the caller spun and never let its child's exit
1205        // be granted; a weak yield, which `sched_yield` makes under chaos,
1206        // would let a caller in a higher band win every turn (the Codex
1207        // review).
1208        if rt_sigsuspend_never_sleeps(&self.cfg.backend) {
1209            let result = self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await;
1210            if result == Err(Errno::ENOSYS) && self.cfg.sequentialize_threads {
1211                if self.cfg.max_timeslice.is_some() {
1212                    guest
1213                        .thread_state_mut()
1214                        .reset_timeslice_for_explicit_yield();
1215                }
1216                let request = Self::sched_yield_request(guest);
1217                resource_request(guest, request).await;
1218            }
1219            return Ok(result?);
1220        }
1221        // The scheduler records the mask the call sleeps under, read here in
1222        // this thread's turn, to choose a SIGCHLD target while the thread is
1223        // outside the runnable set (`Resources::blocked_signal_mask`).
1224        let installed_mask = kernel_installed_signal_mask(temporary_mask);
1225        if pending & !temporary_mask != 0 {
1226            // The kernel will consume an already-pending signal as soon as it
1227            // atomically installs the temporary mask. Keep this immediate case
1228            // out of the terminal-wait classification; the real syscall still
1229            // performs delivery and restores the old mask.
1230            self.record_or_replay_blocking_with_mask(guest, call.into(), Some(installed_mask))
1231                .await
1232        } else {
1233            self.record_or_replay_rt_sigsuspend(guest, call, installed_mask)
1234                .await
1235        }
1236    }
1237
1238    /// A5 step 3c.1d, design D39: `rt_sigsuspend` under the in-guest delivery
1239    /// gate, parked in the scheduler as a `pause` is, under its temporary mask
1240    /// (`temporary_mask`, read at entry in Linux's order, `sigsuspend`,
1241    /// kernel/signal.c:4697-4728 at v6.12), which the kernel never installs:
1242    /// the in-guest runtime's mask holds every signal sent while the thread
1243    /// waits, and Detcore decides.
1244    ///
1245    /// - The default-fatal check under the guest's own mask at entry (D41)
1246    ///   has already run, as the handler's first statement; signal phase 1's
1247    ///   refusal of temporary-mask calls in a process that handles SIGALRM
1248    ///   runs before any handler (`sigalrm_phase1`, F8).
1249    /// - Each check, before the park and after each wake
1250    ///   (`helpers::temporary_mask_check`), ends the wait for a default-fatal
1251    ///   signal the temporary mask lets in: `-ERESTARTNOHAND`, as `sigsuspend`
1252    ///   returns, with the D40 flag, which the restart checkpoint turns into
1253    ///   `-EINTR` for the death path of step 3c.1e. Otherwise it removes the
1254    ///   ignored signals Linux would not hold (D37 rule (ii)): those pending at
1255    ///   entry, queued under the own mask, unless both masks block them (the
1256    ///   transparent restart discards them: `signal_pending` counts them,
1257    ///   :157-166, and `get_signal` drops them, :2815, :2830), and those sent
1258    ///   since, unless the temporary mask blocks them (discarded at send).
1259    ///   The first check runs in this turn, before any request, so everything
1260    ///   pending then was pending at entry.
1261    /// - The temporary mask is the one Linux installs, without SIGKILL and
1262    ///   SIGSTOP (`set_current_blocked`, :3089-3092).
1263    /// - The park is a `SleepUntil(INDEFINITE)` request tagged
1264    ///   `RT_SIGSUSPEND_FYI` that publishes the temporary mask
1265    ///   (`Resources::blocked_signal_mask`, so `publish_request_guest_mask`
1266    ///   publishes it): the scheduler wakes it, as a `pause`, at a send's
1267    ///   commit for a signal deliverable under that mask
1268    ///   (`Scheduler::sleep_interrupting_signals`). It files no in-guest
1269    ///   SIGALRM sleep snapshot, so a one-shot alarm reaches it through the
1270    ///   ordinary send and the same wake, never the phase-1 fatal-alarm
1271    ///   divert, whose check reads the guest's own mask.
1272    /// - A pending real-time timer expiration is observed under the temporary
1273    ///   mask only after the first check found the call waiting, so a D41-like
1274    ///   end never drops one first (IR-22); under the gate the scheduler never
1275    ///   keeps one pending in any case (`real_time_expiration_fate` keeps one
1276    ///   only where signal targets are modelled, which the gate excludes).
1277    async fn parked_rt_sigsuspend<G: Guest<Self>>(
1278        &self,
1279        guest: &mut G,
1280        temporary_mask: KernelSigset,
1281    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1282        use crate::syscalls::helpers::TemporaryMaskCheck;
1283        // The mask Linux installs (`set_current_blocked`, kernel/signal.c:
1284        // 3089-3092 at v6.12, from `sigsuspend`, :4700): never SIGKILL or
1285        // SIGSTOP. It is what the wait decides by and what it publishes, so a
1286        // `/proc` status shows the `SigBlk` Linux shows.
1287        let temporary_mask = kernel_installed_signal_mask(temporary_mask);
1288        let mut entry = Some(!0);
1289        loop {
1290            let first = entry.is_some();
1291            match crate::syscalls::helpers::temporary_mask_check(
1292                guest,
1293                temporary_mask,
1294                entry.take(),
1295                false,
1296            )
1297            .await?
1298            {
1299                TemporaryMaskCheck::Waits => {}
1300                TemporaryMaskCheck::Fatal => return Err(Errno::ERESTARTNOHAND.into()),
1301                TemporaryMaskCheck::Refused | TemporaryMaskCheck::EpollInterrupted => {
1302                    return Err(Errno::EINTR.into());
1303                }
1304            }
1305            if first {
1306                observe_under_mask(guest, temporary_mask).await?;
1307            }
1308            let mut request = Self::sleep_request_abs(guest, LogicalTime::INDEFINITE).await;
1309            request.fyi(crate::resources::RT_SIGSUSPEND_FYI);
1310            request.blocked_signal_mask = Some(temporary_mask);
1311            match resource_request(guest, request).await {
1312                ResumeStatus::Signaled(_) => {}
1313                status @ (ResumeStatus::Normal
1314                | ResumeStatus::NothingElseCanRun
1315                | ResumeStatus::InGuestFatalAlarm) => panic!(
1316                    "Internal violation: a parked rt_sigsuspend returned from the scheduler \
1317                     with {status:?}, not by interruption"
1318                ),
1319            }
1320        }
1321    }
1322
1323    /// rt_sigaction
1324    pub async fn handle_rt_sigaction<G: Guest<Self>>(
1325        &self,
1326        guest: &mut G,
1327        call: syscalls::RtSigaction,
1328    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1329        // Linux rejects an invalid size before inspecting either user pointer.
1330        // Preserve that EINVAL-before-EFAULT ordering even for the signal that
1331        // Hermit reserves for deterministic preemption.
1332        validate_kernel_sigset_size(call.sigsetsize())?;
1333
1334        // Copy the complete kernel object before inspecting the signal number or
1335        // writing old_action. This preserves Linux's action-before-old_action
1336        // pointer ordering, including when both arguments alias.
1337        let kernel_action = match call.action() {
1338            Some(action) => Some(restart_if_first_probe_not_run(
1339                read_kernel_sigaction(guest, action).await,
1340            )?),
1341            None => None,
1342        };
1343
1344        // SIGTRAP is reported here, at the one point where the guest states its
1345        // expectation: it falls through to the ordinary path below (its handler
1346        // really is installed; ptrace just eats the signal).
1347        // SIGSTKFLT's disposition is not installed at all; the branch below
1348        // announces that once per run instead.
1349        if let Some(action) = kernel_action
1350            && call.signum() != reverie::PERF_EVENT_SIGNAL as i32
1351        {
1352            warn_appropriated_signal(call.signum(), action.handler);
1353        }
1354
1355        // PERF_EVENT_SIGNAL is reserved.
1356        if call.signum() == reverie::PERF_EVENT_SIGNAL as i32 {
1357            // A kept fake success, announced: once per run, the coordinator
1358            // names the signal and says the disposition was not installed.
1359            if kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| action.handler != libc::SIG_DFL as u64) {
1360                crate::tool_global::announce_signal_handler_not_installed(guest, call.signum())
1361                    .await;
1362            }
1363            // The go runtime attempts to register this (unused) signal handler.  We will never
1364            // deliver signals of this kind to the guest, so we just turn this action into a noop
1365            // rather than returning `Err(Errno::EINVAL.into())`. Preserve that established
1366            // policy while still honoring the raw syscall's pointer accesses: the virtual old
1367            // disposition is the default action, never Reverie's private preemption handler.
1368            if call.old_action().is_some() {
1369                // SIGKILL's disposition cannot be changed by userspace, so its
1370                // query copies the same four zero words as our virtual default.
1371                // Let the kernel copy them: a short write_value can finish with
1372                // PTRACE_POKEDATA and overwrite a protected final word. This also
1373                // preserves the kernel's partial-copy effects on EFAULT without
1374                // exposing Reverie's private preemption action.
1375                return Ok(guest
1376                    .inject(call.with_signum(libc::SIGKILL).with_action(None))
1377                    .await?);
1378            }
1379            return Ok(0);
1380        }
1381        // Signal phase 1 (step I3): on a backend that keeps a handled SIGALRM
1382        // virtual, the scheduler's ledger must agree with the runtime.
1383        let phase1 = self.cfg.backend.virtualizes_guest_sigalrm
1384            && call.signum() == libc::SIGALRM
1385            && kernel_action.is_some();
1386        let was_handled = guest.thread_state().sigalrm_handled;
1387        let to_handler = kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| {
1388            action.handler != libc::SIG_DFL as u64 && action.handler != libc::SIG_IGN as u64
1389        });
1390        if phase1 {
1391            if to_handler {
1392                self.refuse_sigalrm_handler(guest).await?;
1393            } else if was_handled
1394                && kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| action.handler == libc::SIG_DFL as u64)
1395            {
1396                refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::HandlerToDefault).await?;
1397            }
1398        }
1399        // Whether this call moves the signal from `SIG_IGN` to an action that
1400        // does not ignore it, read before the change: Linux then re-queues a
1401        // periodic POSIX timer's ignored expiry (`posixtimer_sig_unignore`,
1402        // <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293>).
1403        let unignores = crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config())
1404            && (1..32).contains(&call.signum())
1405            && kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| {
1406                action.handler != libc::SIG_IGN as u64
1407                    && !(action.handler == libc::SIG_DFL as u64
1408                        && [libc::SIGCONT, libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SIGWINCH, libc::SIGURG]
1409                            .contains(&call.signum()))
1410            })
1411            && match crate::syscalls::signal_shadow::copied_dispositions_for(guest, call.signum()) {
1412                Some(copied) => copied.ignored & kernel_sigset_bit(call.signum()) != 0,
1413                None => {
1414                    crate::syscalls::threads::read_kernel_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())
1415                        .is_ok_and(|state| state.ignored & kernel_sigset_bit(call.signum()) != 0)
1416                }
1417            };
1418        // D37 rule (iii) under the in-guest delivery gate (A5 step 3c.1d): a
1419        // call that moves the signal away from ignored first removes the
1420        // ignored signals Linux would not hold, before the kernel installs the
1421        // new action, which would otherwise make one deliverable.
1422        if self.cfg.in_guest_signal_delivery
1423            && let Some(action) = kernel_action
1424        {
1425            Box::pin(remove_ignored_before_unignore(
1426                guest,
1427                call.signum(),
1428                action.handler,
1429            ))
1430            .await?;
1431        }
1432        let result = if let Some(kernel_action) = kernel_action {
1433            // The kernel treats `action` as input. Sanitize a private copy instead
1434            // of writing through the guest pointer: the input may be read-only,
1435            // and changing it would make the syscall wrapper observable.
1436            let mut kernel_action = kernel_action;
1437            kernel_action.mask = without_perf_event_signal(kernel_action.mask);
1438            let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
1439            let sanitized_action = stack.push(kernel_action);
1440            let _stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
1441            guest
1442                .inject(call.with_action(Some(sanitized_action.cast())))
1443                .await
1444        } else {
1445            guest.inject(call).await
1446        };
1447        super::signal_shadow::record_rt_sigaction(
1448            guest,
1449            call.signum(),
1450            kernel_action.map(|action| KernelSigaction {
1451                mask: without_perf_event_signal(action.mask),
1452                ..action
1453            }),
1454            call.old_action(),
1455            &result,
1456        );
1457        // Publish the state the runtime now holds whenever the disposition may
1458        // have changed: from a handler, or to one the runtime did not refuse.
1459        // Any result counts, since an EFAULT copying the old action out comes
1460        // after the change. A default or ignored action set while none is
1461        // handled changes nothing the scheduler records, so it costs nothing.
1462        if phase1 && (was_handled || (to_handler && result != Err(Errno::EPERM))) {
1463            self.publish_virtual_sigalrm(guest).await?;
1464        }
1465        // Linux reaps a process's children automatically at exit when its
1466        // SIGCHLD action is SIG_IGN or carries SA_NOCLDWAIT, and a signal to
1467        // such a child then finds no zombie. Mirror the new action for the
1468        // scheduler's exit model, with the same success rule as above
1469        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4091).
1470        if call.signum() == libc::SIGCHLD
1471            && guest.config().sequentialize_threads
1472            && let Some(action) = kernel_action
1473            && matches!(result, Ok(_) | Err(Errno::EFAULT))
1474        {
1475            let dettid = guest.thread_state().dettid;
1476            if let Some(process) = registered_process_of(guest, dettid).await {
1477                set_sigchld_disposition(
1478                    guest,
1479                    process,
1480                    action.handler == libc::SIG_IGN as u64,
1481                    action.flags & libc::SA_NOCLDWAIT as u64 != 0,
1482                )
1483                .await;
1484            }
1485        }
1486        // Linux flushes a pending SIGCHLD when its action becomes SIG_IGN or
1487        // SIG_DFL, which ignores it (`do_sigaction`, `sig_handler_ignored`): the
1488        // child-exit ledger's send window goes with it, in this committed turn
1489        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4126).
1490        if call.signum() == libc::SIGCHLD
1491            && guest.config().sequentialize_threads
1492            && matches!(result, Ok(_) | Err(Errno::EFAULT))
1493            && kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| {
1494                action.handler == libc::SIG_IGN as u64 || action.handler == libc::SIG_DFL as u64
1495            })
1496        {
1497            crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
1498                guest,
1499                crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl::Flushed,
1500            )
1501            .await;
1502        }
1503        // Linux flushes the pending instances of a signal whose disposition
1504        // becomes SIG_IGN (`do_sigaction`), among them a pending real-time timer
1505        // expiration (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4089).
1506        if result.is_ok()
1507            && kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| action.handler == libc::SIG_IGN as u64)
1508            && (1..=64).contains(&call.signum())
1509        {
1510            let bit = kernel_sigset_bit(call.signum());
1511            let pending = pending_real_time_among(guest, bit).await;
1512            act_on_pending_real_time(guest, pending, 0, bit).await;
1513        }
1514        // The same flush takes a `SIGEV_THREAD_ID` timer's pending copy of a
1515        // standard signal, for SIG_IGN or for SIG_DFL on a signal whose
1516        // default is to ignore it (`sig_handler_ignored`), so it no longer
1517        // counts as the timer's own (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4164).
1518        let ignored = |handler: u64| {
1519            handler == libc::SIG_IGN as u64
1520                || (handler == libc::SIG_DFL as u64
1521                    && [libc::SIGCONT, libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SIGWINCH, libc::SIGURG]
1522                        .contains(&call.signum()))
1523        };
1524        // The kernel reads the new action from Detcore's sanitized copy, so an
1525        // EFAULT can only come from writing `old_action`, after the new action
1526        // is installed.
1527        if (result.is_ok() || matches!(result, Err(Errno::EFAULT)))
1528            && kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| ignored(action.handler))
1529            && (1..32).contains(&call.signum())
1530            && crate::scheduler::timer_copies_recorded()
1531        {
1532            crate::tool_global::forget_thread_timer_copies(guest, call.signum()).await;
1533        }
1534        // A handler's mask decides where a POSIX timer's next entry goes when a
1535        // delivery promotes it (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4262>).
1536        if crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config())
1537            && (result.is_ok() || matches!(result, Err(Errno::EFAULT)))
1538            && (1..32).contains(&call.signum())
1539            && let Some(action) = kernel_action
1540        {
1541            crate::tool_global::discarded_signal(
1542                guest,
1543                DiscardedSignalControl::Handler {
1544                    signal: call.signum(),
1545                    caught: action.handler != libc::SIG_IGN as u64
1546                        && action.handler != libc::SIG_DFL as u64,
1547                    mask: action.mask,
1548                    nodefer: action.flags & libc::SA_NODEFER as u64 != 0,
1549                },
1550            )
1551            .await;
1552        }
1553        // The flush takes every pending copy of the signal from every queue of
1554        // the process, so none of them is one Linux discarded any more; and an
1555        // unignore makes a still-current periodic timer expiry a kept one
1556        // (`scheduler::discarded_at_send`).
1557        if crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config())
1558            && (result.is_ok() || matches!(result, Err(Errno::EFAULT)))
1559            && (1..32).contains(&call.signum())
1560        {
1561            let signal = call.signum();
1562            if kernel_action.is_some_and(|action| ignored(action.handler)) {
1563                crate::tool_global::discarded_signal(
1564                    guest,
1565                    DiscardedSignalControl::Flushed { signal },
1566                )
1567                .await;
1568            } else if unignores {
1569                crate::tool_global::discarded_signal(
1570                    guest,
1571                    DiscardedSignalControl::Unignored { signal },
1572                )
1573                .await;
1574            }
1575        }
1576        Ok(result?)
1577    }
1578
1579    /// Signal phase 1's Detcore-side refusals of a new guest SIGALRM handler,
1580    /// each EPERM before anything changes (design section 6 and closure 5):
1581    /// threads not sequentialized (`alarm` would arm a kernel timer; named
1582    /// with a WARN, `unsequentialized_handler_refused_warning`), a signalfd in
1583    /// the process's descriptor table, and a kernel producer of SIGALRM armed
1584    /// anywhere in the run.
1585    async fn refuse_sigalrm_handler<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G) -> Result<(), Error> {
1586        // Without sequentialization a run has no premise P (design 16.2: one
1587        // guest thread runs guest code at a time), so it may not catch a
1588        // signal: the first caught-handler install is refused, by name, with
1589        // EPERM and a WARN (coordinator decision D32). A run that catches no
1590        // signal keeps running, a labelled relaxation as on ptrace. Today this
1591        // phase 1 SIGALRM handler is the only handler the in-guest runtime
1592        // lets through to Detcore (it refuses every other one itself, and
1593        // resets any handler installed before it to SIG_DFL). A5 step 3c.2's
1594        // handler admission must carry the same rule for every handler
1595        // install under --no-sequentialize-threads.
1596        if !guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
1597            tracing::warn!(
1598                "{}",
1599                crate::refused_signal_controls::unsequentialized_handler_refused_warning(
1600                    libc::SIGALRM,
1601                    guest.thread_state().dettid.as_raw(),
1602                )
1603            );
1604            return Err(Errno::EPERM.into());
1605        }
1606        // The model's own signalfds, and any the kernel's table holds that
1607        // the model labels otherwise (an inherited descriptor).
1608        let holds_signalfd = guest
1609            .thread_state()
1610            .file_metadata
1611            .lock()
1612            .expect("file metadata mutex poisoned")
1613            .file_handles
1614            .values()
1615            .any(|fd| fd.ty() == FdType::Signalfd)
1616            || crate::sigalrm_phase1::process_holds_signalfd(guest.pid().as_raw());
1617        if holds_signalfd {
1618            return Err(Errno::EPERM.into());
1619        }
1620        Ok(refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::InstallHandler).await?)
1621    }
1622
1623    /// Read the guest's SIGALRM disposition and blocked bit back from the
1624    /// runtime (which answers these queries from its virtual state), then
1625    /// publish them to the scheduler and record the disposition in the thread
1626    /// state, inside this call's turn.
1627    async fn publish_virtual_sigalrm<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G) -> Result<(), Error> {
1628        let (handled, blocked) = virtual_sigalrm_state(guest).await?;
1629        sigalrm_refuses(guest, SigalrmControl::Publish { handled, blocked }).await;
1630        guest.thread_state_mut().sigalrm_handled = handled;
1631        guest.thread_state_mut().sigalrm_blocked_published = blocked;
1632        Ok(())
1633    }
1634
1635    /// After a guest call that may have changed its virtual mask in a process
1636    /// that handles SIGALRM, publish its SIGALRM blocked bit.
1637    async fn publish_virtual_sigalrm_blocked<G: Guest<Self>>(
1638        &self,
1639        guest: &mut G,
1640    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
1641        let blocked = virtual_sigalrm_blocked(guest).await?;
1642        sigalrm_refuses(guest, SigalrmControl::PublishBlocked(blocked)).await;
1643        guest.thread_state_mut().sigalrm_blocked_published = blocked;
1644        Ok(())
1645    }
1646
1647    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1648    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#1046): Review retrying interrupted signal-mask injections.
1649    /// rt_sigprocmask
1650    pub async fn handle_rt_sigprocmask<G: Guest<Self>>(
1651        &self,
1652        guest: &mut G,
1653        call: syscalls::RtSigprocmask,
1654    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1655        // The kernel checks sigsetsize before copying from either user pointer.
1656        validate_kernel_sigset_size(call.sigsetsize())?;
1657
1658        let (result, validated_set) = self.rt_sigprocmask_inner(guest, call).await;
1659        let set_not_read = matches!(validated_set, ValidatedSet::NotRun);
1660        if guest.thread_state().signal_shadow.is_some() {
1661            use super::signal_shadow::SetInput;
1662            let errno_result = match &result {
1663                Ok(value) => Some(Ok(*value)),
1664                Err(Error::Errno(errno)) => Some(Err(*errno)),
1665                Err(_) => {
1666                    super::signal_shadow::give_up(
1667                        guest,
1668                        "rt_sigprocmask failed without an errno, so its effect is unknown",
1669                    );
1670                    None
1671                }
1672            };
1673            let set = match (call.set(), validated_set) {
1674                (None, _) => SetInput::None,
1675                (Some(_), ValidatedSet::Validated(set)) => {
1676                    SetInput::Validated(without_perf_event_signal(set))
1677                }
1678                (Some(_), ValidatedSet::NotRun) => SetInput::NotRun,
1679                // Read after the call, from memory the kernel itself just
1680                // read, and only when it succeeded; an old mask written over
1681                // any of the set's bytes leaves it unknown.
1682                (Some(set), ValidatedSet::NotValidated) => SetInput::Unvalidated(
1683                    (result.is_ok()
1684                        && !call.oldset().is_some_and(|old| {
1685                            old.as_raw().abs_diff(set.as_raw()) < KERNEL_SIGSET_SIZE
1686                        }))
1687                    .then(|| {
1688                        guest
1689                            .memory()
1690                            .read_value::<_, KernelSigset>(set.cast::<KernelSigset>())
1691                            .ok()
1692                    })
1693                    .flatten()
1694                    .map(without_perf_event_signal),
1695                ),
1696            };
1697            if let Some(errno_result) = errno_result {
1698                super::signal_shadow::record_rt_sigprocmask(
1699                    guest,
1700                    call.how(),
