1701                    set,
1702                    call.oldset(),
1703                    &errno_result,
1704                );
1705            }
1706        }
1707        // The set's probe did not run, and the call restarts
1708        // (`restart_if_first_probe_not_run`): nothing changed, so nothing
1709        // follows from a change; the restarted call does the rest.
1710        if set_not_read && matches!(result, Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ERESTARTNOINTR))) {
1711            return result;
1712        }
1713        if call.how() != libc::SIG_BLOCK && call.set().is_some() {
1714            // The new mask may unblock a pending real-time timer expiration
1715            // that every thread blocked when it expired, which the kernel then
1716            // delivers (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4089). Whatever
1717            // the call returned: an EFAULT copying the old set out comes after
1718            // the change. A mask that cannot be read could unblock any of them.
1719            let pending = pending_real_time_among(guest, REAL_TIME_SIGNALS).await;
1720            if pending != 0 {
1721                let (observable, discarded) =
1722                    match read_thread_wait_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid()) {
1723                        Ok(state) => unblocked_by(&state, state.blocked),
1724                        Err(_) => (REAL_TIME_SIGNALS, 0),
1725                    };
1726                act_on_pending_real_time(guest, pending, observable, discarded).await;
1727            }
1728        }
1729        if self.cfg.backend.virtualizes_guest_sigalrm && guest.thread_state().sigalrm_handled {
1730            // Signal phase 1: the scheduler's blocked bit follows the virtual
1731            // mask, whatever the call returned (an EFAULT copying the old set
1732            // out comes after the change).
1733            self.publish_virtual_sigalrm_blocked(guest).await?;
1734        }
1735        result
1736    }
1737
1738    /// The call itself. Also says whether, and with what set, the kernel ran
1739    /// it: the `SIG_BLOCK` and `SIG_SETMASK` paths read the set through the
1740    /// kernel first and inject nothing when that fails.
1741    async fn rt_sigprocmask_inner<G: Guest<Self>>(
1742        &self,
1743        guest: &mut G,
1744        call: syscalls::RtSigprocmask,
1745    ) -> (Result<i64, Error>, ValidatedSet) {
1746        if call.how() != libc::SIG_BLOCK && call.how() != libc::SIG_SETMASK {
1747            let result = guest.inject_with_retry(call).await;
1748            (result.map_err(Into::into), ValidatedSet::NotValidated)
1749        } else if let Some(set) = call.set() {
1750            let set_mask =
1751                match restart_if_first_probe_not_run(read_kernel_sigset(guest, set).await) {
1752                    Ok(set_mask) => set_mask,
1753                    Err(error) => return (Err(error), ValidatedSet::NotRun),
1754                };
1755            let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
1756            let new_set = stack.push(without_perf_event_signal(set_mask));
1757            let _stack_guard = match stack.commit() {
1758                Ok(guard) => guard,
1759                Err(error) => return (Err(error.into()), ValidatedSet::NotRun),
1760            };
1761            let modified_call = syscalls::RtSigprocmask::new()
1762                .with_how(call.how())
1763                .with_set(Some(new_set.cast()))
1764                .with_oldset(call.oldset())
1765                .with_sigsetsize(call.sigsetsize());
1766            // Keep returning to the handler so post_handler_hook can run, but
1767            // do not expose a tracer preemption as ERESTARTSYS to the guest.
1768            let result = guest.inject_with_retry(modified_call).await;
1769            (
1770                result.map_err(Into::into),
1771                ValidatedSet::Validated(set_mask),
1772            )
1773        } else {
1774            let result = guest.inject_with_retry(call).await;
1775            (result.map_err(Into::into), ValidatedSet::NotValidated)
1776        }
1777    }
1778
1779    /// rt_sigtimedwait system call
1780    ///
1781    /// This is handled by the scheduler and not passed to the record/replay layer,
1782    /// because currently signals are not recorded.
1783    pub async fn handle_rt_sigtimedwait<G: Guest<Self>>(
1784        &self,
1785        guest: &mut G,
1786        call: syscalls::RtSigtimedwait,
1787    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1788        // Linux rejects an invalid mask width before inspecting its pointers.
1789        validate_kernel_sigset_size(call.sigsetsize())?;
1790        // Linux keeps this entry snapshot for the whole wait. The retry helper
1791        // currently injects from the caller's pointer again, so another thread
1792        // can change the set between retries. Fixing that requires sharing one
1793        // scratch-stack guard with the helper's zero-timeout object; do not hide
1794        // that remaining difference by treating this validation read as a snapshot.
1795
1796        let dettid = guest.thread_state().dettid;
1797
1798        let ns_delta = if let Some(timeout) = call.timeout() {
1799            let ts: Timespec = guest.memory().read_value(timeout)?;
1800            Some(Duration::from_secs(ts.tv_sec as u64) + Duration::from_nanos(ts.tv_nsec as u64))
1801        } else {
1802            None
1803        };
1804        let mut rsrc = Resources::new(dettid);
1805        rsrc.insert(ResourceID::InternalIOPolling, Permission::W);
1806        rsrc.fyi("rt_sigtimedwait");
1807        // While it polls, a signal in the set is taken at the next poll
1808        // whatever the thread's mask, which a process-directed signal another
1809        // thread sends must account for
1810        // (`Scheduler::own_process_signal_inputs`). An unreadable set fails in
1811        // the kernel at the first poll.
1812        let waited = match call.set() {
1813            Some(set) => match read_kernel_sigset(guest, set.into()).await {
1814                Err(error @ Error::Errno(Errno::ERESTARTSYS)) => {
1815                    return restart_if_first_probe_not_run(Err(error));
1816                }
1817                read => read.ok(),
1818            },
1819            None => None,
1820        };
1821        // After the set's probe, the call's first injection: observing the
1822        // virtual clock asks the scheduler, which a restart would repeat.
1823        let maybe_timeout = match ns_delta {
1824            Some(ns_delta) => Some(thread_observe_time(guest).await + ns_delta),
1825            None => None,
1826        };
1827        if waited.is_some() {
1828            set_sigtimedwait_set(guest, waited).await;
1829        }
1830        // Linux writes the siginfo only for a signal it returns. A dropped
1831        // copy's siginfo is replaced by what the buffer held before.
1832        let records = crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config());
1833        let before = match call.info() {
1834            Some(info) if self.cfg.backend.reports_child_exit_publication || records => {
1835                Some(read_mapped_prefix(guest, info.as_raw(), 128))
1836            }
1837            _ => None,
1838        };
1839        let result = loop {
1840            let result =
1841                retry_nonblocking_syscall_with_timeout(guest, call, rsrc.clone(), maybe_timeout)
1842                    .await;
1843            // A consumed child-exit copy that the notification ledger drops
1844            // is not returned: the wait goes on, to the same deadline.
1845            let stands = self
1846                .synchronous_sigchld_stands(guest, call, waited, &result)
1847                .await;
1848            // Nor is a copy Linux discarded when it was sent, which Linux never
1849            // dequeues (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293). The
1850            // take went into the guest's own buffer, so the kernel made the
1851            // copy-out with its own partial-write and EFAULT rules for a kept
1852            // copy; for a dropped one, the bytes it changed are put back.
1853            let outcome = if records {
1854                self.take_outcome_of(guest, waited, &result).await
1855            } else {
1856                crate::scheduler::posix_timer_coalesce::TakeOutcome {
1857                    skip: false,
1858                    rewrite: None,
1859                }
1860            };
1861            if stands && !outcome.skip {
1862                // A POSIX timer's entry, or a kept signal standing on another
1863                // sender's record, is returned with its own siginfo
1864                // (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4262>); the
1865                // kernel's copy-out succeeded, so the buffer is writable.
1866                if let (Ok(_), Some(info), Some(rewrite)) = (&result, call.info(), outcome.rewrite)
1867                    && guest
1868                        .memory()
1869                        .write_value(info.cast::<[u8; 128]>(), &rewrite.to_user())
1870                        .is_err()
1871                {
1872                    tracing::warn!(
1873                        "[dtid {}] rt_sigtimedwait's siginfo keeps the physical record",
1874                        guest.thread_state().dettid
1875                    );
1876                }
1877                break result;
1878            }
1879            if let (Some(info), Some(bytes)) = (call.info(), before.as_ref()) {
1880                restore_changed_bytes(guest, info, bytes);
1881            }
1882        };
1883        if waited.is_some() {
1884            set_sigtimedwait_set(guest, None).await;
1885        }
1886        // O2: any completion of a wait for SIGALRM may have dequeued the
1887        // process's ITIMER_REAL copy, including one that then failed copying
1888        // out the siginfo (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4139).
1889        if waited.is_some_and(|set| {
1890            set & crate::syscalls::threads::kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGALRM) != 0
1891        }) {
1892            crate::tool_global::reconcile_alarm(guest).await;
1893        }
1894        result
1895    }
1896
1897    /// What to do with the take that ended this thread's `rt_sigtimedwait`
1898    /// for the signals `waited` with `result` (`take_outcome`, from the
1899    /// queues read after it; for an EFAULT, which names no signal,
1900    /// `DiscardedSignalControl::TakeFaulted`).
1901    async fn take_outcome_of<G: Guest<Self>>(
1902        &self,
1903        guest: &mut G,
1904        waited: Option<u64>,
1905        result: &Result<i64, Error>,
1906    ) -> crate::scheduler::posix_timer_coalesce::TakeOutcome {
1907        use crate::scheduler::posix_timer_coalesce::TakeOutcome;
1908        let skip = |skip| TakeOutcome {
1909            skip,
1910            rewrite: None,
1911        };
1912        match result {
1913            Ok(signal) if (1..32).contains(&(*signal as i32)) => {
1914                let after =
1915                    crate::syscalls::threads::read_kernel_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())
1916                        .ok()
1917                        .map(|state| (state.thread_pending, state.shared_pending));
1918                crate::syscalls::helpers::take_outcome(guest, *signal as i32, after).await
1919            }
1920            Err(Error::Errno(Errno::EFAULT)) => {
1921                let Some(set) = waited else {
1922                    return skip(false);
1923                };
1924                let Ok(state) =
1925                    crate::syscalls::threads::read_kernel_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())
1926                else {
1927                    return skip(false);
1928                };
1929                let at = guest.thread_state().thread_logical_time.as_nanos();
1930                skip(matches!(
1931                    crate::tool_global::discarded_signal(
1932                        guest,
1933                        DiscardedSignalControl::TakeFaulted {
1934                            set,
1935                            thread_pending: state.thread_pending,
1936                            shared_pending: state.shared_pending,
1937                            at,
1938                        },
1939                    )
1940                    .await,
1941                    DiscardedSignalAnswer::Discarded(true)
1942                ))
1943            }
1944            _ => skip(false),
1945        }
1946    }
1947
1948    /// The `signalfd_siginfo` records a successful signalfd `read` returned,
1949    /// `len` bytes at `buf`, reconciled with the child-exit ledger
1950    /// (`reconcile_signalfd_extents`). Returns the bytes kept, or `None` to
1951    /// refuse.
1952    pub(crate) async fn reconcile_signalfd_records<G: Guest<Self>>(
1953        &self,
1954        guest: &mut G,
1955        buf: AddrMut<'_, u8>,
1956        len: usize,
1957        before: &[u8],
1958    ) -> Option<usize> {
1959        self.reconcile_signalfd_extents(guest, &[(buf.as_raw(), len)], len, before)
1960            .await
1961    }
1962
1963    /// The `signalfd_siginfo` records a successful signalfd read returned:
1964    /// the first `read` bytes of the guest buffers `extents` (address,
1965    /// length), in order, as `read`, `readv`, `preadv` and `preadv2` scatter
1966    /// them. They are reconciled with the child-exit ledger as a SIGCHLD
1967    /// delivered to a handler (`Detcore::child_exit_sigchld_delivers`) or
1968    /// consumed by `rt_sigtimedwait` (`synchronous_sigchld_stands`) is: the
1969    /// scheduler's copy gets the siginfo Linux gives the parent, the kernel's
1970    /// coalesced copy for a SIGKILL cohort names the cohort's lowest pid, and
1971    /// a copy for an exit a SIGCHLD already stood for is dropped. Other
1972    /// records are kept as read. The kept records are scattered back over the
1973    /// same extents, in order, with the bytes the buffer held before the read
1974    /// (`before`) over the bytes of dropped ones. Returns the bytes kept, a
1975    /// whole number of records, or `None` when a record is dropped whose bytes
1976    /// `before` does not cover, which the caller refuses
1977    /// (`refuse_unrestorable_signalfd_record`).
1978    pub(crate) async fn reconcile_signalfd_extents<G: Guest<Self>>(
1979        &self,
1980        guest: &mut G,
1981        extents: &[(usize, usize)],
1982        read: usize,
1983        before: &[u8],
1984    ) -> Option<usize> {
1985        let len = read - read % SIGNALFD_RECORD;
1986        let pieces = signalfd_extent_pieces(extents, len);
1987        let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(len);
1988        for (address, length) in &pieces {
1989            let mut piece = vec![0u8; *length];
1990            let Some(address_mut) = AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw(*address) else {
1991                return Some(len);
1992            };
1993            if guest.memory().read_exact(address_mut, &mut piece).is_err() {
1994                use std::os::unix::fs::FileExt;
1995                let read = std::fs::File::open(format!("/proc/{}/mem", guest.tid().as_raw()))
1996                    .and_then(|mem| mem.read_exact_at(&mut piece, *address as u64));
1997                if read.is_err() {
1998                    // The kernel has just written these bytes, so the buffer
1999                    // is mapped and writable; `/proc/<tid>/mem` reads any
2000                    // such page.
2001                    return Some(len);
2002                }
2003            }
2004            bytes.extend_from_slice(&piece);
2005        }
2006        let controls = signalfd_record_controls(&bytes, std::process::id());
2007        let mut answers = Vec::with_capacity(controls.len());
2008        for control in controls {
2009            answers.push(match control {
2010                Some(control) => Some(crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(guest, control).await),
2011                None => None,
2012            });
2013        }
2014        // A record of a copy Linux discarded when it was sent is dropped, as a
2015        // duplicate is: the guest never sees it
2016        // (`crate::syscalls::helpers::take_was_discarded`,
2017        // <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293>).
2018        // A POSIX timer's entry, or a kept signal standing on another
2019        // sender's record, gets its own record in place of the kernel's
2020        // (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4262>), and a stale
2021        // timer entry is dropped as a discarded copy is.
2022        if crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config()) {
2023            for (record, answer) in bytes.chunks_mut(SIGNALFD_RECORD).zip(answers.iter_mut()) {
2024                let signal = signalfd_field(record, 0) as i32;
2025                if !(1..32).contains(&signal) {
2026                    continue;
2027                }
2028                let outcome = crate::syscalls::helpers::take_outcome(guest, signal, None).await;
2029                if outcome.skip {
2030                    *answer = Some(
2031                        crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(true),
2032                    );
2033                } else if let Some(rewrite) = outcome.rewrite {
2034                    record.copy_from_slice(&crate::scheduler::signal_lists::signalfd_record(
2035                        &rewrite,
2036                    ));
2037                }
2038            }
2039        }
2040        let rewritten = match apply_signalfd_answers(&bytes, &answers, before) {
2041            Ok(Some(rewritten)) => rewritten,
2042            Ok(None) => return Some(len),
2043            Err(()) => return None,
2044        };
2045        let mut offset = 0;
2046        for (address, length) in &pieces {
2047            let written = AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw(*address).map(|address| {
2048                guest
2049                    .memory()
2050                    .write_exact(address, &rewritten.bytes[offset..offset + length])
2051            });
2052            if !matches!(written, Some(Ok(()))) {
2053                tracing::warn!(
2054                    "[dtid {}] the signalfd records at {:#x} keep the kernel's siginfo",
2055                    guest.thread_state().dettid,
2056                    address,
2057                );
2058                return Some(len);
2059            }
2060            offset += length;
2061        }
2062        Some(rewritten.kept)
2063    }
2064
2065    /// A child-exit SIGCHLD that a synchronous wait consumed goes through the
2066    /// same notification ledger as one delivered to a handler
2067    /// (`Detcore::child_exit_sigchld_delivers`), on a backend where both the
2068    /// kernel and the scheduler send it (`scheduler::child_exit_sigchld`).
2069    /// The scheduler's copy is returned with the siginfo Linux gives the
2070    /// parent, and a copy for an exit that a SIGCHLD already stood for is
2071    /// dropped. Returns whether the consumed signal stands.
2072    async fn synchronous_sigchld_stands<G: Guest<Self>>(
2073        &self,
2074        guest: &mut G,
2075        call: syscalls::RtSigtimedwait,
2076        waited: Option<u64>,
2077        result: &Result<i64, Error>,
2078    ) -> bool {
2079        if !self.cfg.backend.reports_child_exit_publication {
2080            return true;
2081        }
2082        // Linux copies the set in before it dequeues, and the siginfo out
2083        // after, so an EFAULT with a readable set (`waited`) took a signal it
2084        // never reports; an unreadable set dequeued nothing. Which copy it
2085        // took: a recorded private one if SIGCHLD is gone from the thread's
2086        // private queue, which only committed guest sends fill; otherwise,
2087        // with SIGCHLD the only signal in the set, the shared queue's. A wider
2088        // set falls back to the shared queue's pending bit (the Claude and
2089        // Codex re-checks of https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4131).
2090        if matches!(result, Err(Error::Errno(Errno::EFAULT)))
2091            && guest.config().sequentialize_threads
2092            && let Some(set) = waited
2093        {
2094            let sigchld = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD);
2095            if set & sigchld != 0
2096                && let Ok(state) = crate::syscalls::threads::read_thread_wait_signal_state(
2097                    guest.pid(),
2098                    guest.tid(),
2099                )
2100            {
2101                let shared_pending = if set & !sigchld == 0 {
2102                    false
2103                } else {
2104                    state.shared_pending & sigchld != 0
2105                };
2106                crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
2107                    guest,
2108                    crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl::WaitFaulted {
2109                        private_pending: state.thread_pending & sigchld != 0,
2110                        shared_pending,
2111                    },
2112                )
2113                .await;
2114            }
2115            return true;
2116        }
2117        if !matches!(result, Ok(signal) if *signal == libc::SIGCHLD as i64) {
2118            return true;
2119        }
2120        // Without a siginfo buffer the guest sees only the signal number,
2121        // which every copy shares; the ledger still learns that the wait took
2122        // one (`DequeuedCopy::Unknown`), which may be the shared queue's.
2123        let Some(info) = call.info().map(AddrMut::cast::<GuestSiginfo>) else {
2124            crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
2125                guest,
2126                crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl::Dequeue {
2127                    copy: crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::DequeuedCopy::Unknown,
2128                    admits: false,
2129                },
2130            )
2131            .await;
2132            return true;
2133        };
2134        let Some(bytes) = read_siginfo(guest, info.cast()) else {
2135            return true;
2136        };
2137        Self::synchronous_sigchld_copy_stands(guest, info, &bytes).await
2138    }
2139
2140    /// The child-exit ledger's answer for the `SIGCHLD` with siginfo `bytes`
2141    /// that `rt_sigtimedwait` returned into `info`
2142    /// (`synchronous_sigchld_stands`).
2143    async fn synchronous_sigchld_copy_stands<G: Guest<Self>>(
2144        guest: &mut G,
2145        info: AddrMut<'_, GuestSiginfo>,
2146        bytes: &[u8; 128],
2147    ) -> bool {
2148        use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer;
2149        use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OwnCopy;
2150        // Taken by the wait, a kept copy makes no `InboundSignal` request.
2151        let Some(control) =
2152            crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::control_for(bytes, std::process::id(), false)
2153        else {
2154            return true;
2155        };
2156        match crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(guest, control).await {
2157            ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(dropped) => !dropped,
2158            ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Duplicate) => false,
2159            ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Deliver(own))
2160            | ChildExitSigchldAnswer::ClaimedFor(own) => {
2161                if let Err(error) = guest
2162                    .memory()
2163                    .write_value(info.cast::<[u8; 128]>(), &own.to_bytes())
2164                {
2165                    tracing::warn!(
2166                        "[dtid {}] the SIGCHLD for child {} keeps its siginfo: {}",
2167                        guest.thread_state().dettid,
2168                        own.pid,
2169                        error
2170                    );
2171                }
2172                true
2173            }
2174            _ => true,
2175        }
2176    }
2177
2178    /// Fence an exact self-SIGKILL at the scheduler-selected syscall turn.
2179    ///
2180    /// This applies only when Detcore holds the backend's process signal
2181    /// control (`BackendSignalControlMode::ToolControlled`). A backend that
2182    /// installed that control commits this syscall as an immediate,
2183    /// nonreturning process exit. It therefore has no later return boundary at
2184    /// which the ordinary pending signal path could acquire a delivery permit.
2185    /// Without this preflight, the backend's terminal child callback arrives
2186    /// with no generation-bound reservation and must fail closed.
2187    /// `ResourceID::Exit` is the same fence used by `exit_group`. A run without
2188    /// the installed control (`Unchanged`, including every non-sequentialized
2189    /// run) keeps its ordinary signal path and reserves nothing.
2190    pub(crate) async fn reserve_controlled_self_sigkill_exit<G: Guest<Self>>(
2191        &self,
2192        guest: &mut G,
2193        signal: libc::c_int,
2194        target_process: Option<DetPid>,
2195        target_thread: Option<DetTid>,
2196    ) -> bool {
2197        if guest.signal_control_mode() != reverie::BackendSignalControlMode::ToolControlled {
2198            return false;
2199        }
2200        let (current_thread, mm) = {
2201            let state = guest.thread_state();
2202            (state.dettid, state.mm_id)
2203        };
2204        if !self_sigkill_targets_current_task(
2205            signal,
2206            target_process,
2207            target_thread,
2208            self.detpid,
2209            current_thread,
2210        ) {
2211            return false;
2212        }
2213        let request = guest.thread_state().mk_request(
2214            ResourceID::Exit {
2215                group: true,
2216                process: self.detpid,
2217                mm,
2218            },
2219            Permission::RW,
2220        );
2221        resource_request(guest, request).await;
2222        true
2223    }
2224
2225    /// A backend that cannot deliver a guest's signal (the KVM backend, to
2226    /// another process for example) refuses it with ENOSYS and the typed
2227    /// refusal `UnsupportedOperation::Signal`. Report that refusal by name and
2228    /// route it through the unsupported-operation policy, instead of handing
2229    /// the guest the backend's bare ENOSYS, which left a waiting parent
2230    /// deadlocked. The name goes to tracing and directly to stderr in both
2231    /// dispositions, so neither --log=off nor --log-file hides it. Callers pass
2232    /// the handler's result right after the injection that may have been
2233    /// refused: the next injection replaces the backend's record. Every other
2234    /// result passes through unchanged. No backend is named here; a backend
2235    /// that records no refusal is unaffected.
2236    pub(crate) async fn report_backend_signal_refusal<G: Guest<Self>>(
2237        &self,
2238        guest: &mut G,
2239        sysno: Sysno,
2240        result: Result<i64, Error>,
2241    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
2242        if !matches!(result, Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ENOSYS))) {
2243            return result;
2244        }
2245        let Some(refusal) = guest
2246            .take_unsupported_refusal()
2247            .filter(|refusal| refusal.operation == reverie::UnsupportedOperation::Signal)
2248        else {
2249            return result;
2250        };
2251        let message = format!(
2252            "[dtid {}] {sysno} refused by the backend: {}. No signal was sent.",
2253            guest.thread_state().dettid,
2254            refusal.diagnostic
2255        );
2256        tracing::error!("{message}");
2257        let _ = std::io::Write::write_fmt(
2258            &mut crate::util::RetryingStderr,
2259            format_args!("hermit: {message}\n"),
2260        );
2261        if !self.cfg.panic_on_unsupported_syscalls {
2262            crate::tool_global::report_unsupported_syscall(guest, sysno).await;
2263        }
2264        self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, sysno, Errno::ENOSYS)
2265            .await
2266    }
2267
2268    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2269    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
2270    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1058): Review process-pending signal preservation.
2271    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1119): Review unmaskable process-group SIGKILL forwarding.
2272    /// Resolve signal-zero existence checks in the fixed PID namespace, then route an
2273    /// unambiguous positive-PID process signal through the backend. Backends that can execute
2274    /// with guest PIDs preserve process-directed delivery; DBT translates it to the sole live
2275    /// thread because its native process uses a host PID. An unmaskable SIGKILL to a specific
2276    /// process group is also safe to preserve on backends whose guests use real namespace PIDs;
2277    /// other process-group and broadcast delivery remains refused until Detcore models eligible
2278    /// signal masks.
2279    pub async fn handle_kill<G: Guest<Self>>(
2280        &self,
2281        guest: &mut G,
2282        call: syscalls::Kill,
2283    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
2284        // A group send (pid 0 or below) is not a target here: the paths below
2285        // refuse it (`refuse_group_signal`) unless it is a SIGKILL.
2286        if let Some(refused) = self
2287            .refuse_reserved_signal_send(
2288                guest,
2289                Sysno::kill,
2290                call.sig(),
2291                ReservedSendTarget::Process(call.pid()),
2292                ReservedSendSiginfo::None,
2293            )
2294            .await
2295        {
2296            return refused;
2297        }
2298        // D48 (A5 step 3c.1c): a stop signal under the in-guest delivery
2299        // gate, the caller's own process included.
2300        if let Some(refused) = self
2301            .refuse_stop_signal_send(
2302                guest,
2303                Sysno::kill,
2304                call.sig(),
2305                ReservedSendTarget::Process(call.pid()),
2306                ReservedSendSiginfo::None,
2307            )
2308            .await
2309        {
2310            return refused;
2311        }
2312        self.record_orphaned_group_signals(guest, call.sig()).await;
2313        // A thread below the container's PID namespace names pids of its own
2314        // namespace, where none of Hermit's tasks is visible
2315        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155).
2316        let nested = guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace;
2317        if !guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
2318            // Without sequentialization every other kill goes to the kernel as
2319            // is, but never one to a process group or to every process: the
2320            // guest's process group is inherited from Hermit's, so it can hold
2321            // Hermit and the user's shell or test runner, which a group kill
2322            // would reach (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4057).
2323            // Signal 0 sends nothing, and INT_MIN is ESRCH before any lookup.
2324            if call.sig() != 0 && call.pid() <= 0 && call.pid() != libc::pid_t::MIN {
2325                return self
2326                    .refuse_group_signal(guest, call.pid(), call.sig())
2327                    .await;
2328            }
2329            if !nested
2330                && self
2331                    .drop_signal_to_hermit(guest, Sysno::kill, None, call.pid(), call.sig())
2332                    .await
2333            {
2334                return Ok(0);
2335            }
2336            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
2337        }
2338
2339        // A nested caller's process-group ids are its namespace's, which
2340        // Detcore does not translate yet; a SIGKILL to one would otherwise be
2341        // reserved for the container's group of that number.
2342        if nested && call.pid() <= 0 && call.pid() != libc::pid_t::MIN && call.sig() != 0 {
2343            return self
2344                .refuse_group_signal(guest, call.pid(), call.sig())
2345                .await;
2346        }
2347        let (named, own_init_caught) = if call.pid() > 0 {
2348            match self.name_guest_pid(guest, Sysno::kill, call.pid()).await {
2349                NamedGuestPid::Task {
2350                    task,
2351                    own_init_caught,
2352                } => (task.as_raw(), own_init_caught),
2353                NamedGuestPid::Answer(answer) => return answer,
2354            }
2355        } else {
2356            (call.pid(), None)
2357        };
2358        if Self::own_namespace_init_drops(own_init_caught, call.sig()) {
2359            let task = DetTid::from_raw(named);
2360            return self
2361                .send_to_own_init(guest, call, task, QueueId::Process(task), call.sig())
2362                .await;
2363        }
2364
2365        // Linux's `kill_something_info` answers ESRCH for pid INT_MIN before
2366        // anything else ("-INT_MIN is undefined"), so the kernel's answer is
2367        // deterministic and sends nothing; negating it here would overflow
2368        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4046).
2369        if call.sig() == 0 && call.pid() > 0 {
2370            // An existence check answers from the same model as a signal, so
2371            // that one pid never answers 0 to one and ESRCH to the other: a
2372            // process with no live threads is a zombie (0) or gone (ESRCH)
2373            // whenever Detcore saw it exit, whatever the host has reaped by
2374            // now (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4091).
2375            let (targets, zombie) =
2376                resolve_kill_targets_and_exit(guest, DetPid::from_raw(named)).await;
2377            if targets.is_empty() {
2378                match zombie {
2379                    Some(true) => return Ok(0),
2380                    Some(false) => return Err(Errno::ESRCH.into()),
2381                    None => {}
2382                }
2383            }
2384        }
2385        if call.sig() == 0 || call.pid() == libc::pid_t::MIN {
2386            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
2387        }
2388
2389        let tgid = named;
2390        // A process-group or broadcast SIGALRM is refused by name below. The
2391        // scheduler resolves a thread id to its process itself.
2392        if call.sig() == libc::SIGALRM && tgid > 0 {
2393            refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::SendTo(DetTid::from_raw(tgid))).await?;
2394        }
2395        if self
2396            .drop_signal_to_hermit(guest, Sysno::kill, None, tgid, call.sig())
2397            .await
2398        {
2399            return Ok(0);
2400        }
2401        if can_forward_process_group_signal(
2402            tgid,
2403            call.sig(),
2404            guest
2405                .config()
2406                .backend
2407                .requires_thread_directed_process_signals,
2408        ) {
2409            // The scheduler is not told about each killed process, but it must
2410            // know the group was killed: a held process in it will die, so a
2411            // wait for it is not part of a deadlock
2412            // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3904). Told before
2413            // the kill, inside this committed turn: the sender may be in the
2414            // group and die before the call returns. A kill that then fails
2415            // only suppresses a deadlock report, which is the safe direction.
2416            // `kill(INT_MIN)` names no group; Linux refuses it with ESRCH.
2417            let group = tgid.checked_neg().map(DetPid::from_raw);
2418            if group.is_some() {
2419                crate::tool_global::notify_unresolved_sigkill(guest, group).await;
2420            }
2421            // Every member is retired at this turn
2422            // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3994).
2423            if guest.config().sequentialize_threads
2424                && let Some(group) = group
2425                && let Some(reservation) = reserve_sigkill_group(guest, group).await
2426            {
2427                return self.send_reserved_sigkill(guest, reservation, call).await;
2428            }
2429            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
2430        }
2431        if tgid <= 0 {
2432            return self.refuse_group_signal(guest, tgid, call.sig()).await;
2433        }
2434        // A kill may name the process by any of its live threads' ids (Linux's
2435        // kill_pid_info looks the id up as a task); everything below that acts
2436        // on the process uses its leader
2437        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4094).
2438        let process = signal_target_process(guest, DetPid::from_raw(tgid))
2439            .await
2440            .map_or(tgid, |process| process.as_raw());
2441        // Exact self-SIGKILL is group-fatal, so it has no recipient-selection
2442        // ambiguity even when the process has several live threads. Reserve it
2443        // before the generic process-signal path rejects that thread set.
2444        if self
2445            .reserve_controlled_self_sigkill_exit(
2446                guest,
2447                call.sig(),
2448                Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
2449                None,
2450            )
2451            .await
2452        {
2453            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
2454        }
2455        // A SIGKILL to another process retires every thread of it at this
2456        // turn (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3994). It needs no
2457        // recipient, so a multithreaded target is accepted.
2458        if call.sig() == libc::SIGKILL
2459            && let Some(reservation) = reserve_sigkill(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tgid)).await
2460        {
2461            return self.send_reserved_sigkill(guest, reservation, call).await;
2462        }
2463        match self
2464            .own_process_signal(guest, Sysno::kill, tgid, process, call.sig())
2465            .await?
2466        {
2467            OwnProcessSend::Admitted => {
2468                return self
2469                    .forward_own_process_signal(guest, call.sig(), call, DetTid::from_raw(tgid))
2470                    .await;
2471            }
2472            OwnProcessSend::Refused(answer) => return answer,
2473            OwnProcessSend::NotOwn => {}
2474        }
2475        let named = DetTid::from_raw(tgid);
2476        let tid = match self
2477            .process_signal_target(
2478                guest,
2479                Sysno::kill,
2480                DetPid::from_raw(process),
2481                named,
2482                call.sig(),
2483            )
2484            .await?
2485        {
2486            ProcessSignalTarget::Thread(tid) => tid,
2487            ProcessSignalTarget::SharedQueue => {
2488                return self
2489                    .send_guest_signal(
2490                        guest,
2491                        call,
2492                        DetTid::from_raw(process),
2493                        call.sig(),
2494                        Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
2495                        false,
2496                        named,
2497                    )
2498                    .await;
2499            }
2500            ProcessSignalTarget::Zombie => {
2501                // A zombie: exited, and its parent can still wait for it.
2502                // Linux's kill_pid_info still finds it and the send succeeds,
2503                // discarding the signal, so answer 0 without a system call:
2504                // the host process may already be gone if a backend reaped
2505                // it, and the logical answer must not depend on when
2506                // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4091). Linux
2507                // checks the signal number after finding the task, so an
2508                // invalid one is EINVAL.
2509                if !(1..=libc::SIGRTMAX()).contains(&call.sig()) {
2510                    return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
2511                }
2512                return Ok(0);
2513            }
2514        };
2515        let value = if !guest
2516            .config()
2517            .backend
2518            .requires_thread_directed_process_signals
2519        {
2520            self.send_guest_signal(
2521                guest,
2522                call,
2523                tid,
2524                call.sig(),
2525                Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
2526                false,
2527                DetTid::from_raw(tgid),
2528            )
2529            .await?
2530        } else if process == self.detpid.as_raw()
2531            && tid == guest.thread_state().dettid
2532            && (1..=libc::SIGRTMAX()).contains(&call.sig())
2533        {
2534            // This backend must name the receiving thread, but an original
2535            // process-directed kill still carries SI_USER, not tgkill's
2536            // SI_TKILL. For the current task Linux accepts an explicit SI_USER
2537            // payload. Keep the sender in the same deterministic PID namespace
2538            // as getpid; obtain its real UID from this actual task, not a host
2539            // lookup using the deterministic PID. Other tasks cannot accept
2540            // caller-supplied SI_USER and retain the existing tgkill route.
2541            let uid = guest.inject_with_retry(syscalls::Getuid::new()).await? as u32;
2542            let mut info = [0_u8; 128];
2543            info[0..4].copy_from_slice(&call.sig().to_ne_bytes());
2544            info[8..12].copy_from_slice(&libc::SI_USER.to_ne_bytes());
2545            // Linux x86-64 siginfo's sender union follows three ints and
2546            // alignment padding: pid_t at 16, uid_t at 20.
2547            info[16..20].copy_from_slice(&self.detpid.as_raw().to_ne_bytes());
2548            info[20..24].copy_from_slice(&uid.to_ne_bytes());
2549            let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
2550            let info = stack.push(info);
2551            let _stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
2552            let targeted = syscalls::RtTgsigqueueinfo::new()
2553                .with_tgid(process)
2554                .with_tid(tid.as_raw())
2555                .with_sig(call.sig())
2556                // The syscall wrapper uses AddrMut, but Linux only reads this
2557                // private siginfo. Its committed storage outlives the send.
2558                .with_siginfo(Some(unsafe { info.cast().into_mut() }));
2559            // A process-directed kill: Linux classifies the send against
2560            // the leader (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293).
2561            self.send_guest_signal(
2562                guest,
2563                targeted,
2564                tid,
2565                call.sig(),
2566                Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
2567                false,
2568                DetTid::from_raw(tgid),
2569            )
2570            .await?
2571        } else {
2572            let targeted = syscalls::Tgkill::new()
2573                .with_tgid(process)
2574                .with_tid(tid.as_raw())
2575                .with_sig(call.sig());
2576            self.send_guest_signal(
2577                guest,
2578                targeted,
2579                tid,
2580                call.sig(),
2581                Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
2582                false,
2583                DetTid::from_raw(tgid),
2584            )
2585            .await?
2586        };
2587        self.notify_cross_task_signal(
2588            guest,
2589            tid,
2590            call.sig(),
2591            Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
2592            crate::tool_global::SignalScope::Process,
2593        )
2594        .await;
2595        Ok(value)
2596    }
2597
2598    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2599    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4046)
2600    /// Refuse by name a `kill` to a process group (`pid` 0 for the caller's own,
2601    /// `-pgid` for another) or to every process it may signal (`pid` -1).
2602    ///
2603    /// Linux sends the signal to each process in the set
2604    /// (`__kill_pgrp_info`, `kill_something_info` in kernel/signal.c), and each
2605    /// process then picks its receiving thread as a process-directed signal
2606    /// does. Detcore models neither the set's membership nor that choice, so
2607    /// the call goes through the unsupported-operation policy: a fail-closed
2608    /// run stops with the policy-refusal status and
2609    /// `--allow-unsupported-syscalls` returns ENOSYS, as the call did before.
2610    /// A SIGKILL to another group is forwarded before this point
2611    /// (`can_forward_process_group_signal`) when threads are sequentialized,
2612    /// except on a backend that needs thread-directed process signals (DBT),
2613    /// where it is refused here too. Without sequentialization every such kill
2614    /// is refused here, for host safety: the guest's process group is
2615    /// inherited from Hermit's (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4057).
2616    async fn refuse_group_signal<G: Guest<Self>>(
2617        &self,
2618        guest: &mut G,
2619        pid: libc::pid_t,
2620        sig: libc::c_int,
2621    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
2622        let target = match pid {
2623            0 => "its own process group".to_owned(),
2624            -1 => "every process it may signal".to_owned(),
2625            pid => format!("process group {}", pid.unsigned_abs()),
2626        };
2627        let reason = if guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
2628            "Detcore does not model process-group membership or each process's receiving \
2629             thread (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4046)"
2630        } else {
2631            "with threads not sequentialized (--no-sequentialize-threads, --strace-only) it \
2632             would go to the host kernel as is, and the guest's process group is inherited from \
2633             Hermit's, so it can reach Hermit and the processes that started it \
2634             (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4057)"
2635        };
2636        tracing::error!(
2637            "[detcore, dtid {}] signal {} to {} by kill is not supported: {}",
2638            guest.thread_state().dettid,
2639            sig,
2640            target,
2641            reason,
2642        );
2643        self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, Sysno::kill, Errno::ENOSYS)
2644            .await
2645    }
2646
2647    /// Decide whether a process-directed `sig` that the calling thread sends
2648    /// by `sysno` to `pid`, when `pid` names its own process (`process`, the
2649    /// process that `pid` names: any of its live threads' ids names it), and
2650    /// the process has another live thread, goes to the kernel unchanged
2651    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034). It does when the
2652    /// schedule forces the kernel's choice of receiver and the sender's
2653    /// dequeue (`own_process_signal_refusal` finds nothing against it);
2654    /// otherwise the ordinary path chooses the receiver
2655    /// (`process_signal_target`).
2656    /// Only where the kernel's signal state for a guest thread is the guest's
2657    /// own (`BackendCapabilities::kernel_signal_state_is_guests`: ptrace and
2658    /// e9patch): the decision reads each thread's blocked, pending, ignored
2659    /// and caught sets from the kernel, and Detcore reads every other input
2660    /// from the schedule.
2661    async fn own_process_signal<G: Guest<Self>>(
2662        &self,
2663        guest: &mut G,
2664        sysno: Sysno,
2665        pid: libc::pid_t,
2666        process: libc::pid_t,
2667        sig: libc::c_int,
2668    ) -> Result<OwnProcessSend, Error> {
2669        if !guest.config().backend.kernel_signal_state_is_guests {
2670            return Ok(OwnProcessSend::NotOwn);
2671        }
2672        let sender = guest.thread_state().dettid;
2673        if process != self.detpid.as_raw() {
2674            return Ok(OwnProcessSend::NotOwn);
2675        }
2676        // Linux prefers the thread `pid` names when it takes the signal; a
2677        // sibling's id, like the process's, leaves the sender as the receiver
2678        // only when every sibling blocks it.
2679        let names_sender = pid == sender.as_raw();
2680        let inputs = own_process_signal_inputs(guest).await;
2681        if inputs
2682            .siblings
2683            .iter()
2684            .all(|(_, state)| *state == SiblingSignalState::ExitGranted)
2685        {
2686            return Ok(OwnProcessSend::NotOwn);
2687        }
2688        let state = read_thread_wait_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())?;
2689        let send = OwnProcessSignal {
2690            sig,
2691            names_sender,
2692            blocked: state.blocked,
2693            thread_pending: state.thread_pending,
2694            ignored: state.ignored,
2695            caught: state.caught,
2696            inputs,
2697            record_replay: self.cfg.recordreplay_modes,
2698            schedule_replay: self.cfg.replay_schedule_from.is_some(),
2699            stacktrace_signal: self.cfg.stacktrace_signal.is_some(),
2700            posthook_anchor: !guest.thread_state().stats.hb_pending_posthook.is_empty(),
2701            signalfd: guest.thread_state().holds_a_signalfd(),
2702        };
2703        tracing::debug!(
2704            "[detcore, dtid {}] signal {} to its own process: {:?}",
2705            sender,
2706            sig,
2707            send
2708        );
2709        let Some(refusal) = own_process_signal_refusal(&send) else {
2710            return Ok(OwnProcessSend::Admitted);
2711        };
2712        // A recorded schedule of a send that was forwarded, its sender holding
2713        // its turn, cannot be replayed by a send Detcore routes.
2714        if refusal == OwnProcessSignalRefusal::ScheduleReplay
2715            && own_process_signal_refusal(&OwnProcessSignal {
2716                schedule_replay: false,
2717                ..send.clone()
2718            })
2719            .is_none()
2720        {
2721            tracing::error!(
2722                "[detcore, dtid {}] process-directed signal {} to process {} by {} is not \
2723                 supported: {} (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034)",
2724                sender,
2725                sig,
2726                self.detpid,
2727                sysno,
2728                refusal,
2729            );
2730            return Ok(OwnProcessSend::Refused(
2731                self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, sysno, Errno::ENOSYS)
2732                    .await,
2733            ));
2734        }
2735        tracing::debug!(
2736            "[detcore, dtid {}] process-directed signal {} to its own process {} by {} is not \
2737             forwarded as it is: {}; Detcore chooses the receiving thread",
2738            sender,
2739            sig,
2740            self.detpid,
2741            sysno,
2742            refusal,
2743        );
2744        Ok(OwnProcessSend::NotOwn)
2745    }
2746
2747    /// Forward an admitted process-directed signal to the sender's own process
2748    /// unchanged, holding the sender's turn until it takes the signal
2749    /// (`own_signal_turn_hold`). A call the kernel fails sends nothing and
2750    /// holds nothing.
2751    async fn forward_own_process_signal<G: Guest<Self>, C>(
2752        &self,
2753        guest: &mut G,
2754        sig: libc::c_int,
2755        call: C,
2756        named: DetTid,
2757    ) -> Result<i64, Error>
2758    where
2759        C: Into<syscalls::Syscall>,
2760        syscalls::Syscall: From<C>,
2761    {
2762        let token = self
2763            .discard_pre_send(guest, named, QueueId::Process(self.detpid), sig)
2764            .await;
2765        let call: syscalls::Syscall = call.into();
2766        let siginfo = sent_siginfo(guest, &call, sig);
2767        guest.thread_state_mut().own_signal_turn_hold = Some(sig);
2768        let result = self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await;
2769        if result.is_err() {
2770            guest.thread_state_mut().own_signal_turn_hold = None;
2771        } else {
2772            self.discard_sent(guest, token, siginfo).await;
2773        }
2774        Ok(result?)
2775    }
2776
2777    /// Forward a signal to a task of the caller's own PID namespace's init
2778    /// that the kernel drops (`own_namespace_init_drops`), recording, around
2779    /// the send, the copy that ptrace still queues (`discard_pre_send`).
2780    async fn send_to_own_init<G: Guest<Self>, C>(
2781        &self,
2782        guest: &mut G,
2783        call: C,
2784        named: DetTid,
2785        queue: QueueId,
2786        sig: libc::c_int,
2787    ) -> Result<i64, Error>
2788    where
2789        C: Into<syscalls::Syscall>,
2790        syscalls::Syscall: From<C>,
2791    {
2792        let token = self.discard_pre_send(guest, named, queue, sig).await;
2793        let call: syscalls::Syscall = call.into();
2794        let siginfo = sent_siginfo(guest, &call, sig);
2795        let result = self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await;
2796        if result.is_ok() {
2797            self.discard_sent(guest, token, siginfo).await;
2798        }
2799        Ok(result?)
2800    }
2801
2802    /// Classify, in the sender's turn and before the physical send, a guest's
2803    /// send of `raw_signal` into `queue` naming `named`, and read the queue
2804    /// (`scheduler::discarded_at_send`, <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293>).
2805    /// `None` where Detcore does not sequentialize threads, or for a signal
2806    /// the record does not track.
2807    pub(crate) async fn discard_pre_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
2808        &self,
2809        guest: &mut G,
2810        named: DetTid,
2811        queue: QueueId,
2812        raw_signal: i32,
2813    ) -> Option<DiscardToken> {
2814        if !crate::scheduler::discarded_at_send::records(guest.config())
2815            || !(1..32).contains(&raw_signal)
2816        {
2817            return None;
2818        }
2819        match crate::tool_global::discarded_signal(
2820            guest,
2821            DiscardedSignalControl::PreSend {
2822                named,
2823                queue,
2824                signal: raw_signal,
2825            },
2826        )
2827        .await
2828        {
2829            DiscardedSignalAnswer::Token(token) => Some(token),
2830            _ => None,
2831        }
2832    }
2833
2834    /// Record a send `token` classified (`discard_pre_send`) once the send
2835    /// succeeded, with the siginfo the receiver sees (`sent_siginfo`).
2836    pub(crate) async fn discard_sent<G: Guest<Self>>(
2837        &self,
2838        guest: &mut G,
2839        token: Option<DiscardToken>,
2840        siginfo: Option<KernelSiginfo>,
2841    ) {
2842        if let Some(token) = token {
2843            crate::tool_global::discarded_signal(
2844                guest,
2845                DiscardedSignalControl::Sent(token, siginfo),
2846            )
2847            .await;
2848        }
2849    }
2850
2851    /// What an `rt_sigqueueinfo` whose `siginfo_t` Hermit may not be able to
2852    /// read comes to, decided before admission (`do_rt_sigqueueinfo` in
2853    /// kernel/signal.c). No call that Linux could deliver reaches the kernel
2854    /// without admission (Codex re-check 2 of
2855    /// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4116).
2856    ///
2857    /// The kernel always copies the first 48 bytes (`kernel_siginfo_t`) and
2858    /// the other 80 only for a `si_code` whose layout it does not know
2859    /// (`copy_siginfo_from_user`). Its `copy_from_user` reads what user mode
2860    /// can: on x86 a page with any of read, write or execute permission; it
2861    /// faults on a `PROT_NONE` page; and a fault in a hole just below a
2862    /// grow-down mapping can grow that mapping and succeed. The tracer's own
2863    /// reads say nothing about that (`process_vm_readv` refuses a write-only
2864    /// page, and `/proc/<tid>/mem` force-reads a `PROT_NONE` one), so the
2865    /// mappings of `/proc/<tid>/smaps` decide first:
2866    /// - the first byte of the 48 that user mode cannot read lies in a
2867    ///   `PROT_NONE` mapping, or in a hole whose next mapping above is not
2868    ///   grow-down (`VmFlags` `gd`; only that mapping can grow into it):
2869    ///   EFAULT is certain, and Hermit answers it without the kernel;
2870    /// - any other byte of the 128 that user mode cannot read (a hole below a
2871    ///   grow-down mapping, a tail running into a hole or a `PROT_NONE` page),
2872    ///   or a listing that cannot be read: `Unreadable`, refused by name;
2873    /// - all 128 user-readable: the tracer reads them (`process_vm_readv`, then
2874    ///   `/proc/<tid>/mem` for a write-only page), and a page neither reaches
2875    ///   (a `VM_IO`/`VM_PFNMAP` page such as the vDSO's `[vvar]`) is
2876    ///   `Unreadable`. A `si_code` only the target thread itself may use
2877    ///   (`si_code >= 0` or `SI_TKILL`, from a caller that is not `pid`) is
2878    ///   then `KernelFails`: after a complete copy Linux fails it with EPERM,
2879    ///   or E2BIG first, and sends nothing, so it goes to the kernel unchanged.
2880    fn rt_sigqueueinfo_precheck<G: Guest<Self>>(
2881        &self,
2882        guest: &mut G,
2883        call: &syscalls::RtSigqueueinfo,
2884    ) -> RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck {
2885        const SIZE: usize = std::mem::size_of::<libc::siginfo_t>();
2886        // `sizeof(kernel_siginfo_t)` on x86_64: the part every copy reads.
2887        const KERNEL_SIGINFO: usize = 48;
2888        let address = call.siginfo().map_or(0, |address| address.as_raw());
2889        let Ok(smaps) = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("/proc/{}/smaps", guest.tid().as_raw()))
2890        else {
2891            return RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Unreadable;
2892        };
2893        let mappings = parse_smaps(&smaps);
2894        match first_user_unreadable(&mappings, address, KERNEL_SIGINFO) {
2895            None => {}
2896            Some(Unreadable::NoPermission)
2897            | Some(Unreadable::Hole {
2898                below_growsdown: false,
2899            }) => {
2900                return RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Efault;
2901            }
2902            Some(Unreadable::Hole {
2903                below_growsdown: true,
2904            }) => {
2905                return RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Unreadable;
2906            }
2907        }
2908        if first_user_unreadable(&mappings, address, SIZE).is_some() {
2909            return RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Unreadable;
2910        }
2911        let read = call.siginfo().and_then(|info| {
2912            guest.memory().read_value(info).ok().or_else(|| {
2913                use std::os::unix::fs::FileExt;
2914                let mut bytes = [0u8; SIZE];
2915                std::fs::File::open(format!("/proc/{}/mem", guest.tid().as_raw()))
2916                    .and_then(|mem| mem.read_exact_at(&mut bytes, address as u64))
2917                    .ok()?;
2918                // SAFETY: `siginfo_t` is plain data, and `bytes` holds exactly
2919                // one, read from the guest.
2920                Some(unsafe { std::ptr::read_unaligned(bytes.as_ptr().cast::<libc::siginfo_t>()) })
2921            })
2922        });
2923        let Some(info) = read else {
2924            return RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Unreadable;
2925        };
2926        let code = info.si_code;
2927        if (code >= 0 || code == libc::SI_TKILL)
2928            && guest.thread_state().dettid.as_raw() != call.tgid()
2929        {
2930            RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::KernelFails
2931        } else {
2932            RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Proceeds
2933        }
2934    }
2935
2936    /// Record, in the sending call's turn and before it reaches the kernel, the
2937    /// signals a send of a default-stop signal lets Linux send to an orphaned
2938    /// process group later (`orphaned_group_signals`), for every process of
2939    /// the container, whatever the target and its disposition and whether the
2940    /// send then succeeds: no gated wait ends for them, and a process-directed
2941    /// send the sender does not block them for is refused
2942    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034). A send of SIGSTKFLT,
2943    /// Reverie's timer marker, is recorded too
2944    /// (`Scheduler::record_guest_marker_signal`).
2945    pub(crate) async fn record_orphaned_group_signals<G: Guest<Self>>(
2946        &self,
2947        guest: &mut G,
2948        sig: libc::c_int,
2949    ) {
2950        if sig == libc::SIGSTKFLT {
2951            crate::tool_global::record_guest_marker_signal(guest).await;
2952        }
2953        let signals = orphaned_group_signals(sig);
2954        if signals != 0 && guest.config().backend.host_timed_signals_are_physical {
2955            record_host_timed_signals(guest, HostTimedSignalScope::Container, signals).await;
2956        }
2957    }
2958
2959    /// Whether to drop, answering 0 and sending nothing, a signal `sig` the
2960    /// guest addresses to `target` (a pid, or a tid in thread group `tgid` for
2961    /// the calls that name one) that is one of Hermit's own tasks: its container
2962    /// init, PID 1 of the guest's PID namespace, or another thread of that
2963    /// process. A forwarded SIGTERM ended Hermit's run
2964    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4066).
2965    ///
2966    /// This is what Linux does for a signal sent from inside a PID namespace to
2967    /// its init when the init has no handler for it: `sig_task_ignored` drops
2968    /// it (the init is `SIGNAL_UNKILLABLE`, and a sender in its own namespace
2969    /// does not force it), SIGKILL included, and the call returns 0. To the
2970    /// guest Hermit is such an init; the handlers Hermit installs are for
2971    /// supervisors outside the namespace. Signal 0, an invalid signal number
2972    /// and a nonpositive target go to the caller's own path, as do targets
2973    /// that are guest tasks ([`crate::tool_global::hermit_task_named`]).
2974    /// The task a positive pid the calling thread names refers to. At depth 0
2975    /// the pid is the DetTid. In a nested PID namespace it is a pid of that
2976    /// namespace, translated through the scheduler's record
2977    /// (`scheduler::pid_namespaces`, https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155);
2978    /// a pid no task of the namespace has is ESRCH, as Linux's `find_vpid`
2979    /// answers. Detcore's own bookkeeping uses the translated task; a call
2980    /// forwarded to the kernel keeps the guest's number, which the kernel reads
2981    /// in the caller's namespace. On a backend that rewrites a process signal
2982    /// into a thread-directed one with Detcore's ids, a nested caller is
2983    /// refused by name.
2984    async fn name_guest_pid<G: Guest<Self>>(
2985        &self,
2986        guest: &mut G,
2987        sysno: Sysno,
2988        pid: libc::pid_t,
2989    ) -> NamedGuestPid {
2990        if !guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace {
2991            return NamedGuestPid::Task {
2992                task: DetTid::from_raw(pid),
2993                own_init_caught: None,
2994            };
2995        }
2996        let refusal = if guest
2997            .config()
2998            .backend
2999            .requires_thread_directed_process_signals
3000        {
3001            "this backend sends process signals with Detcore's container-namespace ids".to_owned()
3002        } else {
3003            match crate::tool_global::guest_pid(guest, pid).await {
3004                GuestPidAnswer::Task {
3005                    task,
3006                    own_init_caught,
3007                } => {
3008                    return NamedGuestPid::Task {
3009                        task,
3010                        own_init_caught,
3011                    };
3012                }
3013                GuestPidAnswer::NoSuchPid => {
3014                    return NamedGuestPid::Answer(Err(Errno::ESRCH.into()));
3015                }
3016                GuestPidAnswer::Untranslatable(reason) => reason,
3017            }
3018        };
3019        tracing::error!(
3020            "[detcore, dtid {}] {} naming pid {} from a nested PID namespace is not supported: {} (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155)",
3021            guest.thread_state().dettid,
3022            sysno,
3023            pid,
3024            refusal,
3025        );
3026        NamedGuestPid::Answer(
3027            self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, sysno, Errno::ENOSYS)
3028                .await,
3029        )
3030    }
3031
3032    /// Whether the kernel drops `sig` sent to a task of the caller's own PID
3033    /// namespace's init (`own_init_caught`, `name_guest_pid`): Linux drops it
3034    /// unless the init has a handler for it, and SIGKILL and SIGSTOP always
3035    /// (`SIGNAL_UNKILLABLE`, `sig_task_ignored`). Such a call is forwarded
3036    /// unchanged: the kernel makes its own checks (signal number, siginfo),
3037    /// then drops the signal and answers 0, and nothing reaches a task, so
3038    /// Detcore has nothing to schedule or record.
3039    pub(crate) fn own_namespace_init_drops(own_init_caught: Option<u64>, sig: libc::c_int) -> bool {
3040        own_init_caught.is_some_and(|caught| {
3041            (1..=libc::SIGRTMAX()).contains(&sig)
3042                && (sig == libc::SIGKILL
3043                    || sig == libc::SIGSTOP
3044                    || caught & kernel_sigset_bit(sig) == 0)
3045        })
3046    }
3047
3048    async fn drop_signal_to_hermit<G: Guest<Self>>(
3049        &self,
3050        guest: &mut G,
3051        sysno: Sysno,
3052        tgid: Option<libc::pid_t>,
3053        target: libc::pid_t,
3054        sig: libc::c_int,
3055    ) -> bool {
3056        if !(1..=libc::SIGRTMAX()).contains(&sig) || target <= 0 {
3057            return false;
3058        }
3059        if !crate::tool_global::names_hermit_task(guest, tgid, target).await {
3060            return false;
3061        }
3062        tracing::info!(
3063            "[detcore, dtid {}] {} of signal {} to task {}, one of Hermit's own: dropped and \
3064             answered 0, as Linux drops a signal from inside a PID namespace to its init \
3065             (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4066)",
3066            guest.thread_state().dettid,
3067            sysno,
3068            sig,
3069            target,
3070        );
3071        true
3072    }
3073
3074    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
3075    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034)
3076    /// How the process-directed signal `sig`, which the calling thread sends
3077    /// by `sysno` naming the task `named` of process `tgid`, reaches it.
3078    ///
3079    /// With one live thread, that thread takes it however it is sent. With
3080    /// several, Linux's `complete_signal` (kernel/signal.c) picks the thread,
3081    /// and Detcore applies the same rule to its own model of the threads'
3082    /// masks, pending signals and run states
3083    /// (`Scheduler::process_signal_receiver`,
3084    /// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034): the receiver, or no
3085    /// thread when none wants it. Where that model is not available (a
3086    /// backend that does not model signal targets, a mask Detcore does not
3087    /// know, an exec in flight, a run that records or replays), the send is
3088    /// refused through the unsupported-operation policy, so a fail-closed run
3089    /// stops with the policy-refusal status and `--allow-unsupported-syscalls`
3090    /// returns ENOSYS.
3091    pub(crate) async fn process_signal_target<G: Guest<Self>>(
3092        &self,
3093        guest: &mut G,
3094        sysno: Sysno,
3095        tgid: DetPid,
3096        named: DetTid,
3097        sig: libc::c_int,
3098    ) -> Result<ProcessSignalTarget, Error> {
3099        let (targets, zombie) = resolve_kill_targets_and_exit(guest, tgid).await;
3100        // SIGKILL ends the whole thread group whichever thread receives it, so
3101        // it needs no recipient choice. On a backend that sends process
3102        // signals to a named thread (DBT), whose kill notice retires the
3103        // target's whole group (`Scheduler::note_process_sigkill`), send it to
3104        // the lowest live thread (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4362).
3105        // Elsewhere a SIGKILL to another process is reserved and retired
3106        // before this point.
3107        if sig == libc::SIGKILL
3108            && targets.len() > 1
3109            && guest
3110                .config()
3111                .backend
3112                .requires_thread_directed_process_signals
3113        {
3114            return Ok(ProcessSignalTarget::Thread(targets[0]));
3115        }
3116        match deterministic_kill_target(&targets, zombie, sig) {
3117            Err(Errno::ENOSYS) => {}
3118            Ok(None) => return Ok(ProcessSignalTarget::Zombie),
3119            Ok(Some(thread)) => return Ok(ProcessSignalTarget::Thread(thread)),
3120            Err(errno) => return Err(errno.into()),
3121        }
3122        // Reverie takes these for itself, so the receiver's handler would never
3123        // run (`APPROPRIATED_SIGNALS`).
3124        let appropriated = APPROPRIATED_SIGNALS
3125            .iter()
3126            .find(|(signal, _)| *signal == sig)
3127            .map(|(_, why)| *why);
3128        // A schedule replay decides as a run does: every input of the choice
3129        // is a function of the schedule, which the replay follows, and the
3130        // forwarded copy is taken by the first thread the replay resumes that
3131        // does not block it, which is the thread its recording resumed
3132        // (`Scheduler::process_signal_receiver` refuses a replay whose
3133        // schedule runs another first).
3134        let receiver = if appropriated.is_some() || self.cfg.recordreplay_modes {
3135            None
3136        } else {
3137            Some(crate::tool_global::process_signal_receiver(guest, tgid, named, sig).await)
3138        };
3139        let reason = match receiver {
3140            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::Thread { thread, .. }) => {
3141                return Ok(ProcessSignalTarget::Thread(thread));
3142            }
3143            // Linux queues no second copy and chooses no thread: the call is
3144            // forwarded, and the thread chosen for the copy already queued
3145            // keeps it.
3146            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::AlreadyQueued) => {
3147                return Ok(ProcessSignalTarget::SharedQueue);
3148            }
3149            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::SharedQueue) => {
3150                return Ok(ProcessSignalTarget::SharedQueue);
3151            }
3152            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::UnknownMask(thread)) => {
3153                format!("thread {thread}'s signal mask is unknown")
3154            }
3155            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::SenderTakesIt) => "the sending thread does not block the \
3156                 signal, so under ptrace, where every other thread of the process is stopped, it \
3157                 is the one thread the kernel's complete_signal admits, and it would take the \
3158                 copy before the receiving thread could"
3159                .to_owned(),
3160            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::OutOfSchedule(thread)) => format!(
3161                "thread {thread} runs or sleeps outside the schedule and does not block the \
3162                 signal, so the kernel can wake it for the copy at a moment host timing sets"
3163            ),
3164            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::TakenOutOfTurn(thread)) => format!(
3165                "thread {thread} can take the copy from the shared queue outside its turn: its \
3166                 call's temporary mask admits the signal, a signalfd of the process covers it, \
3167                 or it waits for it in rt_sigtimedwait"
3168            ),
3169            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::ExecInFlight) => {
3170                "an exec is in flight in the process".to_owned()
3171            }
3172            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::NotModeled) => {
3173                "Detcore models the threads' signal masks only where the kernel's signal \
3174                 state is the guest's (ptrace)"
3175                    .to_owned()
3176            }
3177            Some(ProcessSignalReceiver::ScheduleRunsFirst { receiver, first }) => format!(
3178                "the replayed schedule runs thread {first} next, which could take the copy \
3179                 before thread {receiver}, the thread its recording gave it: the replay has \
3180                 left its recording"
3181            ),
3182            None => match appropriated {
3183                Some(why) => format!("the receiving thread could not take it: {why}"),
3184                None => "the run records or replays, and a replay does not reproduce the masks \
3185                         the choice reads"
3186                    .to_owned(),
3187            },
3188        };
3189        tracing::error!(
3190            "[detcore, dtid {}] process-directed signal {} to process {} by {} is not supported: \
3191             the process has {} live threads, and Detcore cannot choose the receiving thread \
3192             as Linux's complete_signal does: {} \
3193             (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034)",
3194            guest.thread_state().dettid,
3195            sig,
3196            tgid,
3197            sysno,
3198            targets.len(),
3199            reason,
3200        );
3201        self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, sysno, Errno::ENOSYS)
3202            .await
3203            .and(Err(Errno::ENOSYS.into()))
3204    }
3205
3206    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
3207    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
3208    /// Send a thread-directed signal through the kernel. Guest PID/TID values are
3209    /// stable in the fresh PID namespace and delivery is scheduler-serialized.
3210    pub async fn handle_tgkill<G: Guest<Self>>(
3211        &self,
3212        guest: &mut G,
3213        call: syscalls::Tgkill,
3214    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
3215        if let Some(refused) = self
3216            .refuse_reserved_signal_send(
3217                guest,
3218                Sysno::tgkill,
3219                call.sig(),
3220                ReservedSendTarget::Thread {
3221                    tgid: Some(call.tgid()),
3222                    tid: call.tid(),
3223                },
3224                ReservedSendSiginfo::None,
3225            )
3226            .await
3227        {
3228            return refused;
3229        }
3230        // D48 (A5 step 3c.1c): a stop signal under the in-guest delivery
3231        // gate, the caller's own process included.
3232        if let Some(refused) = self
3233            .refuse_stop_signal_send(
3234                guest,
3235                Sysno::tgkill,
3236                call.sig(),
3237                ReservedSendTarget::Thread {
3238                    tgid: Some(call.tgid()),
3239                    tid: call.tid(),
3240                },
3241                ReservedSendSiginfo::None,
3242            )
3243            .await
3244        {
3245            return refused;
3246        }
3247        self.record_orphaned_group_signals(guest, call.sig()).await;
3248        // A nested caller names its own namespace's ids; Detcore's model uses
3249        // the tasks they name, and the forwarded call keeps them
3250        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155). Non-positive ids
3251        // are EINVAL in Linux before any lookup.
3252        let nested = guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace;
3253        let (tgid, tid) = if nested && call.tgid() > 0 && call.tid() > 0 {
3254            let (tid, own_init_caught) =
3255                match self.name_guest_pid(guest, Sysno::tgkill, call.tid()).await {
3256                    NamedGuestPid::Task {
3257                        task,
3258                        own_init_caught,
3259                    } => (task, own_init_caught),
3260                    NamedGuestPid::Answer(answer) => return answer,
3261                };
3262            let tgid = match self.name_guest_pid(guest, Sysno::tgkill, call.tgid()).await {
3263                NamedGuestPid::Task { task, .. } => task,
3264                NamedGuestPid::Answer(answer) => return answer,
3265            };
3266            if Self::own_namespace_init_drops(own_init_caught, call.sig()) {
3267                return self
3268                    .send_to_own_init(guest, call, tid, QueueId::Thread(tid), call.sig())
3269                    .await;
3270            }
3271            (tgid.as_raw(), tid.as_raw())
3272        } else {
3273            (call.tgid(), call.tid())
3274        };
3275        if call.sig() == libc::SIGALRM {
3276            refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::SendTo(DetTid::from_raw(tid))).await?;
3277        }
3278        if !nested
3279            && self
3280                .drop_signal_to_hermit(guest, Sysno::tgkill, Some(tgid), tid, call.sig())
3281                .await
3282        {
3283            return Ok(0);
3284        }
3285        let _reserved = self
3286            .reserve_controlled_self_sigkill_exit(
3287                guest,
3288                call.sig(),
3289                Some(DetPid::from_raw(tgid)),
3290                Some(DetTid::from_raw(tid)),
3291            )
3292            .await;
3293        // A SIGKILL to a thread of another process ends that whole process,
3294        // and is retired at this turn, like `kill`'s. Only a valid pair is
3295        // reserved: Linux rejects a thread outside the named group (ESRCH) or a
3296        // non-positive id (EINVAL) before anything happens, so such a send
3297        // takes the ordinary path and gets the kernel's errno. Nor is a send
3298        // to a leader that has exited while its siblings run on
3299        // (`sigkill_names_an_exited_leader`).
3300        let mut exited_leader = false;
3301        if call.sig() == libc::SIGKILL
3302            && guest.config().sequentialize_threads
3303            && tgid > 0
3304            && tid > 0
3305            && registered_process_of(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await
3306                == Some(DetPid::from_raw(tgid))
3307        {
3308            exited_leader = sigkill_names_an_exited_leader(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await;
3309            if !exited_leader
3310                && let Some(reservation) = reserve_sigkill(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await
3311            {
3312                return self.send_reserved_sigkill(guest, reservation, call).await;
3313            }
3314        }
3315        let value = self
3316            .send_guest_signal(
3317                guest,
3318                call,
3319                DetTid::from_raw(call.tid()),
3320                call.sig(),
3321                None,
3322                true,
3323                DetTid::from_raw(tid),
3324            )
3325            .await?;
3326        // The kernel accepted a SIGKILL that changes nothing: no thread takes
3327        // it and no process dies, so the scheduler is not told of a kill.
3328        if exited_leader {
