3329            return Ok(value);
3330        }
3331        // `pthread_kill` lowers to `tgkill`, so this is the ordinary way one
3332        // guest THREAD signals a sibling. Like `kill`, a successful cross-task
3333        // send must tell the scheduler, or a target parked in a child wait is
3334        // never woken and the wait hangs.
3335        self.notify_cross_task_signal(
3336            guest,
3337            DetTid::from_raw(tid),
3338            call.sig(),
3339            None,
3340            crate::tool_global::SignalScope::Thread,
3341        )
3342        .await;
3343        Ok(value)
3344    }
3345
3346    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
3347    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#812)
3348    /// Send a thread-directed signal through the older two-argument `tkill`.
3349    /// Like its `tgkill` sibling, the target thread is addressed by a guest TID
3350    /// that is stable in the fresh PID namespace and delivery is
3351    /// scheduler-serialized, so forwarding the kernel call is deterministic.
3352    pub async fn handle_tkill<G: Guest<Self>>(
3353        &self,
3354        guest: &mut G,
3355        call: syscalls::Tkill,
3356    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
3357        if let Some(refused) = self
3358            .refuse_reserved_signal_send(
3359                guest,
3360                Sysno::tkill,
3361                call.sig(),
3362                ReservedSendTarget::Thread {
3363                    tgid: None,
3364                    tid: call.tid(),
3365                },
3366                ReservedSendSiginfo::None,
3367            )
3368            .await
3369        {
3370            return refused;
3371        }
3372        // D48 (A5 step 3c.1c): a stop signal under the in-guest delivery
3373        // gate, the caller's own process included.
3374        if let Some(refused) = self
3375            .refuse_stop_signal_send(
3376                guest,
3377                Sysno::tkill,
3378                call.sig(),
3379                ReservedSendTarget::Thread {
3380                    tgid: None,
3381                    tid: call.tid(),
3382                },
3383                ReservedSendSiginfo::None,
3384            )
3385            .await
3386        {
3387            return refused;
3388        }
3389        self.record_orphaned_group_signals(guest, call.sig()).await;
3390        // A nested caller names its own namespace's tid
3391        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155).
3392        let nested = guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace;
3393        let tid = if nested && call.tid() > 0 {
3394            match self.name_guest_pid(guest, Sysno::tkill, call.tid()).await {
3395                NamedGuestPid::Task {
3396                    task,
3397                    own_init_caught,
3398                } => {
3399                    if Self::own_namespace_init_drops(own_init_caught, call.sig()) {
3400                        return self
3401                            .send_to_own_init(guest, call, task, QueueId::Thread(task), call.sig())
3402                            .await;
3403                    }
3404                    task.as_raw()
3405                }
3406                NamedGuestPid::Answer(answer) => return answer,
3407            }
3408        } else {
3409            call.tid()
3410        };
3411        if call.sig() == libc::SIGALRM {
3412            refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::SendTo(DetTid::from_raw(tid))).await?;
3413        }
3414        if !nested
3415            && self
3416                .drop_signal_to_hermit(guest, Sysno::tkill, None, tid, call.sig())
3417                .await
3418        {
3419            return Ok(0);
3420        }
3421        let _reserved = self
3422            .reserve_controlled_self_sigkill_exit(
3423                guest,
3424                call.sig(),
3425                None,
3426                Some(DetTid::from_raw(tid)),
3427            )
3428            .await;
3429        // A non-positive tid is EINVAL in Linux, before anything happens.
3430        let mut exited_leader = false;
3431        if call.sig() == libc::SIGKILL && guest.config().sequentialize_threads && tid > 0 {
3432            exited_leader = sigkill_names_an_exited_leader(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await;
3433            if !exited_leader
3434                && let Some(reservation) = reserve_sigkill(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await
3435            {
3436                return self.send_reserved_sigkill(guest, reservation, call).await;
3437            }
3438        }
3439        let value = self
3440            .send_guest_signal(
3441                guest,
3442                call,
3443                DetTid::from_raw(call.tid()),
3444                call.sig(),
3445                None,
3446                true,
3447                DetTid::from_raw(tid),
3448            )
3449            .await?;
3450        // As in `tgkill`, a SIGKILL to an exited leader changes nothing.
3451        if exited_leader {
3452            return Ok(value);
3453        }
3454        // Same wakeup obligation as `tgkill`; `tkill` is the older two-argument
3455        // spelling of the same thread-directed send.
3456        self.notify_cross_task_signal(
3457            guest,
3458            DetTid::from_raw(tid),
3459            call.sig(),
3460            None,
3461            crate::tool_global::SignalScope::Thread,
3462        )
3463        .await;
3464        Ok(value)
3465    }
3466
3467    /// Complete a reserved SIGKILL (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3994).
3468    ///
3469    /// A send whose victims exclude the caller's own process returns: it is
3470    /// sent, and then committed with its real result. One that includes the
3471    /// caller's process cannot return, because the kernel ends the caller:
3472    /// the caller records its own exit status for its parent (`CLD_KILLED`,
3473    /// `SIGKILL`), takes the `exit_group` grant for its own process, as
3474    /// `exit_group` does, commits the other victims inside that exit turn, and
3475    /// then sends. The commit comes after the grant: it installs the physical
3476    /// fence, which waits for victims that only the send itself kills. The
3477    /// reservation validated the target against the scheduler's record, so the
3478    /// send cannot fail in a way the caller sees.
3479    pub(crate) async fn send_reserved_sigkill<G: Guest<Self>>(
3480        &self,
3481        guest: &mut G,
3482        reservation: crate::scheduler::sigkill_retirement::SigkillReservation,
3483        call: impl Into<syscalls::Syscall> + Copy,
3484    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
3485        if !reservation.includes_sender {
3486            self.record_sigkill_robust_lists(guest, reservation.token)
3487                .await;
3488            let result = self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await;
3489            commit_sigkill(guest, reservation.token, result.is_ok()).await;
3490            return Ok(result?);
3491        }
3492        if self.cfg.backend.reports_kernel_child_exit_signals {
3493            let uid = crate::syscalls::real_uid(guest.tid().as_raw())
3494                .unwrap_or_else(|| unsafe { libc::getuid() });
3495            crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
3496                guest,
3497                crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl::ExitStatus {
3498                    code: libc::CLD_KILLED,
3499                    status: libc::SIGKILL,
3500                    uid,
3501                },
3502            )
3503            .await;
3504        }
3505        let request = guest.thread_state().mk_request(
3506            ResourceID::Exit {
3507                group: true,
3508                process: guest.thread_state().detpid.expect("detpid unset"),
3509                mm: guest.thread_state().mm_id,
3510            },
3511            Permission::RW,
3512        );
3513        resource_request(guest, request).await;
3514        // After the grant: from filing the request until it is granted, other
3515        // threads may still run and change their lists.
3516        self.record_sigkill_robust_lists(guest, reservation.token)
3517            .await;
3518        commit_sigkill(guest, reservation.token, true).await;
3519        Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?)
3520    }
3521
3522    /// Send a guest's signal with `syscall`, first recording, for a `SIGCHLD`,
3523    /// the queue its copy will join (`record_guest_sigchld_send`), and undoing
3524    /// that record if the send fails. Recorded before the physical send: a
3525    /// thread outside the schedule (in a real `rt_sigsuspend` or blocking
3526    /// call) can take the copy the moment it is queued, before this handler
3527    /// could record it afterwards (the Claude re-check of
3528    /// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4131). `thread_directed`: the
3529    /// call names one thread (`tkill`, `tgkill`, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, a
3530    /// thread-scoped `pidfd_send_signal`), whose private queue the copy
3531    /// joins, as it does on a backend that must name a thread for every
3532    /// process signal. `named`: the task Linux reads to decide whether it
3533    /// discards the signal (`kill`'s pid, `tgkill`'s tid), which the
3534    /// discarded-at-send record classifies the send against
3535    /// (`discard_pre_send`).
3536    #[expect(
3537        clippy::too_many_arguments,
3538        reason = "each argument is one fact about the send the kernel call cannot tell"
3539    )]
3540    pub(crate) async fn send_guest_signal<G, S>(
3541        &self,
3542        guest: &mut G,
3543        syscall: S,
3544        target: DetTid,
3545        raw_signal: i32,
3546        target_process: Option<DetPid>,
3547        thread_directed: bool,
3548        named: DetTid,
3549    ) -> Result<i64, Error>
3550    where
3551        G: Guest<Self>,
3552        S: Into<reverie::syscalls::Syscall>,
3553    {
3554        use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer;
3555        use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl;
3556        let syscall: syscalls::Syscall = syscall.into();
3557        // Whether the physical send names one thread, so its copy joins that
3558        // thread's private queue: the call does, or the backend must name a
3559        // thread for every process signal.
3560        let to_one_thread = thread_directed
3561            || guest
3562                .config()
3563                .backend
3564                .requires_thread_directed_process_signals;
3565        // D44 asks about the thread the send reaches: for a thread-directed
3566        // send the one it names, as the scheduler numbers it (`named`, which
3567        // the handlers translate from a nested PID namespace's id; `target`
3568        // there is the caller's own id), and for a process-directed one the
3569        // receiving thread the handler chose (`target`).
3570        let receiver = if thread_directed { named } else { target };
3571        if let Some(refused) = self
3572            .refuse_kernel_run_target_send(guest, syscall, receiver, named, raw_signal)
3573            .await
3574        {
3575            return refused;
3576        }
3577        let mut undo = None;
3578        if raw_signal == libc::SIGCHLD && guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
3579            // Without a process, or sent to one thread, the copy joins the
3580            // target's private queue.
3581            if to_one_thread || target_process.is_none() {
3582                let answer = crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
3583                    guest,
3584                    ChildExitSigchldControl::PrivateSent { target },
3585                )
3586                .await;
3587                // A copy already pending there coalesces: only a new record is
3588                // undone.
3589                if answer == ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Placed(true) {
3590                    undo = Some(ChildExitSigchldControl::SendFailed {
3591                        private: Some(target),
3592                        shared: None,
3593                    });
3594                }
3595            } else if let Some(process) = target_process {
3596                crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
3597                    guest,
3598                    ChildExitSigchldControl::SharedSent { target: process },
3599                )
3600                .await;
3601                undo = Some(ChildExitSigchldControl::SendFailed {
3602                    private: None,
3603                    shared: Some(process),
3604                });
3605            }
3606        }
3607        // Without a process, or sent to one thread, the copy joins the
3608        // target's private queue.
3609        let queue = match target_process {
3610            Some(process) if !to_one_thread => QueueId::Process(process),
3611            _ => QueueId::Thread(target),
3612        };
3613        let token = self.discard_pre_send(guest, named, queue, raw_signal).await;
3614        let siginfo = sent_siginfo(guest, &syscall, raw_signal);
3615        let result = self.record_or_replay(guest, syscall).await;
3616        if result.is_err()
3617            && let Some(undo) = undo
3618        {
3619            crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(guest, undo).await;
3620        }
3621        if result.is_ok() {
3622            self.discard_sent(guest, token, siginfo).await;
3623        }
3624        Ok(result?)
3625    }
3626
3627    /// A5 step 3c.1c, design D44(b): under the in-guest delivery gate, refuse a
3628    /// send of `raw_signal` to `target`, which the scheduler's committed state
3629    /// shows blocked in a kernel-run call whose continuation turn has not been
3630    /// granted (`GlobalRequest::KernelRunTarget`, never which pool holds the
3631    /// thread), when the signal would interrupt that call
3632    /// (`wait_signal_end::kernel_run_send_refused`: unblocked by the mask its
3633    /// request published, and caught or default-fatal; for a vfork parent
3634    /// only a non-core-dumping default-fatal one). The call runs in the kernel
3635    /// under the in-guest runtime's mask, so the signal would stay pending and
3636    /// the call never end for it; a kill followed by a `waitpid` would hang.
3637    /// So the send gets `EPERM`, after the run-wide stopping refusal
3638    /// `HERMIT_KERNEL_RUN_SIGNAL_REFUSED` (D49), in the sender's turn; under
3639    /// the gate that ends the run and this does not return. Dispositions that
3640    /// cannot be read refuse, fail closed. Signal 0, SIGKILL (Detcore retires
3641    /// its victims at the sender's turn), a send to the sender itself, and
3642    /// every send with the gate off take the ordinary path (`None`).
3643    ///
3644    /// Only a valid send reaches the refusal: a send `syscall` the kernel
3645    /// would fail before sending anything gets that errno instead, with no
3646    /// refusal (`refused_send_kernel_errno`, with `named`, the task the call
3647    /// names, as the scheduler numbers it).
3648    async fn refuse_kernel_run_target_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
3649        &self,
3650        guest: &mut G,
3651        syscall: syscalls::Syscall,
3652        target: DetTid,
3653        named: DetTid,
3654        raw_signal: i32,
3655    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
3656        let sysno = reverie::syscalls::SyscallInfo::number(&syscall);
3657        if !guest.config().in_guest_signal_delivery
3658            || raw_signal == 0
3659            || raw_signal == libc::SIGKILL
3660            || target == guest.thread_state().dettid
3661        {
3662            return None;
3663        }
3664        let block = crate::tool_global::kernel_run_target(guest, target).await?;
3665        let task = reverie::Pid::from_raw(target.as_raw());
3666        let state = crate::syscalls::read_thread_signal_state(task, task);
3667        let refused = match &state {
3668            Ok(state) => crate::wait_signal_end::kernel_run_send_refused(
3669                state,
3670                block.mask.unwrap_or(state.blocked),
3671                raw_signal,
3672                block.vfork,
3673                guest.config().in_guest_reserved_signals,
3674            ),
3675            Err(_) => true,
3676        };
3677        if !refused {
3678            return None;
3679        }
3680        let (siginfo, group) = refused_send_arguments(syscall);
3681        if let Some(errno) = self
3682            .refused_send_kernel_errno(guest, raw_signal, siginfo, named, group)
3683            .await
3684        {
3685            return Some(Err(errno.into()));
3686        }
3687        let reason = crate::refused_signal_controls::kernel_run_signal_refusal(
3688            sysno.name(),
3689            raw_signal,
3690            Some(guest.thread_state().dettid.as_raw()),
3691            target.as_raw(),
3692            block.vfork,
3693            state.is_err(),
3694        );
3695        tracing::error!("{}", reason);
3696        crate::tool_global::stopping_refusal(guest, reason).await;
3697        Some(Err(Errno::EPERM.into()))
3698    }
3699
3700    /// Tell the scheduler that a successful thread-directed send left a signal
3701    /// physically pending for another task.
3702    ///
3703    /// Shared by `kill`, `tgkill` and `tkill` so the three cannot drift: a fix
3704    /// applied to one spelling of "signal another task" must apply to all of
3705    /// them, which is exactly the gap that let a `pthread_kill` from a sibling
3706    /// thread hang a `waitid` that a `kill` from a sibling process could
3707    /// interrupt. Self-directed signals are excluded: the sender is running, so
3708    /// it is not parked waiting to be woken.
3709    pub(crate) async fn notify_cross_task_signal<G: Guest<Self>>(
3710        &self,
3711        guest: &mut G,
3712        target: DetTid,
3713        raw_signal: i32,
3714        target_process: Option<DetPid>,
3715        scope: crate::tool_global::SignalScope,
3716    ) {
3717        // ⚠️ NO `Signal::try_from` GATE. It used to stand here, and because
3718        // `nix`'s `Signal` models only 1..=31 it rejected EVERY realtime signal:
3719        // measured in-tree, the gate admitted exactly 1..=31 and zero of the 31
3720        // realtime signals. The notification was skipped silently, so a target
3721        // parked on `ResourceID::WaitChild` was never woken and the wait hung.
3722        // `SigWrapper` now carries the raw number, so every deliverable signal
3723        // can be represented and none is dropped for being unnameable. Signal
3724        // zero is only an existence/permission probe and queues no signal.
3725        if should_notify_cross_task_signal(guest.thread_state().dettid, target, raw_signal) {
3726            notify_signal_pending(guest, target, SigWrapper(raw_signal), target_process, scope)
3727                .await;
3728        }
3729    }
3730
3731    /// Refuse, with EPERM and a WARN, a send of one of the in-guest runtime's
3732    /// reserved signals (`Config::in_guest_reserved_signals`) to a process
3733    /// other than the caller's own. `None` leaves the call to its handler's
3734    /// ordinary path, unchanged. A5 step 3c, design section 16.30: such a
3735    /// signal would run the target's runtime handler on a thread that does not
3736    /// hold the scheduler's turn.
3737    ///
3738    /// Each send handler calls this beside its other opening checks (pidfd's
3739    /// once its descriptor names a target), so every later path (record and
3740    /// replay, a nested caller's own-init forward) is covered. It decides from
3741    /// the configured set, the signal number and Detcore's model of processes
3742    /// and their live threads alone:
3743    /// - a signal outside the set takes the ordinary path before any request
3744    ///   is made, so with the set empty (every backend but the in-guest ones'
3745    ///   default route) nothing at all changes; signal 0 is never in it;
3746    /// - a target in the caller's own process takes the ordinary path. A
3747    ///   caller in the container's PID namespace that names its own thread or
3748    ///   process makes no request for it; a caller in a nested namespace first
3749    ///   has each id translated (one `GuestPid` request per id);
3750    /// - only a target with a live thread in Detcore's model is refused. A
3751    ///   target with none takes the ordinary path, which keeps Linux's answer:
3752    ///   a zombie (exited, not yet reaped) still takes a signal (0), and a
3753    ///   reaped process, an id no task has, or a thread that has exited is
3754    ///   ESRCH. So are an id Detcore cannot translate, a non-positive id, and a
3755    ///   thread outside the group `tgkill` names;
3756    /// - group sends (`kill(0)`, `kill(-pgid)`, `kill(-1)`, and
3757    ///   `pidfd_send_signal`'s process-group scope) are not passed here: their
3758    ///   handlers refuse every non-SIGKILL group send first
3759    ///   (`refuse_group_signal`), a stopping refusal or ENOSYS by policy, and
3760    ///   SIGKILL is never reserved.
3761    ///
3762    /// A send it would refuse first gets the answers Linux gives before any
3763    /// permission check, without the WARN: a `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`
3764    /// whose tgid is not the named thread's own process is ESRCH
3765    /// (`do_send_specific`), and an unreadable or malformed `siginfo`
3766    /// (`siginfo`) is EFAULT, E2BIG or, for `pidfd_send_signal`, EINVAL
3767    /// (`reserved_send_siginfo_errno`). Nothing is delivered either way.
3768    pub(crate) async fn refuse_reserved_signal_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
3769        &self,
3770        guest: &mut G,
3771        sysno: Sysno,
3772        signal: i32,
3773        target: ReservedSendTarget,
3774        siginfo: ReservedSendSiginfo,
3775    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
3776        if !guest.config().reserves_in_guest_signal(signal.into()) {
3777            return None;
3778        }
3779        let own = self.detpid;
3780        let caller = guest.thread_state().dettid;
3781        // `query`, the id whose process's live threads decide, `thread`, the
3782        // thread a thread-directed send must reach, and `group`, the thread
3783        // group a `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` names.
3784        let (query, thread, group) = match target {
3785            ReservedSendTarget::Process(pid) => {
3786                if pid <= 0 {
3787                    return None;
3788                }
3789                let named = self.reserved_send_task(guest, pid).await?;
3790                if reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, named, None) {
3791                    return None;
3792                }
3793                (named, None, None)
3794            }
3795            ReservedSendTarget::Thread { tgid, tid } => {
3796                if tid <= 0 || tgid.is_some_and(|tgid| tgid <= 0) {
3797                    return None;
3798                }
3799                let thread = self.reserved_send_task(guest, tid).await?;
3800                let group = match tgid {
3801                    Some(tgid) => Some(self.reserved_send_task(guest, tgid).await?),
3802                    None => None,
3803                };
3804                if reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, thread, group) {
3805                    return None;
3806                }
3807                (group.unwrap_or(thread), Some(thread), group)
3808            }
3809            ReservedSendTarget::Pidfd { named, thread } => {
3810                let named = named?;
3811                if reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, named, None) {
3812                    return None;
3813                }
3814                (named, thread.then_some(named), None)
3815            }
3816        };
3817        let (targets, _) = resolve_kill_targets_and_exit(guest, query).await;
3818        if !reserved_send_refuses(caller, thread, &targets) {
3819            return None;
3820        }
3821        let leader = registered_process_of(guest, targets[0]).await;
3822        if !reserved_send_names_its_group(group, leader) {
3823            return None;
3824        }
3825        if let Some(errno) = self.reserved_send_siginfo_errno(guest, signal, siginfo) {
3826            return Some(Err(errno.into()));
3827        }
3828        let process = leader.unwrap_or(query);
3829        tracing::warn!(
3830            "{}",
3831            crate::refused_signal_controls::reserved_signal_send_refused_warning(
3832                sysno.name(),
3833                signal,
3834                caller.as_raw(),
3835                process.as_raw(),
3836            )
3837        );
3838        Some(Err(Errno::EPERM.into()))
3839    }
3840
3841    /// A5 step 3c.1c, design D48 with IR-4(b): under the in-guest delivery
3842    /// gate, refuse with EPERM and a WARN a send of a stop signal to any
3843    /// in-guest process, the caller's own included: SIGSTOP always, and
3844    /// SIGTSTP, SIGTTIN or SIGTTOU while the target's action is `SIG_DFL`. The
3845    /// in-guest backend cannot stop a guest process on the schedule, and a
3846    /// stopped one would hold the run (16.1, D48(a)). SIGCONT is admitted: no
3847    /// guest process is stopped, so it acts as a default-ignored signal (D48(i)).
3848    ///
3849    /// "The target's action is `SIG_DFL`" is read from the named live
3850    /// thread's `/proc/<tid>/status` (`SigIgn` and `SigCgt`), in the sender's
3851    /// turn: under premise P the target is inside its runtime then, and on the
3852    /// in-guest backends a guest thread id names its host task
3853    /// (`procfs_inode::guest_tids_name_host_tasks`). A status that cannot be
3854    /// read refuses the send, fail closed. A target with no live thread in
3855    /// Detcore's model keeps the ordinary path (a zombie takes the signal, 0;
3856    /// a reaped or unknown id is ESRCH), as do a thread outside the group a
3857    /// `tgkill` names, a non-positive id, and group sends, which their handlers
3858    /// refuse first (F15). With the gate off, or another signal, `None`.
3859    ///
3860    /// Only a valid send reaches the refusal: one the kernel would fail
3861    /// before sending anything (its `siginfo`, a forged `si_code`;
3862    /// `refused_send_kernel_errno`) gets that errno, without the WARN.
3863    pub(crate) async fn refuse_stop_signal_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
3864        &self,
3865        guest: &mut G,
3866        sysno: Sysno,
3867        signal: i32,
3868        target: ReservedSendTarget,
3869        siginfo: ReservedSendSiginfo,
3870    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
3871        use crate::refused_signal_controls::StopSendRefusal;
3872        if !guest.config().in_guest_signal_delivery
3873            || !matches!(
3874                signal,
3875                libc::SIGSTOP | libc::SIGTSTP | libc::SIGTTIN | libc::SIGTTOU
3876            )
3877        {
3878            return None;
3879        }
3880        let (query, thread) = match target {
3881            ReservedSendTarget::Process(pid) => {
3882                if pid <= 0 {
3883                    return None;
3884                }
3885                (self.reserved_send_task(guest, pid).await?, None)
3886            }
3887            ReservedSendTarget::Thread { tgid, tid } => {
3888                if tid <= 0 || tgid.is_some_and(|tgid| tgid <= 0) {
3889                    return None;
3890                }
3891                let thread = self.reserved_send_task(guest, tid).await?;
3892                let group = match tgid {
3893                    Some(tgid) => Some(self.reserved_send_task(guest, tgid).await?),
3894                    None => None,
3895                };
3896                (group.unwrap_or(thread), Some(thread))
3897            }
3898            ReservedSendTarget::Pidfd { named, thread } => {
3899                let named = named?;
3900                (named, thread.then_some(named))
3901            }
3902        };
3903        let (targets, _) = resolve_kill_targets_and_exit(guest, query).await;
3904        let receiver = match thread {
3905            Some(thread) if targets.contains(&thread) => thread,
3906            Some(_) => return None,
3907            None => *targets.first()?,
3908        };
3909        let why = if signal == libc::SIGSTOP {
3910            StopSendRefusal::Sigstop
3911        } else {
3912            let task = reverie::Pid::from_raw(receiver.as_raw());
3913            match crate::syscalls::read_thread_signal_state(task, task) {
3914                Ok(state) if (state.ignored | state.caught) & kernel_sigset_bit(signal) != 0 => {
3915                    return None;
3916                }
3917                Ok(_) => StopSendRefusal::DefaultAction,
3918                Err(_) => StopSendRefusal::StatusUnreadable,
3919            }
3920        };
3921        // A thread-directed send names `receiver`; a process-directed one
3922        // names the process `query` is.
3923        let named = thread.unwrap_or(query);
3924        if let Some(errno) = self
3925            .refused_send_kernel_errno(guest, signal, siginfo, named, None)
3926            .await
3927        {
3928            return Some(Err(errno.into()));
3929        }
3930        tracing::warn!(
3931            "{}",
3932            crate::refused_signal_controls::stop_signal_send_refused_warning(
3933                sysno.name(),
3934                signal,
3935                guest.thread_state().dettid.as_raw(),
3936                receiver.as_raw(),
3937                why,
3938            )
3939        );
3940        Some(Err(Errno::EPERM.into()))
3941    }
3942
3943    /// A5 step 3c.1c: Linux's answer, before anything is sent, for a send to
3944    /// the live task `named` (as the scheduler numbers it) that a D44 or D48
3945    /// refusal would otherwise refuse, so that only a valid send reaches a
3946    /// refusal. In the kernel's order (v6.12 kernel/signal.c):
3947    ///
3948    /// 1. the copy of the `siginfo` from the caller's memory, EFAULT or E2BIG,
3949    ///    and for `pidfd_send_signal` EINVAL when its `si_signo` is not the
3950    ///    signal (`reserved_send_siginfo_errno`; `rt_sigqueueinfo`,
3951    ///    :4079-4087, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, :4119-4127, `pidfd_send_signal`,
3952    ///    :3959-3966);
3953    /// 2. a non-positive tgid named by `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`
3954    ///    (`group`), EINVAL (:4038-4039, :4106-4107);
3955    /// 3. a `si_code` only the target thread itself may use (`si_code >= 0`
3956    ///    or `SI_TKILL`) from a caller that is not the task the call names,
3957    ///    EPERM (`do_rt_sigqueueinfo`, :4065-4067; `do_rt_tgsigqueueinfo`,
3958    ///    :4112-4114; `pidfd_send_signal`, :3968-3972);
3959    /// 4. a tgid that is not `named`'s process, ESRCH (`do_send_specific`,
3960    ///    :3994). A tgid Detcore cannot name (a nested caller's, on a backend
3961    ///    that names no nested ids) is not judged here.
3962    ///
3963    /// `None` for a valid send. The signal number is valid: D44 and D48 pass
3964    /// only signals 1 to 64, which `valid_signal` (:836) admits, and the target
3965    /// exists. A siginfo Linux could copy but Detcore cannot read
3966    /// (`refused_send_siginfo`, `Unverifiable`) leaves its later checks
3967    /// unjudged, so the send takes its refusal, fail closed, rather than an
3968    /// errno Linux would not give.
3969    async fn refused_send_kernel_errno<G: Guest<Self>>(
3970        &self,
3971        guest: &mut G,
3972        signal: i32,
3973        siginfo: ReservedSendSiginfo,
3974        named: DetTid,
3975        group: Option<libc::pid_t>,
3976    ) -> Option<Errno> {
3977        let code = match refused_send_siginfo(guest, signal, siginfo) {
3978            RefusedSendSiginfo::Errno(errno) => return Some(errno),
3979            RefusedSendSiginfo::NoCopy => None,
3980            RefusedSendSiginfo::Copied { code } => Some(code),
3981            RefusedSendSiginfo::Unverifiable => {
3982                if group.is_some_and(|tgid| tgid <= 0) {
3983                    return Some(Errno::EINVAL);
3984                }
3985                tracing::info!(
3986                    "[detcore, dtid {}] a send's siginfo lies in memory the caller's own reads \
3987                     cannot reach though Linux could copy it: its si_code and target checks are \
3988                     not judged, and the send takes its refusal",
3989                    guest.thread_state().dettid,
3990                );
3991                return None;
3992            }
3993        };
3994        if group.is_some_and(|tgid| tgid <= 0) {
3995            return Some(Errno::EINVAL);
3996        }
3997        if let Some(code) = code
3998            && (code >= 0 || code == libc::SI_TKILL)
3999            && named != guest.thread_state().dettid
4000        {
4001            return Some(Errno::EPERM);
4002        }
4003        if let Some(tgid) = group
4004            && let Some(process) = self.reserved_send_task(guest, tgid).await
4005        {
4006            let leader = registered_process_of(guest, named).await;
4007            if leader != Some(DetPid::from_raw(process.as_raw())) {
4008                return Some(Errno::ESRCH);
4009            }
4010        }
4011        None
4012    }
4013
4014    /// Linux's answer for the `siginfo` of a send that would otherwise be
4015    /// refused (`refuse_reserved_signal_send`), which it gives before any
4016    /// permission check: the copy from the caller's memory, read here in the
4017    /// caller's own turn and in Linux's order (`siginfo_copy_errno`): the
4018    /// head, then the expansion only for a layout Linux does not know. A
4019    /// range that would pass the end of the address space is EFAULT, never
4020    /// computed. `None` when the copy succeeds or the call carries none.
4021    ///
4022    /// The bytes are read through `Guest::memory()`. On the in-guest backends
4023    /// that is `process_vm_readv` (reverie-inguest `read_guest`,
4024    /// src/guest/host.rs:2463-2476 at a214eb85), which refuses some memory
4025    /// x86's `copy_from_user` can read, such as a write-only mapping: such a
4026    /// siginfo is EFAULT here where Linux would copy it. Detcore's ptrace-side
4027    /// readers (`read_siginfo`, `read_mapped_prefix`,
4028    /// `rt_sigqueueinfo_precheck`) fall back to `/proc/<tid>/mem` for that,
4029    /// which cannot be reused in-guest, where guest thread ids are not host
4030    /// thread ids.
4031    fn reserved_send_siginfo_errno<G: Guest<Self>>(
4032        &self,
4033        guest: &mut G,
4034        signal: i32,
4035        siginfo: ReservedSendSiginfo,
4036    ) -> Option<Errno> {
4037        let (address, pidfd) = match siginfo {
4038            ReservedSendSiginfo::None => return None,
4039            ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(address) => (address, false),
4040            ReservedSendSiginfo::Pidfd(None) => return None,
4041            ReservedSendSiginfo::Pidfd(Some(address)) => (address, true),
4042        };
4043        // Each range must end inside the address space: a pointer within a
4044        // siginfo's length of its top cannot be copied (EFAULT).
4045        let head = address
4046            .checked_add(KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE)
4047            .and_then(|_| AddrMut::<[u8; KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE]>::from_raw(address))
4048            .and_then(|head| guest.memory().read_value(head).ok());
4049        let read_expansion = || {
4050            let start = address.checked_add(KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE)?;
4051            start.checked_add(SI_EXPANSION_SIZE)?;
4052            let expansion = AddrMut::<[u8; SI_EXPANSION_SIZE]>::from_raw(start)?;
4053            guest.memory().read_value(expansion).ok()
4054        };
4055        siginfo_copy_errno(signal, pidfd, head, read_expansion)
4056    }
4057
4058    /// The task a reserved send's `id` names (`refuse_reserved_signal_send`):
4059    /// the id itself for a caller in the container's PID namespace, and for a
4060    /// caller in a nested one the task its namespace's `id` is. `None` when
4061    /// Detcore cannot name one, which leaves the call to its ordinary path.
4062    async fn reserved_send_task<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G, id: i32) -> Option<DetTid> {
4063        if !guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace {
4064            return Some(DetTid::from_raw(id));
4065        }
4066        if guest
4067            .config()
4068            .backend
4069            .requires_thread_directed_process_signals
4070        {
4071            return None;
4072        }
4073        match crate::tool_global::guest_pid(guest, id).await {
4074            GuestPidAnswer::Task { task, .. } => Some(task),
4075            GuestPidAnswer::NoSuchPid | GuestPidAnswer::Untranslatable(_) => None,
4076        }
4077    }
4078
4079    /// Refuse, with EPERM and a WARN, an arming of one of the in-guest
4080    /// runtime's reserved signals (`Config::in_guest_reserved_signals`) as a
4081    /// notification signal by `arming`, whatever the target: its deliveries
4082    /// would reach the runtime's handler for the signal rather than the
4083    /// guest's action (A5 step 3c, design section 16.30). The caller makes
4084    /// Linux's earlier checks of the call first. A disarm (signal 0) is never
4085    /// reserved, and with the set empty nothing changes.
4086    pub(crate) fn refuse_reserved_signal_arming<G: Guest<Self>>(
4087        guest: &mut G,
4088        arming: crate::refused_signal_controls::ReservedArming,
4089        signal: i64,
4090    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
4091        if !guest.config().reserves_in_guest_signal(signal) {
4092            return Ok(());
4093        }
4094        tracing::warn!(
4095            "{}",
4096            crate::refused_signal_controls::reserved_signal_arming_refused_warning(
4097                arming,
4098                signal as i32,
4099                guest.thread_state().dettid.as_raw(),
4100            )
4101        );
4102        Err(Errno::EPERM.into())
4103    }
4104
4105    /// Refuse by name a guest's `SIGCHLD` that carries the scheduler's own-copy
4106    /// mark (`SI_QUEUE` with `OWN_SIGCHLD_MARK`), which a receiver in a nested
4107    /// PID namespace would take for the scheduler's copy
4108    /// (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155>). `None` for any other
4109    /// call; an unreadable siginfo is left to the kernel's EFAULT.
4110    pub(crate) async fn refuse_forged_own_sigchld<G: Guest<Self>>(
4111        &self,
4112        guest: &mut G,
4113        sysno: Sysno,
4114        signal: i32,
4115        info: Option<AddrMut<'_, GuestSiginfo>>,
4116    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
4117        if signal != libc::SIGCHLD {
4118            return None;
4119        }
4120        let bytes = read_siginfo(guest, info?.cast())?;
4121        let (code, _) = crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::siginfo_code_and_pid(&bytes);
4122        let value = i32::from_ne_bytes(bytes[24..28].try_into().expect("four bytes"));
4123        if code != libc::SI_QUEUE || value != crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OWN_SIGCHLD_MARK
4124        {
4125            return None;
4126        }
4127        tracing::error!(
4128            "[detcore, dtid {}] {} sends a SIGCHLD carrying the scheduler's own-copy mark {:#x}, which a nested PID namespace's receiver could not tell from the scheduler's (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155)",
4129            guest.thread_state().dettid,
4130            sysno,
4131            crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OWN_SIGCHLD_MARK,
4132        );
4133        Some(
4134            self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, sysno, Errno::EPERM)
4135                .await,
4136        )
4137    }
4138
4139    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
4140    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#812)
4141    /// Queue a thread-directed signal with an accompanying `siginfo_t`. Like
4142    /// `tgkill`, the target is a specific thread named by stable guest TGID/TID
4143    /// and delivery is scheduler-serialized; the guest-supplied siginfo is
4144    /// deterministic input, so forwarding the kernel call is deterministic.
4145    pub async fn handle_rt_tgsigqueueinfo<G: Guest<Self>>(
4146        &self,
4147        guest: &mut G,
4148        call: syscalls::RtTgsigqueueinfo,
4149    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
4150        if let Some(refused) = self
4151            .refuse_forged_own_sigchld(
4152                guest,
4153                Sysno::rt_tgsigqueueinfo,
4154                call.sig(),
4155                call.siginfo().map(AddrMut::cast),
4156            )
4157            .await
4158        {
4159            return refused;
4160        }
4161        if let Some(refused) = self
4162            .refuse_reserved_signal_send(
4163                guest,
4164                Sysno::rt_tgsigqueueinfo,
4165                call.sig(),
4166                ReservedSendTarget::Thread {
4167                    tgid: Some(call.tgid()),
4168                    tid: call.tid(),
4169                },
4170                ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(call.siginfo().map_or(0, |info| info.as_raw())),
4171            )
4172            .await
4173        {
4174            return refused;
4175        }
4176        // D48 (A5 step 3c.1c): a stop signal under the in-guest delivery
4177        // gate, the caller's own process included.
4178        if let Some(refused) = self
4179            .refuse_stop_signal_send(
4180                guest,
4181                Sysno::rt_tgsigqueueinfo,
4182                call.sig(),
4183                ReservedSendTarget::Thread {
4184                    tgid: Some(call.tgid()),
4185                    tid: call.tid(),
4186                },
4187                ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(call.siginfo().map_or(0, |info| info.as_raw())),
4188            )
4189            .await
4190        {
4191            return refused;
4192        }
4193        self.record_orphaned_group_signals(guest, call.sig()).await;
4194        // A nested caller names its own namespace's tid
4195        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155); a tid no task of
4196        // that namespace has goes to the kernel, which checks the siginfo
4197        // first and then answers ESRCH.
4198        let nested = guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace;
4199        let tid = if nested && call.tgid() > 0 && call.tid() > 0 {
4200            match self
4201                .name_guest_pid(guest, Sysno::rt_tgsigqueueinfo, call.tid())
4202                .await
4203            {
4204                NamedGuestPid::Task {
4205                    task,
4206                    own_init_caught,
4207                } => {
4208                    if Self::own_namespace_init_drops(own_init_caught, call.sig()) {
4209                        return self
4210                            .send_to_own_init(guest, call, task, QueueId::Thread(task), call.sig())
4211                            .await;
4212                    }
4213                    task.as_raw()
4214                }
4215                NamedGuestPid::Answer(Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ESRCH))) => {
4216                    return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4217                }
4218                NamedGuestPid::Answer(answer) => return answer,
4219            }
4220        } else {
4221            call.tid()
4222        };
4223        if call.sig() == libc::SIGALRM {
4224            refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::SendTo(DetTid::from_raw(tid))).await?;
4225        }
4226        if !nested
4227            && self
4228                .drop_signal_to_hermit(
4229                    guest,
4230                    Sysno::rt_tgsigqueueinfo,
4231                    Some(call.tgid()),
4232                    tid,
4233                    call.sig(),
4234                )
4235                .await
4236        {
4237            return Ok(0);
4238        }
4239        // A SIGKILL to an exited leader changes nothing, as in `tgkill`
4240        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4445).
4241        let exited_leader = call.sig() == libc::SIGKILL
4242            && guest.config().sequentialize_threads
4243            && tid > 0
4244            && sigkill_names_an_exited_leader(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await;
4245        // Any other SIGKILL to a thread of a valid pair in another process
4246        // ends that thread's whole process, and is reserved and retired at
4247        // this turn as `tgkill`'s is
4248        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3994); before, its victims
4249        // were retired at their host-timed deaths. A send the kernel then
4250        // refuses (EFAULT, EPERM, E2BIG: its siginfo checks are subtle)
4251        // releases the reservation, because `send_reserved_sigkill` commits a
4252        // send that excludes the sender with its outcome. A send to the
4253        // sender's own process is not reserved: it would take the sender's
4254        // Exit grant before the call runs, and so must know beforehand that
4255        // the kernel accepts the siginfo (Codex review); it keeps its
4256        // host-timed retirement. Nor is a nested caller's send, whose tgid
4257        // names its own namespace.
4258        if call.sig() == libc::SIGKILL
4259            && !nested
4260            && !exited_leader
4261            && guest.config().sequentialize_threads
4262            && call.tgid() > 0
4263            && tid > 0
4264            && guest
4265                .thread_state()
4266                .detpid
4267                .is_none_or(|own| own.as_raw() != call.tgid())
4268            && registered_process_of(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await
4269                == Some(DetPid::from_raw(call.tgid()))
4270            && let Some(reservation) = reserve_sigkill(guest, DetTid::from_raw(tid)).await
4271        {
4272            if reservation.includes_sender {
4273                // Only when the sender's own process was not known above: give
4274                // the reservation back and take the unreserved path.
4275                commit_sigkill(guest, reservation.token, false).await;
4276            } else {
4277                return self.send_reserved_sigkill(guest, reservation, call).await;
4278            }
4279        }
4280        let value = self
4281            .send_guest_signal(
4282                guest,
4283                call,
4284                DetTid::from_raw(call.tid()),
4285                call.sig(),
4286                None,
4287                true,
4288                DetTid::from_raw(tid),
4289            )
4290            .await?;
4291        if exited_leader {
4292            return Ok(value);
4293        }
4294        // Thread-directed like `tgkill`, so it carries the same wakeup
4295        // obligation. `sigqueue`/`pthread_sigqueue` reach a parked sibling
4296        // through here.
4297        self.notify_cross_task_signal(
4298            guest,
4299            DetTid::from_raw(tid),
4300            call.sig(),
4301            None,
4302            crate::tool_global::SignalScope::Thread,
4303        )
4304        .await;
4305        Ok(value)
4306    }
4307
4308    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
4309    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#812)
4310    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1058): Review queued process-signal preservation.
4311    /// Queue a process-directed signal with an accompanying `siginfo_t`. Mirrors `handle_kill`:
4312    /// preserve process-directed delivery when the backend accepts guest PIDs, otherwise route an
4313    /// unambiguous positive-PID target to its sole live thread via `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`. Ambiguous
4314    /// multithreaded process-directed delivery is refused until Detcore models eligible masks.
4315    pub async fn handle_rt_sigqueueinfo<G: Guest<Self>>(
4316        &self,
4317        guest: &mut G,
4318        call: syscalls::RtSigqueueinfo,
4319    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
4320        if let Some(refused) = self
4321            .refuse_forged_own_sigchld(
4322                guest,
4323                Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo,
4324                call.sig(),
4325                call.siginfo().map(AddrMut::cast),
4326            )
4327            .await
4328        {
4329            return refused;
4330        }
4331        // Linux names no group here: a non-positive pid is ESRCH.
4332        if let Some(refused) = self
4333            .refuse_reserved_signal_send(
4334                guest,
4335                Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo,
4336                call.sig(),
4337                ReservedSendTarget::Process(call.tgid()),
4338                ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(call.siginfo().map_or(0, |info| info.as_raw())),
4339            )
4340            .await
4341        {
4342            return refused;
4343        }
4344        // D48 (A5 step 3c.1c): a stop signal under the in-guest delivery
4345        // gate, the caller's own process included.
4346        if let Some(refused) = self
4347            .refuse_stop_signal_send(
4348                guest,
4349                Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo,
4350                call.sig(),
4351                ReservedSendTarget::Process(call.tgid()),
4352                ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(call.siginfo().map_or(0, |info| info.as_raw())),
4353            )
4354            .await
4355        {
4356            return refused;
4357        }
4358        self.record_orphaned_group_signals(guest, call.sig()).await;
4359        let nested = guest.thread_state().nested_pid_namespace;
4360        if !guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
4361            if !nested
4362                && self
4363                    .drop_signal_to_hermit(
4364                        guest,
4365                        Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo,
4366                        None,
4367                        call.tgid(),
4368                        call.sig(),
4369                    )
4370                    .await
4371            {
4372                return Ok(0);
4373            }
4374            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4375        }
4376
4377        // A nested caller names its own namespace's pid
4378        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155); a pid no task of
4379        // that namespace has goes to the kernel, which checks the siginfo
4380        // first and then answers ESRCH.
4381        let tgid = if nested && call.tgid() > 0 {
4382            match self
4383                .name_guest_pid(guest, Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo, call.tgid())
4384                .await
4385            {
4386                NamedGuestPid::Task {
4387                    task,
4388                    own_init_caught,
4389                } => {
4390                    if Self::own_namespace_init_drops(own_init_caught, call.sig()) {
4391                        return self
4392                            .send_to_own_init(guest, call, task, QueueId::Process(task), call.sig())
4393                            .await;
4394                    }
4395                    task.as_raw()
4396                }
4397                NamedGuestPid::Answer(Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ESRCH))) => {
4398                    return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4399                }
4400                NamedGuestPid::Answer(answer) => return answer,
4401            }
4402        } else {
4403            call.tgid()
4404        };
4405        if tgid <= 0 {
4406            // POSIX.1b has no process groups: Linux's `do_rt_sigqueueinfo` looks
4407            // the pid up with `find_vpid`, which finds no process for 0 or a
4408            // negative pid, so the call fails (EFAULT for an unreadable
4409            // `siginfo_t`, EPERM for a forged `si_code`, E2BIG for an unknown
4410            // `si_code` with nonzero expansion bytes, otherwise ESRCH) and
4411            // sends nothing. The kernel's own answer is therefore deterministic
4412            // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4046).
4413            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4414        }
4415        if call.sig() == 0 {
4416            // An existence check answers from the same exit model as a signal
4417            // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4124): a process
4418            // Detcore saw exit and that is gone is ESRCH after the siginfo
4419            // checks, whatever the host has reaped by now.
4420            let (targets, zombie) =
4421                resolve_kill_targets_and_exit(guest, DetPid::from_raw(tgid)).await;
4422            if targets.is_empty() && zombie == Some(false) {
4423                return Ok(self
4424                    .record_or_replay(guest, call.with_tgid(NO_SUCH_PID))
4425                    .await?);
4426            }
4427            return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4428        }
4429        if call.sig() == libc::SIGALRM {
4430            refuse_sigalrm(guest, SigalrmControl::SendTo(DetTid::from_raw(tgid))).await?;
4431        }
4432        if self
4433            .drop_signal_to_hermit(guest, Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo, None, tgid, call.sig())
4434            .await
4435        {
4436            return Ok(0);
4437        }
4438        // A call the kernel fails without sending, after a complete copy of
4439        // its `siginfo_t` (EPERM for a `si_code` only the target thread itself
4440        // may use), goes to it unchanged; a copy that certainly faults is
4441        // answered EFAULT here; and a `siginfo_t` Hermit cannot read whole is
4442        // refused by name (`rt_sigqueueinfo_precheck`).
4443        if guest.config().backend.host_task_reads_reach_guest_threads {
4444            match self.rt_sigqueueinfo_precheck(guest, &call) {
4445                RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::KernelFails => {
4446                    return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4447                }
4448                RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Efault => return Err(Errno::EFAULT.into()),
4449                RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Unreadable => {
4450                    tracing::error!(
4451                        "[detcore, dtid {}] rt_sigqueueinfo with a siginfo_t at {:#x} is not \
4452                         supported: Hermit cannot read all 128 bytes of it to decide the call \
4453                         (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034)",
4454                        guest.thread_state().dettid,
4455                        call.siginfo().map_or(0, |info| info.as_raw()),
4456                    );
4457                    return self
4458                        .refuse_unserviceable_operation(
4459                            guest,
4460                            Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo,
4461                            Errno::ENOSYS,
4462                        )
4463                        .await;
4464                }
4465                RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck::Proceeds => {}
4466            }
4467        }
4468        // As for kill, a live non-leader thread's id names its process
4469        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4094).
4470        let process = signal_target_process(guest, DetPid::from_raw(tgid))
4471            .await
4472            .map_or(tgid, |process| process.as_raw());
4473        match self
4474            .own_process_signal(guest, Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo, tgid, process, call.sig())
4475            .await?
4476        {
4477            OwnProcessSend::Admitted => {
4478                return self
4479                    .forward_own_process_signal(guest, call.sig(), call, DetTid::from_raw(tgid))
4480                    .await;
4481            }
4482            OwnProcessSend::Refused(answer) => return answer,
4483            OwnProcessSend::NotOwn => {}
4484        }
4485        let named = DetTid::from_raw(tgid);
4486        let target = match self
4487            .process_signal_target(
4488                guest,
4489                Sysno::rt_sigqueueinfo,
4490                DetPid::from_raw(process),
4491                named,
4492                call.sig(),
4493            )
4494            .await
4495        {
4496            // Gone: Linux checks the siginfo before it looks the process up
4497            // (EFAULT, then E2BIG for an unknown code with padding, then EPERM
4498            // for a forged code), and only then answers ESRCH. Let the kernel
4499            // make those checks against a pid no task can have, so its answer
4500            // cannot depend on whether the host has reaped the real one yet
4501            // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4124).
4502            Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ESRCH)) => {
4503                return Ok(self
4504                    .record_or_replay(guest, call.with_tgid(NO_SUCH_PID))
4505                    .await?);
4506            }
4507            target => target?,
4508        };
4509        let tid = match target {
4510            ProcessSignalTarget::Thread(tid) => tid,
4511            ProcessSignalTarget::SharedQueue => {
4512                return self
4513                    .send_guest_signal(
4514                        guest,
4515                        call,
4516                        DetTid::from_raw(process),
4517                        call.sig(),
4518                        Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
4519                        false,
4520                        named,
4521                    )
4522                    .await;
4523            }
4524            ProcessSignalTarget::Zombie => {
4525                // A zombie: its parent can still wait for it, so Linux still
4526                // finds it. Unlike kill, the kernel checks the siginfo first
4527                // (EFAULT, EPERM for a forged si_code, E2BIG), so let it
4528                // answer: it validates, finds the zombie, and returns 0. A
4529                // backend that must name a receiving thread has none to name,
4530                // and keeps ESRCH
4531                // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4091).
4532                if guest
4533                    .config()
4534                    .backend
4535                    .requires_thread_directed_process_signals
4536                {
4537                    return Err(Errno::ESRCH.into());
4538                }
4539                return Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?);
4540            }
4541        };
4542        let value = if !guest
4543            .config()
4544            .backend
4545            .requires_thread_directed_process_signals
4546        {
4547            self.send_guest_signal(
4548                guest,
4549                call,
4550                tid,
4551                call.sig(),
4552                Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
4553                false,
4554                DetTid::from_raw(tgid),
4555            )
4556            .await?
4557        } else {
4558            let targeted = syscalls::RtTgsigqueueinfo::new()
4559                .with_tgid(process)
4560                .with_tid(tid.as_raw())
4561                .with_sig(call.sig())
4562                .with_siginfo(call.siginfo());
4563            self.send_guest_signal(
4564                guest,
4565                targeted,
4566                tid,
4567                call.sig(),
4568                Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
4569                false,
4570                DetTid::from_raw(tgid),
4571            )
4572            .await?
4573        };
4574        self.notify_cross_task_signal(
4575            guest,
4576            tid,
4577            call.sig(),
4578            Some(DetPid::from_raw(process)),
4579            crate::tool_global::SignalScope::Process,
4580        )
4581        .await;
4582        Ok(value)
4583    }
4584
4585    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
4586    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
4587    /// Read the kernel pending-signal mask after Detcore has serialized all signal
4588    /// generation and delivery events that can change it.
4589    pub async fn handle_rt_sigpending<G: Guest<Self>>(
4590        &self,
4591        guest: &mut G,
4592        call: syscalls::RtSigpending,
4593    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
4594        // Signal phase 1 refuses rt_sigpending in a process that handles
4595        // SIGALRM (`sigalrm_phase1`), so the ledger's entry never needs to be
4596        // shown here. The pending set would show a real-time timer expiration
4597        // that every thread blocked when it expired, which the kernel never
4598        // queued (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4089).
4599        let pending = pending_real_time_among(guest, REAL_TIME_SIGNALS).await;
4600        act_on_pending_real_time(guest, pending, REAL_TIME_SIGNALS, 0).await;
4601        Ok(self.record_or_replay(guest, call).await?)
4602    }
4603
4604    /// A delivered signal may run a handler, whose return restores, through an
4605    /// `rt_sigreturn` that ptrace does not stop at, a mask Detcore cannot read
4606    /// here: the thread's own mask, or for a handler that interrupts a call
4607    /// with a temporary mask, the mask saved before the call. So every
4608    /// delivery records every real-time signal as possibly unblocked by a
4609    /// handler's return (`Scheduler::handler_restore_unblocked`), and a
4610    /// real-time timer expiration of the process is never kept pending after
4611    /// it (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4089). Only where real-time
4612    /// expirations are kept pending at all (`models_signal_targets`), and once
4613    /// per thread.
4614    pub(crate) async fn record_handler_restore_mask<G: Guest<Self>>(
4615        &self,
4616        guest: &mut G,
4617        signal: SigWrapper,
4618    ) {
4619        if !self.cfg.models_signal_targets()
4620            || guest.thread_state().handler_restore_rt_reported == REAL_TIME_SIGNALS
4621        {
4622            return;
4623        }
4624        // Only a caught signal runs a handler that returns. The dispositions
4625        // are the process's, so they say that whatever mask the delivery
4626        // interrupted; a signal acted on by default (the teardown's own
4627        // termination signals among them) records nothing.
4628        let caught =
4629            match crate::syscalls::signal_shadow::copied_dispositions_for(guest, signal.raw()) {
4630                Some(copied) => Ok(copied.caught),
4631                None => read_thread_wait_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())
4632                    .map(|state| state.caught),
4633            };
4634        if let Ok(caught) = caught
4635            && caught & kernel_sigset_bit(signal.raw()) == 0
4636        {
4637            return;
4638        }
4639        let Some(detpid) = guest.thread_state().detpid else {
4640            return;
4641        };
4642        crate::tool_global::record_handler_restore_mask(guest, detpid, REAL_TIME_SIGNALS).await;
4643        guest.thread_state_mut().handler_restore_rt_reported = REAL_TIME_SIGNALS;
4644    }
4645
4646    /// rt_sigreturn: the mask it restores, from the frame's `uc_sigmask`, may
4647    /// unblock a pending real-time timer expiration that every thread blocked
4648    /// when it expired, which the kernel then delivers as the call returns
4649    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4089). The call itself
4650    /// passes through.
4651    pub(crate) async fn observe_sigreturn_mask<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G) {
4652        let pending = pending_real_time_among(guest, REAL_TIME_SIGNALS).await;
4653        if pending == 0 {
4654            return;
4655        }
4656        // `rt_sigreturn` restores the `uc_sigmask` of the frame at `sp`.
4657        let sp = crate::arch::stack_pointer(&guest.regs().await) as usize;
4658        let restored = Addr::<u64>::from_raw(sp.wrapping_add(UC_SIGMASK_OFFSET))
4659            .and_then(|addr| guest.memory().read_value(addr).ok());
4660        let (observable, discarded) = match (
4661            restored,
4662            read_thread_wait_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid()),
4663        ) {
4664            (Some(mask), Ok(state)) => unblocked_by(&state, mask),
4665            // A frame the kernel cannot read either kills the guest with
4666            // SIGSEGV, which delivers nothing pending.
4667            (None, _) => (0, 0),
4668            // A state that cannot be read could hide any delivery.
4669            (Some(_), Err(_)) => (REAL_TIME_SIGNALS, 0),
4670        };
4671        act_on_pending_real_time(guest, pending, observable, discarded).await;
4672    }
4673}
4674
4675/// What [`Detcore::rt_sigprocmask_inner`] knows of the set the kernel used.
4676enum ValidatedSet {
4677    /// The kernel read and accepted this set before Detcore injected the call.
4678    Validated(KernelSigset),
4679    /// Detcore injected nothing: its own read of the set failed first.
4680    NotRun,
4681    /// The call ran as the guest made it; only the kernel read its set.
4682    NotValidated,
4683}
4684
4685/// The offset from the stack pointer at `rt_sigreturn` of the `uc_sigmask` it
4686/// restores.
4687pub(super) const UC_SIGMASK_OFFSET: usize = crate::arch::RT_SIGRETURN_SIGMASK_OFFSET;
4688
4689fn should_notify_cross_task_signal(sender: DetTid, target: DetTid, raw_signal: i32) -> bool {
4690    raw_signal != 0 && target != sender
4691}
4692
4693/// Which check [`sleep_signal`] makes for an emulated `pause` or `nanosleep`.
4694#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
4695pub(crate) enum SleepCheck {
4696    /// Before the sleep parks: whether a signal already pending for the thread
4697    /// ends it at once.
4698    BeforePark,
4699    /// After the scheduler woke the sleep for these signals.
4700    AfterWake(Option<Vec<SigWrapper>>),
4701}
4702
4703/// What ends an emulated `pause` or `nanosleep`, per [`sleep_signal`].
4704#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
4705pub(crate) enum SleepSignal {
4706    /// A signal pending for this thread, unblocked and caught or acted on by
4707    /// default: the kernel delivers it as the call returns. `pause` returns
4708    /// `ERESTARTNOHAND` for it and `nanosleep` `EINTR`.
4709    Pending,
4710    /// The scheduler woke the sleep for a held signal of its own that ends it
4711    /// (`held_wake_ends_sleep`), or this backend does not report the guest's
4712    /// signal state: the call returns `EINTR`, as every wake did before.
4713    Held,
4714    /// Nothing ends the sleep, and it waits (again) with its original deadline.
4715    None,
4716    /// The scheduler's `SIGCHLD`, in a process whose send window is unusable,
4717    /// that this thread claimed (`claim_pending_sigchld`): it is held for
4718    /// delivery to this thread as it resumes, so a signal is always pending
4719    /// then. Each call returns its restart code, as Linux's does: `EINTR` when
4720    /// a handler runs, whatever its `SA_RESTART`, and a restart when none does
4721    /// (the child-exit ledger drops a duplicate copy). `pause` and an absolute
4722    /// `clock_nanosleep` return `ERESTARTNOHAND`, and the kernel re-executes
4723    /// them. A relative `nanosleep` returns `ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` and keeps
4724    /// its deadline for the kernel's `restart_syscall`, which only a restart
4725    /// makes (`helpers::RestartCall::Nanosleep`).
4726    Claimed,
4727}
4728
4729/// Decides, in this thread's own turn, whether a signal ends an emulated
4730/// `pause` or `nanosleep` (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3982>).
4731///
4732/// Only on a backend whose kernel reports the guest's signal state; on any
4733/// other, a sleep never checks before it parks, and a wake always ends it
4734/// (`Held`). A signal ends the sleep when it is pending for this thread,
4735/// unblocked, and caught or acted on by default
4736/// (`KernelSignalState::pending_interrupting`). Before the sleep parks, a stop
4737/// signal does so only when `stops_end_it`, which `nanosleep` leaves unset
4738/// because Detcore does not keep its deadline for the kernel's restart after a
4739/// stop, so the stop takes effect when the sleep ends, as before. After a wake,
4740/// which only the scheduler's own sends (a timer, a child-exit `SIGCHLD`) can
4741/// commit for a stop on a `nanosleep`, a pending stop ends it, as every wake
4742/// did before, so that the stop takes effect promptly.
4743///
4744/// `SIGCHLD` and the signals a host-timed source armed by a guest can post
4745/// (`host_timed_signals`) are held: the kernel can post them at a moment set by
4746/// host timing, and `/proc` cannot tell that copy from one a guest sent. They
4747/// never end a sleep before it parks, nor does the scheduler record a guest's
4748/// cross-task send of one for a sleep. A wake the scheduler committed for its
4749/// own child-exit `SIGCHLD` ends the sleep with `Held` only while the copy it
4750/// sent is still pending in the process's shared queue
4751/// (`ChildExitSigchldControl::PlacedPending`, which `/proc` cannot answer),
4752/// `/proc` shows it pending, and it is unblocked and caught or default-fatal
4753/// (`held_wake_ends_sleep`); a host-timed held signal, from the mask and
4754/// dispositions alone. Like the precise futex
4755/// wait's check, this reads the pending set before the sleep parks as well as
4756/// after a wake: a signal sent while the thread was stopped short of filing
4757/// its request, as when its timeslice ended in the syscall's prehook, is
4758/// pending and was never recorded for its sleep. After a wake whose
4759/// non-held signals are no longer pending, a `SIGCONT` having cancelled a stop
4760/// or `sigaction(SIG_IGN)` having flushed it, a Linux sleep would still be
4761/// asleep, so this returns `None`.
4762///
4763/// A state that cannot be read is handled as a wait's first read is
4764/// (`read_wait_signal_state`, here `read_thread_wait_signal_state`, since
4765/// Reverie can report the creator's pid for a process a raw `clone` made): a
4766/// thread that no longer exists ends the call
4767/// with `ERESTARTNOINTR`, which nothing observes, and otherwise the run ends
4768/// with a diagnostic.
4769pub(crate) async fn sleep_signal<G, T>(
4770    guest: &mut G,
4771    check: SleepCheck,
4772    stops_end_it: bool,
4773) -> Result<SleepSignal, Error>
4774where
4775    G: Guest<Detcore<T>>,
4776    T: RecordOrReplay,
4777{
4778    // D50 reader 8 under the in-guest delivery gate (A5 step 3c.1c, design
4779    // 16.11): before the park and after a wake alike, the sleep ends exactly
4780    // when the design's deliverable set under the thread's published mask is
4781    // not empty, read here in its own turn (`gated_wait_ends`, which records
4782    // the end for the restart checkpoint, D40). Nothing is held: on in-guest
4783    // a child's exit is published inside the every-turn hold, and host-timed
4784    // producers are refused unless ignored (step 3c.0b). A default stop is
4785    // never deliverable (stop sends are refused under the gate, D48), so
4786    // `stops_end_it` changes nothing here.
4787    //
4788    // Signal phase 1's SIGALRM is virtual: the scheduler's ledger wakes a
4789    // `pause` for it (`Scheduler::sigalrm_wake`) with no physical copy
4790    // pending, so such a wake counts as a caught SIGALRM pending on the
4791    // process's shared queue, where Linux's timer would queue it, and takes
4792    // its place in Linux's dequeue order among the deliverable signals
4793    // (`wait_signal_end::classify`): a deliverable default-fatal signal that
4794    // does not dump core still ends the process, and so does a core-dumping
4795    // one Linux would dequeue first; one behind the SIGALRM ends it unless
4796    // the guest's SIGALRM handler's mask holds it, which the restart
4797    // checkpoint reads (`WaitSignalEnd::BehindCaught`). IR-12: the `pause`
4798    // returns `ERESTARTNOHAND`, which the restart checkpoint turns into
4799    // `EINTR`. Phase 1 refuses every other sleep in a process that handles
4800    // SIGALRM (F8).
4801    if guest.config().in_guest_signal_delivery {
4802        let _ = stops_end_it;
4803        let phase1_wake = matches!(&check, SleepCheck::AfterWake(Some(woken))
4804            if woken.iter().any(|signal| signal.raw() == libc::SIGALRM))
4805            && guest.config().backend.virtualizes_guest_sigalrm
4806            && guest.thread_state().sigalrm_handled;
4807        let virtual_caught = if phase1_wake {
4808            kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGALRM)
4809        } else {
4810            0
4811        };
4812        let end =
4813            crate::syscalls::helpers::gated_wait_end_with_virtual(guest, None, virtual_caught)?;
4814        let Some(end) = end else {
4815            return Ok(SleepSignal::None);
4816        };
4817        guest.thread_state_mut().wait_signal_end = Some(end);
4818        return Ok(SleepSignal::Pending);
4819    }
4820    if !guest
4821        .config()
4822        .backend
4823        .supports_blocked_wait_signal_interruption
4824    {
4825        return Ok(match check {
4826            SleepCheck::BeforePark => SleepSignal::None,
4827            SleepCheck::AfterWake(_) => SleepSignal::Held,
4828        });
4829    }
4830    let host_timed = host_timed_signals(guest).await;
4831    let held = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD) | host_timed;
4832    let before_park = check == SleepCheck::BeforePark;
4833    // `held_woken`: the held signals the scheduler woke this sleep for.
4834    // `unknown_held_wake`: a wake that names no signal.
4835    let (deciding, held_woken, unknown_held_wake) = match check {
4836        SleepCheck::BeforePark => (!held, 0, false),
4837        SleepCheck::AfterWake(None) => (0, 0, true),
4838        SleepCheck::AfterWake(Some(woken)) => {
4839            let woken = woken
4840                .iter()
4841                .fold(0, |set, signal| set | kernel_sigset_bit(signal.raw()));
4842            (woken & !held, woken & held, false)
4843        }
4844    };
4845    if deciding != 0 || held_woken != 0 {
4846        let state = read_thread_wait_signal_state(guest.pid(), guest.tid())?;
4847        let mut ending = state.pending_interrupting(state.blocked, false) & deciding;
4848        if !stops_end_it && before_park {
4849            ending &= !(state.default_job_control_stops() | kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGSTOP));
4850        }
4851        // A copy Linux discarded when it was sent ends nothing, except a
4852        // signal the scheduler committed at the wake, which is kept
4853        // (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293>).
4854        let committed = if before_park { 0 } else { deciding };
4855        let ending =
4856            crate::syscalls::threads::without_discarded(guest, &state, ending, committed).await;
4857        if ending != 0 {
4858            return Ok(SleepSignal::Pending);
4859        }
4860        // A committed `SIGCHLD` counts only while the schedule says this thread
4861        // will take it (`PlacedPending`): a guest's thread-directed copy in its
4862        // private queue, or the scheduler's or a guest's shared copy still in
4863        // the window. `/proc` cannot tell either from a kernel copy that host
4864        // timing posted, so its bits only prompt the question: a woken sleep
4865        // asks for the `SIGCHLD` it was woken for, and any sleep asks when its
4866        // private queue holds one, which a guest sent before it parked
4867        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4132).
4868        let sigchld = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD) & !host_timed;
4869        let committed = if asks_committed_sigchld(held_woken, &state, sigchld)
4870            && matches!(
4871                crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
4872                    guest,
4873                    crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl::PlacedPending,
4874                )
4875                .await,
4876                crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Placed(true)
4877            ) {
4878            // A committed copy shows in `/proc`: an invariant, not a second
4879            // condition.
4880            if state.pending & sigchld == 0 {
4881                tracing::error!(
4882                    "[dtid {}] a committed SIGCHLD is pending by the schedule but not in /proc",
4883                    guest.thread_state().dettid
4884                );
4885                debug_assert!(false, "a committed SIGCHLD is not pending in /proc");
4886            }
4887            sigchld
4888        } else {
4889            0
4890        };
4891        if let Some(verdict) = committed_sigchld_verdict(&state, held_woken, committed) {
4892            return Ok(verdict);
4893        }
4894        // Where the process does not use the send window, a thread outside the
4895        // schedule could take the scheduler's copy at a moment host timing
4896        // chooses, so the window records none, and `PlacedPending` says no
4897        // whether or not that thread took it. Parked again, the sleep ran to its
4898        // deadline with the copy pending and the handler ran as it returned, an
4899        // outcome Linux never gives (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4229).
4900        // So this thread claims the copy itself, in its own turn
4901        // (`claim_pending_sigchld`), while SIGCHLD is the signal its kernel
4902        // dequeues next: held for delivery to it, where no other thread can
4903        // take it, so the handler runs as the sleep returns (`Claimed`). If the
4904        // outside thread took it first, the claim finds none and the sleep
4905        // parks again, as on Linux, where the woken sleeper finds no signal. Reading the pending bit and then ending the sleep
4906        // left a gap in which the outside thread could take it, and then the
4907        // sleep returned EINTR with no handler
4908        // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4341).
4909        //
4910        // In record and replay too, but only while no other thread of the
4911        // process is outside the schedule in a call that could take the copy
4912        // (`Scheduler::outside_call_may_take_sigchld`, through
4913        // `WindowUnusable`). Then nothing but this claim can take it, so its
4914        // pending bit, and the claim, are the same in a recording and its
4915        // replay (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4343). While such a
4916        // call is out, whether it took the copy is host-timed, and replay
4917        // serves a signalfd read from the log without taking anything, so the
4918        // two could decide differently; the sleep parks again, as before.
4919        if committed == 0
4920            && held_woken & sigchld != 0
4921            && woken_sigchld_pending(&state, sigchld)
4922            && matches!(
4923                crate::tool_global::child_exit_sigchld(
4924                    guest,
4925                    crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl::WindowUnusable,
4926                )
4927                .await,
4928                crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Placed(true)
4929            )
4930            && state.next_dequeued(state.blocked).map(|(signal, _)| signal) == Some(libc::SIGCHLD)
4931            && {
4932                send_claim_fault_signal(guest, ClaimFault::Usr1BeforeClaim);
4933                let claimed = claim_pending_sigchld(guest).await?;
4934                if claimed {
4935                    send_claim_fault_signal(guest, ClaimFault::Usr1AfterClaim);
4936                }
4937                claimed
4938            }
4939        {
4940            // Its delivery stop places it, so it is not deferred behind a
