4941            // runnable sibling (`ThreadState::claimed_sigchld`).
4942            guest.thread_state_mut().claimed_sigchld = true;
4943            return Ok(SleepSignal::Claimed);
4944        }
4945        let held_woken = held_woken & !sigchld;
4946        if held_wake_ends_sleep(&state, held_woken) {
4947            return Ok(SleepSignal::Held);
4948        }
4949        return Ok(SleepSignal::None);
4950    }
4951    Ok(if unknown_held_wake {
4952        SleepSignal::Held
4953    } else {
4954        SleepSignal::None
4955    })
4956}
4957
4958/// Whether this backend's `rt_sigsuspend` returns at once rather than sleeping
4959/// until a signal arrives (`Detcore::handle_rt_sigsuspend`). Reverie names no
4960/// capability for it, so this derives it from the one that implies it: a
4961/// backend that owns guest signal delivery (`provides_process_signal_control`,
4962/// KVM) implements the call itself, and its scheduler-modeled wake does not
4963/// exist, so it returns EINTR for a signal already pending under the temporary
4964/// mask and refuses a wait for one that is not (ENOSYS) rather than parking
4965/// the vCPU outside the schedule. Every other backend runs the kernel's call,
4966/// which sleeps. `rt_sigsuspend_never_sleeps_per_backend` pins the answer for
4967/// every backend Reverie defines; a backend that gains a sleeping
4968/// `rt_sigsuspend` must change it.
4969pub(crate) fn rt_sigsuspend_never_sleeps(backend: &reverie::BackendCapabilities) -> bool {
4970    backend.provides_process_signal_control
4971}
4972
4973/// Whether a `SIGCHLD` this thread claimed (`ThreadState::claimed_sigchld`,
4974/// `claimed`) is still claimed at a syscall entry, or after a `SIGCHLD` the
4975/// ledger dropped: SIGCHLD is still pending in its queue or its process's,
4976/// whatever its mask. The claim may have held another signal instead
4977/// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4346), whose handler can block
4978/// SIGCHLD and make syscalls before its `rt_sigreturn` lets SIGCHLD be
4979/// delivered (the Codex review of the claimed-delivery fix), or a duplicate
4980/// the ledger drops while another copy waits (the Claude review).
4981/// Once SIGCHLD is not pending, its delivery never came to this thread. A
4982/// state that cannot be read (`read_state`, asked only for a claim) forgets
4983/// the claim, so the delivery is deferred as before the claim existed.
4984pub(crate) fn claim_still_pending<E>(
4985    claimed: bool,
4986    read_state: impl FnOnce() -> Result<crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState, E>,
4987) -> bool {
4988    claimed && read_state().is_ok_and(|state| state.pending & kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD) != 0)
4989}
4990
4991/// Whether `sigchld`, the scheduler's copy a sleep was woken for, is still
4992/// pending for it and ends it: in the thread's or its process's queue, and
4993/// unblocked and caught or acted on by default (`sleep_signal`, for a process
4994/// whose send window is unusable).
4995fn woken_sigchld_pending(
4996    state: &crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState,
4997    sigchld: KernelSigset,
4998) -> bool {
4999    state.pending & state.interrupting(state.blocked) & sigchld != 0
5000}
5001
5002/// Whether a sleep's own check (`sleep_signal`) asks the scheduler whether a
5003/// committed `SIGCHLD` is pending for it (`PlacedPending`): it was woken for
5004/// one (`held_woken`), or its private queue holds one. The latter is how a
5005/// guest's thread-directed copy sent before the sleeper filed its request is
5006/// not lost: the sender's turn found no sleep to wake, so only the sleeper's
5007/// check before it parks sees it (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4132).
5008fn asks_committed_sigchld(
5009    held_woken: KernelSigset,
5010    state: &crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState,
5011    sigchld: KernelSigset,
5012) -> bool {
5013    (held_woken | state.thread_pending) & sigchld != 0
5014}
5015
5016/// What a committed `SIGCHLD` (`committed`, the scheduler's answer) makes of
5017/// the sleep: it ends it if unblocked and caught or acted on by default, as
5018/// the held signal the wake named (`Held`) or as one pending for it
5019/// (`Pending`); `None` leaves the decision to the rest of the check.
5020fn committed_sigchld_verdict(
5021    state: &crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState,
5022    held_woken: KernelSigset,
5023    committed: KernelSigset,
5024) -> Option<SleepSignal> {
5025    let verdict = if held_woken & committed != 0 {
5026        SleepSignal::Held
5027    } else {
5028        SleepSignal::Pending
5029    };
5030    (state.interrupting(state.blocked) & committed != 0).then_some(verdict)
5031}
5032
5033/// The size of a `struct signalfd_siginfo`, which has ssi_signo at 0,
5034/// ssi_code at 8, ssi_pid at 12, ssi_uid at 16, ssi_status at 40, ssi_int at
5035/// 44, ssi_utime at 56 and ssi_stime at 64.
5036pub(crate) const SIGNALFD_RECORD: usize = 128;
5037
5038/// The signals signalfd `fd` of process `pid` reads, from its `sigmask:`
5039/// line in `/proc/<pid>/fdinfo/<fd>` (a kernel sigset in hex), or `None`
5040/// when it cannot be read.
5041pub(crate) fn signalfd_mask(pid: i32, fd: i32) -> Option<u64> {
5042    let info = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("/proc/{pid}/fdinfo/{fd}")).ok()?;
5043    info.lines()
5044        .find_map(|line| line.strip_prefix("sigmask:"))
5045        .and_then(|mask| u64::from_str_radix(mask.trim(), 16).ok())
5046}
5047
5048fn signalfd_field(record: &[u8], at: usize) -> u32 {
5049    u32::from_ne_bytes(record[at..at + 4].try_into().expect("four bytes"))
5050}
5051
5052/// For each `signalfd_siginfo` record in `bytes`, the child-exit ledger
5053/// question it raises (`Detcore::reconcile_signalfd_records`): a SIGCHLD with
5054/// a child-exit code is the kernel's copy for that child, a SIGCHLD the
5055/// scheduler sent (`is_own_sigchld_mark`) is its copy, and any other record
5056/// raises none.
5057fn signalfd_record_controls(
5058    bytes: &[u8],
5059    tracer: u32,
5060) -> Vec<Option<crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl>> {
5061    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl;
5062    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::DequeuedCopy;
5063    bytes
5064        .chunks(SIGNALFD_RECORD)
5065        .map(|record| {
5066            if signalfd_field(record, 0) != libc::SIGCHLD as u32 {
5067                return None;
5068            }
5069            let code = signalfd_field(record, 8) as i32;
5070            let pid = signalfd_field(record, 12);
5071            let value = signalfd_field(record, 44) as i32;
5072            let copy = if crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::is_child_exit_code(code) {
5073                DequeuedCopy::Kernel {
5074                    child: DetPid::from_raw(pid as i32),
5075                }
5076            } else if crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::is_own_sigchld_mark(
5077                code, pid as i32, value, tracer,
5078            ) {
5079                DequeuedCopy::Scheduler
5080            } else if code == libc::SI_TKILL {
5081                DequeuedCopy::Thread
5082            } else {
5083                DequeuedCopy::Other
5084            };
5085            // Read, a kept record makes no `InboundSignal` request.
5086            Some(ChildExitSigchldControl::Dequeue {
5087                copy,
5088                admits: false,
5089            })
5090        })
5091        .collect()
5092}
5093
5094/// The pieces of the guest buffers `extents` (address, length) that hold the
5095/// first `len` bytes a vectored read scattered, in order; empty extents are
5096/// skipped.
5097pub(crate) fn signalfd_extent_pieces(
5098    extents: &[(usize, usize)],
5099    len: usize,
5100) -> Vec<(usize, usize)> {
5101    let mut left = len;
5102    let mut pieces = Vec::new();
5103    for (address, length) in extents {
5104        if left == 0 {
5105            break;
5106        }
5107        let take = (*length).min(left);
5108        if take > 0 {
5109            pieces.push((*address, take));
5110            left -= take;
5111        }
5112    }
5113    pieces
5114}
5115
5116/// Signalfd records rewritten by the child-exit ledger's answers.
5117#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
5118struct RewrittenSignalfdRecords {
5119    /// The buffer to write back: the kept records in order, then what the
5120    /// buffer held before the read over the bytes of dropped ones, so no
5121    /// dropped record stays readable.
5122    bytes: Vec<u8>,
5123    /// How many bytes the read returns.
5124    kept: usize,
5125}
5126
5127/// Apply the ledger's `answers`, one per record of `bytes`
5128/// (`signalfd_record_controls`): a claimed duplicate or a duplicate scheduler
5129/// copy is dropped, a copy that stands for an owned child is given that
5130/// child's siginfo with zero CPU times, and every other record is kept as read.
5131/// `Ok(None)` when no record changed. `Err(())` when a record is dropped but
5132/// `before`, the buffer's bytes captured before the read
5133/// (`capture_signalfd_buffer`), does not cover the bytes it leaves: they are
5134/// never invented (the Codex re-check of
5135/// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4351, P2-2).
5136fn apply_signalfd_answers(
5137    bytes: &[u8],
5138    answers: &[Option<crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer>],
5139    before: &[u8],
5140) -> Result<Option<RewrittenSignalfdRecords>, ()> {
5141    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer;
5142    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OwnCopy;
5143    let mut kept = Vec::with_capacity(bytes.len());
5144    let mut changed = false;
5145    for (record, answer) in bytes.chunks(SIGNALFD_RECORD).zip(answers) {
5146        let mut record = record.to_vec();
5147        match answer {
5148            Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(true))
5149            | Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Duplicate)) => {
5150                changed = true;
5151                continue;
5152            }
5153            Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Deliver(own)))
5154            | Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::ClaimedFor(own)) => {
5155                // The record Linux writes for a child exit (SIL_CHLD) starts
5156                // zeroed and fills in only these: nothing of the copy it
5157                // replaces is kept, such as the own copy's SI_QUEUE mark in
5158                // ssi_int and ssi_ptr. CPU times are 0 (natively, host time).
5159                let signo = signalfd_field(&record, 0);
5160                record.fill(0);
5161                record[0..4].copy_from_slice(&signo.to_ne_bytes());
5162                record[8..12].copy_from_slice(&own.code.to_ne_bytes());
5163                record[12..16].copy_from_slice(&(own.pid as u32).to_ne_bytes());
5164                record[16..20].copy_from_slice(&own.uid.to_ne_bytes());
5165                record[40..44].copy_from_slice(&own.status.to_ne_bytes());
5166                changed = true;
5167            }
5168            _ => {}
5169        }
5170        kept.extend_from_slice(&record);
5171    }
5172    if !changed {
5173        return Ok(None);
5174    }
5175    let kept_len = kept.len();
5176    let len = bytes.len() - bytes.len() % SIGNALFD_RECORD;
5177    // The bytes past the kept records are those the caller's buffer held
5178    // before the read: a dropped record must leave the buffer as Linux, which
5179    // never queued it, leaves it (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4293).
5180    if before.len() < len {
5181        return Err(());
5182    }
5183    kept.extend_from_slice(&before[kept_len..len]);
5184    Ok(Some(RewrittenSignalfdRecords {
5185        bytes: kept,
5186        kept: kept_len,
5187    }))
5188}
5189
5190#[cfg(test)]
5191mod signalfd_record_tests {
5192    use super::*;
5193    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldAnswer;
5194    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSigchldControl;
5195    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::ChildExitSiginfo;
5196    use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OwnCopy;
5197
5198    const TRACER: u32 = 4242;
5199
5200    /// `apply_signalfd_answers` with a captured buffer of zeros, so a dropped
5201    /// record's bytes become zeros.
5202    fn apply_without_capture(
5203        bytes: &[u8],
5204        answers: &[Option<ChildExitSigchldAnswer>],
5205    ) -> Option<RewrittenSignalfdRecords> {
5206        apply_signalfd_answers(bytes, answers, &vec![0; bytes.len()]).unwrap()
5207    }
5208
5209    /// A record dropped where the capture does not reach (a buffer past
5210    /// `SIGNALFD_CAPTURED`) is refused, never given invented bytes (the Codex
5211    /// re-check of https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4351, P2-2).
5212    #[test]
5213    fn a_dropped_record_past_the_capture_is_refused() {
5214        let mut bytes = vec![0u8; 2 * SIGNALFD_RECORD];
5215        bytes[0..4].copy_from_slice(&10u32.to_ne_bytes());
5216        bytes[SIGNALFD_RECORD..SIGNALFD_RECORD + 4].copy_from_slice(&12u32.to_ne_bytes());
5217        let before = vec![0xa5u8; SIGNALFD_RECORD];
5218        assert_eq!(
5219            apply_signalfd_answers(
5220                &bytes,
5221                &[Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(true)), None],
5222                &before,
5223            ),
5224            Err(())
5225        );
5226        // Nothing dropped needs no capture.
5227        assert_eq!(apply_signalfd_answers(&bytes, &[None, None], &[]), Ok(None));
5228    }
5229
5230    /// A dropped record's bytes get what the buffer held before the read.
5231    #[test]
5232    fn a_dropped_record_leaves_the_captured_bytes() {
5233        let record = |signo: u32| {
5234            let mut bytes = vec![0u8; SIGNALFD_RECORD];
5235            bytes[0..4].copy_from_slice(&signo.to_ne_bytes());
5236            bytes
5237        };
5238        let bytes = [record(10), record(12)].concat();
5239        let before = vec![0xa5u8; 2 * SIGNALFD_RECORD];
5240        let rewritten = apply_signalfd_answers(
5241            &bytes,
5242            &[Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(true)), None],
5243            &before,
5244        )
5245        .unwrap()
5246        .unwrap();
5247        assert_eq!(rewritten.kept, SIGNALFD_RECORD);
5248        assert_eq!(&rewritten.bytes[..SIGNALFD_RECORD], &record(12)[..]);
5249        assert!(
5250            rewritten.bytes[SIGNALFD_RECORD..]
5251                .iter()
5252                .all(|byte| *byte == 0xa5)
5253        );
5254    }
5255
5256    fn record(signo: i32, code: i32, pid: u32, status: i32) -> Vec<u8> {
5257        let mut record = vec![0u8; SIGNALFD_RECORD];
5258        record[0..4].copy_from_slice(&(signo as u32).to_ne_bytes());
5259        record[8..12].copy_from_slice(&code.to_ne_bytes());
5260        record[12..16].copy_from_slice(&pid.to_ne_bytes());
5261        record[40..44].copy_from_slice(&status.to_ne_bytes());
5262        record[56..72].fill(7);
5263        record
5264    }
5265
5266    /// At a receiver in a nested PID namespace, a record of the scheduler's
5267    /// marked `SI_QUEUE` copy (pid 0, `ssi_int` the mark) is its own copy; an
5268    /// ancestor's `SI_USER` from pid 0 and an unmarked `SI_QUEUE` are another
5269    /// (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4155>).
5270    #[test]
5271    fn a_nested_receivers_marked_record_asks_for_the_own_copy() {
5272        use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::DequeuedCopy;
5273        let marked = |value: i32| {
5274            let mut record = record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_QUEUE, 0, 0);
5275            record[44..48].copy_from_slice(&value.to_ne_bytes());
5276            record
5277        };
5278        let bytes = [
5279            marked(crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OWN_SIGCHLD_MARK),
5280            marked(7),
5281            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_USER, 0, 0),
5282        ]
5283        .concat();
5284        let dequeue = |copy| {
5285            Some(ChildExitSigchldControl::Dequeue {
5286                copy,
5287                admits: false,
5288            })
5289        };
5290        assert_eq!(
5291            signalfd_record_controls(&bytes, TRACER),
5292            vec![
5293                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Scheduler),
5294                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Other),
5295                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Other),
5296            ]
5297        );
5298    }
5299
5300    /// Each `SIGCHLD` record from the shared queue tells the ledger it was
5301    /// taken, as which copy: a kernel child-exit copy names its child, the
5302    /// scheduler's `SI_USER` copy is its own, a guest's `SI_USER` is another,
5303    /// and a `SI_TKILL` copy is thread-directed; any other signal asks nothing.
5304    /// A read makes no `InboundSignal` request (`admits` unset).
5305    #[test]
5306    fn each_record_asks_the_ledger_only_about_a_child_exit_copy() {
5307        use crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::DequeuedCopy;
5308        let bytes = [
5309            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::CLD_KILLED, 31, libc::SIGKILL),
5310            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_USER, TRACER, 0),
5311            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_USER, 77, 0),
5312            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_TKILL, 77, 0),
5313            record(libc::SIGUSR1, libc::SI_USER, TRACER, 0),
5314        ]
5315        .concat();
5316        let dequeue = |copy| {
5317            Some(ChildExitSigchldControl::Dequeue {
5318                copy,
5319                admits: false,
5320            })
5321        };
5322        assert_eq!(
5323            signalfd_record_controls(&bytes, TRACER),
5324            vec![
5325                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Kernel {
5326                    child: DetPid::from_raw(31)
5327                }),
5328                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Scheduler),
5329                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Other),
5330                dequeue(DequeuedCopy::Thread),
5331                None,
5332            ]
5333        );
5334    }
5335
5336    /// A dropped record leaves the read, the records after it move up, and
5337    /// zeros cover its old bytes; a standing copy gets its owned child's
5338    /// siginfo, a cohort's lowest pid included (`ClaimedFor`), with zero CPU
5339    /// times.
5340    #[test]
5341    fn dropped_records_leave_and_standing_ones_take_the_ledgers_siginfo() {
5342        let own = |pid| ChildExitSiginfo {
5343            code: libc::CLD_KILLED,
5344            pid,
5345            uid: 0,
5346            status: libc::SIGKILL,
5347        };
5348        let first = record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::CLD_KILLED, 31, libc::SIGKILL);
5349        let second = record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_USER, TRACER, 0);
5350        let third = record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::CLD_KILLED, 33, libc::SIGKILL);
5351        let bytes = [first, second, third].concat();
5352        let rewritten = apply_without_capture(
5353            &bytes,
5354            &[
5355                Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(true)),
5356                Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Deliver(own(32)))),
5357                Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::ClaimedFor(own(30))),
5358            ],
5359        )
5360        .expect("records changed");
5361        assert_eq!(rewritten.kept, 2 * SIGNALFD_RECORD);
5362        assert_eq!(rewritten.bytes.len(), 3 * SIGNALFD_RECORD);
5363        let expect = |pid: u32| {
5364            let mut wanted = record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::CLD_KILLED, pid, libc::SIGKILL);
5365            wanted[56..72].fill(0);
5366            wanted
5367        };
5368        assert_eq!(&rewritten.bytes[..SIGNALFD_RECORD], &expect(32)[..]);
5369        assert_eq!(
5370            &rewritten.bytes[SIGNALFD_RECORD..2 * SIGNALFD_RECORD],
5371            &expect(30)[..]
5372        );
5373        assert!(
5374            rewritten.bytes[2 * SIGNALFD_RECORD..]
5375                .iter()
5376                .all(|byte| *byte == 0)
5377        );
5378    }
5379
5380    /// The marked own copy a nested receiver reads is rewritten to the
5381    /// record Linux writes for a child exit, which keeps nothing of the
5382    /// copy: `ssi_int` and `ssi_ptr` (bytes 44..56), where the mark was, are
5383    /// 0, like every field a child exit does not fill in (Codex re-check #2
5384    /// of https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4172).
5385    #[test]
5386    fn a_rewritten_own_copy_keeps_none_of_its_marked_record() {
5387        let mut marked = record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_QUEUE, 0, 0);
5388        marked[44..48]
5389            .copy_from_slice(&crate::scheduler::child_exit_sigchld::OWN_SIGCHLD_MARK.to_ne_bytes());
5390        marked[48..56].fill(9);
5391        marked[20..40].fill(5);
5392        marked[72..].fill(6);
5393        let rewritten = apply_without_capture(
5394            &marked,
5395            &[Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Deliver(
5396                ChildExitSiginfo {
5397                    code: libc::CLD_KILLED,
5398                    pid: 3,
5399                    uid: 0,
5400                    status: libc::SIGKILL,
5401                },
5402            )))],
5403        )
5404        .expect("records changed");
5405        let mut wanted = vec![0u8; SIGNALFD_RECORD];
5406        wanted[0..4].copy_from_slice(&(libc::SIGCHLD as u32).to_ne_bytes());
5407        wanted[8..12].copy_from_slice(&libc::CLD_KILLED.to_ne_bytes());
5408        wanted[12..16].copy_from_slice(&3u32.to_ne_bytes());
5409        wanted[40..44].copy_from_slice(&libc::SIGKILL.to_ne_bytes());
5410        assert_eq!(rewritten.bytes, wanted);
5411        assert!(rewritten.bytes[44..56].iter().all(|byte| *byte == 0));
5412    }
5413
5414    /// A vectored read's returned bytes are found across its buffers in
5415    /// order, a record split between two buffers included, and only as far
5416    /// as the read returned.
5417    #[test]
5418    fn a_vectored_reads_records_are_found_across_its_buffers() {
5419        let extents = [(0x1000, 100), (0x2000, 0), (0x3000, 200), (0x4000, 128)];
5420        assert_eq!(
5421            signalfd_extent_pieces(&extents, 256),
5422            vec![(0x1000, 100), (0x3000, 156)]
5423        );
5424        assert_eq!(
5425            signalfd_extent_pieces(&extents, 428),
5426            vec![(0x1000, 100), (0x3000, 200), (0x4000, 128)]
5427        );
5428        assert_eq!(signalfd_extent_pieces(&extents, 0), vec![]);
5429    }
5430
5431    /// A read whose every record is dropped returns nothing and leaves only
5432    /// zeros; a read the ledger leaves alone is not rewritten.
5433    #[test]
5434    fn a_fully_dropped_read_leaves_no_record_and_an_untouched_read_is_not_rewritten() {
5435        let bytes = [
5436            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::CLD_KILLED, 31, libc::SIGKILL),
5437            record(libc::SIGCHLD, libc::SI_USER, TRACER, 0),
5438        ]
5439        .concat();
5440        let rewritten = apply_without_capture(
5441            &bytes,
5442            &[
5443                Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(true)),
5444                Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Duplicate)),
5445            ],
5446        )
5447        .expect("records changed");
5448        assert_eq!(rewritten.kept, 0);
5449        assert!(rewritten.bytes.iter().all(|byte| *byte == 0));
5450        assert_eq!(
5451            apply_without_capture(
5452                &bytes,
5453                &[
5454                    Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::Claimed(false)),
5455                    Some(ChildExitSigchldAnswer::OwnCopy(OwnCopy::Unowned)),
5456                ],
5457            ),
5458            None
5459        );
5460    }
5461}
5462
5463/// The signals a thread may send to its own multi-threaded process with `kill`
5464/// or `rt_sigqueueinfo` (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034): each
5465/// is numbered below SIGCHLD, so a child-exit SIGCHLD, whenever the kernel
5466/// posts it, is dequeued after it; none is in the kernel's synchronous set
5467/// (`SYNCHRONOUS_MASK`); and none is a signal Reverie gives a meaning
5468/// (SIGTRAP, its stop signal, and SIGSTKFLT, its timer marker).
5469const OWN_PROCESS_SIGNALS: [libc::c_int; 7] = [
5470    libc::SIGHUP,
5471    libc::SIGINT,
5472    libc::SIGQUIT,
5473    libc::SIGUSR1,
5474    libc::SIGUSR2,
5475    libc::SIGPIPE,
5476    libc::SIGTERM,
5477];
5478
5479/// Why a process-directed signal a thread sends to its own multi-threaded
5480/// process does not go to the kernel unchanged, each a named refusal of the
5481/// design for https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034: Detcore then
5482/// chooses the receiving thread itself (`Detcore::process_signal_target`).
5483#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
5484pub(crate) enum OwnProcessSignalRefusal {
5485    /// The signal is not one of `OWN_PROCESS_SIGNALS`.
5486    SignalNotAdmitted,
5487    /// The process leaves the signal at its default, fatal, disposition.
5488    DefaultDisposition,
5489    /// The run records or replays.
5490    RecordReplay,
5491    /// The run replays a recorded schedule (`--replay-schedule-from`).
5492    ScheduleReplay,
5493    /// A guest has sent SIGSTKFLT, which Reverie uses as its timer marker.
5494    GuestMarkerSignal,
5495    /// `--stacktrace-signal` is configured.
5496    StacktraceSignal,
5497    /// The sender blocks the signal.
5498    SenderBlocks,
5499    /// A thread of the process runs or sleeps outside the schedule.
5500    OutOfSchedule(DetTid),
5501    /// A thread of the process has an unknown mask.
5502    UnknownMask(DetTid),
5503    /// The sender is not the thread the call names, and a live thread does
5504    /// not block the signal.
5505    NotSoleReceiver(DetTid),
5506    /// The sender's private queue holds these signals, which it does not block.
5507    PrivatePending(u64),
5508    /// These host-timed sources, which the sender does not block, are armed.
5509    HostTimedSource(u64),
5510    /// Unreaped children have these exit signals, which the sender does not block.
5511    ForeignExitSignal(u64),
5512    /// The scheduler sent these signals, at or below the one sent, and no
5513    /// thread has taken them yet.
5514    UndeliveredSchedulerSignal(u64),
5515    /// A happens-before anchor is set on this call's completion.
5516    PosthookAnchor,
5517    /// The process holds a signalfd.
5518    Signalfd,
5519    /// An exec is in flight in the process.
5520    ExecInFlight,
5521}
5522
5523impl std::fmt::Display for OwnProcessSignalRefusal {
5524    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
5525        match self {
5526            Self::SignalNotAdmitted => write!(
5527                f,
5528                "only SIGHUP, SIGINT, SIGQUIT, SIGUSR1, SIGUSR2, SIGPIPE and SIGTERM are delivered \
5529                 deterministically to the sender"
5530            ),
5531            Self::DefaultDisposition => write!(
5532                f,
5533                "the signal has its default disposition, which ends the process"
5534            ),
5535            Self::RecordReplay => write!(f, "the run records or replays"),
5536            Self::ScheduleReplay => write!(
5537                f,
5538                "the run replays a recorded schedule (--replay-schedule-from), whose trace turn \
5539                 after the send can hand the turn to another thread"
5540            ),
5541            Self::GuestMarkerSignal => write!(
5542                f,
5543                "a guest sent SIGSTKFLT, which Reverie also uses as its timer marker, so a pending \
5544                 copy cannot be told from Reverie's"
5545            ),
5546            Self::StacktraceSignal => write!(f, "--stacktrace-signal is configured"),
5547            Self::SenderBlocks => write!(f, "the sending thread blocks the signal"),
5548            Self::OutOfSchedule(tid) => write!(
5549                f,
5550                "thread {tid} runs or sleeps outside the schedule, at moments host timing sets"
5551            ),
5552            Self::UnknownMask(tid) => write!(f, "thread {tid}'s signal mask is unknown"),
5553            Self::NotSoleReceiver(tid) => write!(
5554                f,
5555                "thread {tid} does not block the signal, so Linux could give it to either thread"
5556            ),
5557            Self::PrivatePending(set) => write!(
5558                f,
5559                "the sending thread has signals pending ({set:#x}) that it would take first"
5560            ),
5561            Self::HostTimedSource(set) => write!(
5562                f,
5563                "a source that sends signals {set:#x} at moments host timing sets is armed"
5564            ),
5565            Self::ForeignExitSignal(set) => write!(
5566                f,
5567                "a child with exit signal {set:#x} is unreaped, and the kernel sends that signal at \
5568                 a moment host timing sets"
5569            ),
5570            Self::UndeliveredSchedulerSignal(set) => write!(
5571                f,
5572                "the scheduler sent signals {set:#x} that no thread has taken yet"
5573            ),
5574            Self::PosthookAnchor => {
5575                write!(f, "a happens-before anchor is set on the call's completion")
5576            }
5577            Self::Signalfd => write!(f, "the process holds a signalfd"),
5578            Self::ExecInFlight => write!(f, "an exec is in flight in the process"),
5579        }
5580    }
5581}
5582
5583/// Everything a process-directed signal `sig` that a thread sends to its own
5584/// multi-threaded process is decided from, all fixed by the schedule at the
5585/// sender's turn (`own_process_signal_refusal`).
5586#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
5587pub(crate) struct OwnProcessSignal {
5588    pub(crate) sig: libc::c_int,
5589    /// Whether the call names the sender itself (S1 of the design).
5590    pub(crate) names_sender: bool,
5591    /// The sender's mask (`SigBlk`).
5592    pub(crate) blocked: u64,
5593    /// The sender's private queue (`SigPnd`).
5594    pub(crate) thread_pending: u64,
5595    /// The process's `SIG_IGN` dispositions (`SigIgn`).
5596    pub(crate) ignored: u64,
5597    /// The process's handlers (`SigCgt`).
5598    pub(crate) caught: u64,
5599    pub(crate) inputs: OwnProcessSignalInputs,
5600    pub(crate) record_replay: bool,
5601    pub(crate) schedule_replay: bool,
5602    pub(crate) stacktrace_signal: bool,
5603    pub(crate) posthook_anchor: bool,
5604    pub(crate) signalfd: bool,
5605}
5606
5607/// The refusal for `send`, or `None` when the kernel's choice of receiver and
5608/// the sender's dequeue are forced: the sender takes the signal on its way back
5609/// to user mode, before any other thread of the process can run
5610/// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4034).
5611pub(crate) fn own_process_signal_refusal(
5612    send: &OwnProcessSignal,
5613) -> Option<OwnProcessSignalRefusal> {
5614    use OwnProcessSignalRefusal as R;
5615    let bit = kernel_sigset_bit(send.sig);
5616    if !OWN_PROCESS_SIGNALS.contains(&send.sig) {
5617        return Some(R::SignalNotAdmitted);
5618    }
5619    if (send.ignored | send.caught) & bit == 0 {
5620        return Some(R::DefaultDisposition);
5621    }
5622    if send.record_replay {
5623        return Some(R::RecordReplay);
5624    }
5625    if send.schedule_replay {
5626        return Some(R::ScheduleReplay);
5627    }
5628    if send.stacktrace_signal {
5629        return Some(R::StacktraceSignal);
5630    }
5631    if send.blocked & bit != 0 {
5632        return Some(R::SenderBlocks);
5633    }
5634    for (tid, state) in &send.inputs.siblings {
5635        if *state == SiblingSignalState::OutOfSchedule {
5636            return Some(R::OutOfSchedule(*tid));
5637        }
5638    }
5639    for (tid, state) in &send.inputs.siblings {
5640        if *state == SiblingSignalState::InSchedule(None) {
5641            return Some(R::UnknownMask(*tid));
5642        }
5643    }
5644    if !send.names_sender {
5645        for (tid, state) in &send.inputs.siblings {
5646            if matches!(state, SiblingSignalState::InSchedule(Some(mask)) if mask & bit == 0) {
5647                return Some(R::NotSoleReceiver(*tid));
5648            }
5649        }
5650    }
5651    let unblocked = !send.blocked;
5652    let private = without_perf_event_signal(send.thread_pending) & unblocked;
5653    if private != 0 {
5654        return Some(R::PrivatePending(private));
5655    }
5656    let host_timed = send.inputs.host_timed & unblocked;
5657    if host_timed != 0 {
5658        return Some(R::HostTimedSource(host_timed));
5659    }
5660    let foreign = send.inputs.foreign_exit_signals & unblocked;
5661    if foreign != 0 {
5662        return Some(R::ForeignExitSignal(foreign));
5663    }
5664    // A signal numbered at or below `sig` is dequeued first or coalesces.
5665    let at_or_below = bit | (bit - 1);
5666    let undelivered = send.inputs.undelivered_scheduler_signals & unblocked & at_or_below;
5667    if undelivered != 0 {
5668        return Some(R::UndeliveredSchedulerSignal(undelivered));
5669    }
5670    if send.posthook_anchor {
5671        return Some(R::PosthookAnchor);
5672    }
5673    if send.signalfd {
5674        return Some(R::Signalfd);
5675    }
5676    if send.inputs.exec_in_flight {
5677        return Some(R::ExecInFlight);
5678    }
5679    if send.inputs.guest_marker_signal {
5680        return Some(R::GuestMarkerSignal);
5681    }
5682    None
5683}
5684
5685/// What a send names, as `Detcore::refuse_reserved_signal_send` reads it.
5686pub(crate) enum ReservedSendTarget {
5687    /// The process a positive pid names (`kill`, `rt_sigqueueinfo`): any of
5688    /// its live threads' ids names it, as in Linux's `kill_pid_info`. Not a
5689    /// group: a non-positive pid takes the ordinary path.
5690    Process(libc::pid_t),
5691    /// A thread (`tkill`), and for `tgkill` and `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` the
5692    /// thread group the call says it is in.
5693    Thread {
5694        tgid: Option<libc::pid_t>,
5695        tid: libc::pid_t,
5696    },
5697    /// What a `pidfd_send_signal` descriptor names, already Detcore's id
5698    /// (`None` for a descriptor with no recorded target), and whether the
5699    /// call is thread-scoped (`PIDFD_SIGNAL_THREAD`).
5700    Pidfd { named: Option<DetPid>, thread: bool },
5701}
5702
5703/// The `siginfo` a send carries, as `Detcore::refuse_reserved_signal_send`
5704/// checks it before a refusal: none (`kill`, `tkill`, `tgkill`); the
5705/// address `rt_sigqueueinfo` and `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` copy (0 for a null
5706/// pointer, which Linux's copy refuses too); or `pidfd_send_signal`'s,
5707/// which may be absent.
5708#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
5709pub(crate) enum ReservedSendSiginfo {
5710    None,
5711    Queued(usize),
5712    Pidfd(Option<usize>),
5713}
5714
5715/// The copy of a refused send's `siginfo` (`Detcore::refused_send_kernel_errno`).
5716enum RefusedSendSiginfo {
5717    /// The call carries none.
5718    NoCopy,
5719    /// Linux's answer to the copy: EFAULT, E2BIG, or for `pidfd_send_signal`
5720    /// EINVAL when `si_signo` is not the signal.
5721    Errno(Errno),
5722    /// The copy succeeds; `code` is its `si_code`.
5723    Copied { code: i32 },
5724    /// The caller cannot read bytes Linux's copy could read, or the caller's
5725    /// mappings cannot be read to tell: Linux's answer is not known.
5726    Unverifiable,
5727}
5728
5729/// A5 step 3c.1c: the copy of a refused send's `siginfo` from the caller's
5730/// memory, in Linux's order (`siginfo_copy_errno`: the head, then the
5731/// expansion only for a layout Linux does not know, then `si_signo` for
5732/// `pidfd_send_signal`). The bytes are read through `Guest::memory()`, which
5733/// on the in-guest backends refuses some memory x86's `copy_from_user` reads
5734/// (a write-only mapping; `reserved_send_siginfo_errno`). So a read that
5735/// fails is EFAULT only where Linux's copy faults too, as the caller's own
5736/// `/proc/<tid>/smaps`, read in its turn, shows: a byte in no mapping (and
5737/// not below a grow-down one) or in a mapping without read, write or execute
5738/// permission (`first_user_unreadable`). Any other failure, or mappings that
5739/// cannot be read, is `Unverifiable`. 3c.0a's reserved-send path
5740/// (`reserved_send_siginfo_errno`) keeps answering such a read EFAULT, a
5741/// known residual.
5742fn refused_send_siginfo<T: RecordOrReplay, G: Guest<Detcore<T>>>(
5743    guest: &mut G,
5744    signal: i32,
5745    siginfo: ReservedSendSiginfo,
5746) -> RefusedSendSiginfo {
5747    let (address, pidfd) = match siginfo {
5748        ReservedSendSiginfo::None | ReservedSendSiginfo::Pidfd(None) => {
5749            return RefusedSendSiginfo::NoCopy;
5750        }
5751        ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(address) => (address, false),
5752        ReservedSendSiginfo::Pidfd(Some(address)) => (address, true),
5753    };
5754    let tid = guest.tid().as_raw();
5755    // Why `[start, start + len)` could not be read: EFAULT only where Linux
5756    // would fault.
5757    let unreadable = |start: usize, len: usize| {
5758        if start.checked_add(len).is_none() {
5759            return RefusedSendSiginfo::Errno(Errno::EFAULT);
5760        }
5761        let Ok(smaps) = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("/proc/{tid}/smaps")) else {
5762            return RefusedSendSiginfo::Unverifiable;
5763        };
5764        match first_user_unreadable(&parse_smaps(&smaps), start, len) {
5765            Some(Unreadable::NoPermission)
5766            | Some(Unreadable::Hole {
5767                below_growsdown: false,
5768            }) => RefusedSendSiginfo::Errno(Errno::EFAULT),
5769            _ => RefusedSendSiginfo::Unverifiable,
5770        }
5771    };
5772    let head = address
5773        .checked_add(KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE)
5774        .and_then(|_| AddrMut::<[u8; KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE]>::from_raw(address))
5775        .and_then(|head| guest.memory().read_value(head).ok());
5776    let Some(head) = head else {
5777        return unreadable(address, KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE);
5778    };
5779    let field = |offset: usize| {
5780        i32::from_ne_bytes(head[offset..offset + 4].try_into().expect("four bytes"))
5781    };
5782    let (si_signo, code) = (field(0), field(8));
5783    if !known_siginfo_layout(if pidfd { si_signo } else { signal }, code) {
5784        let start = address + KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE;
5785        let expansion = start
5786            .checked_add(SI_EXPANSION_SIZE)
5787            .and_then(|_| AddrMut::<[u8; SI_EXPANSION_SIZE]>::from_raw(start))
5788            .and_then(|expansion| guest.memory().read_value(expansion).ok());
5789        match expansion {
5790            None => return unreadable(start, SI_EXPANSION_SIZE),
5791            Some(bytes) if bytes.iter().any(|byte| *byte != 0) => {
5792                return RefusedSendSiginfo::Errno(Errno::E2BIG);
5793            }
5794            Some(_) => {}
5795        }
5796    }
5797    if pidfd && si_signo != signal {
5798        return RefusedSendSiginfo::Errno(Errno::EINVAL);
5799    }
5800    RefusedSendSiginfo::Copied { code }
5801}
5802
5803/// What a send `syscall` carries that Linux checks before sending
5804/// (`Detcore::refused_send_kernel_errno`): its `siginfo` (`rt_sigqueueinfo`,
5805/// `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, `pidfd_send_signal`'s third argument) and the tgid a
5806/// `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` names.
5807fn refused_send_arguments(
5808    syscall: syscalls::Syscall,
5809) -> (ReservedSendSiginfo, Option<libc::pid_t>) {
5810    let address = |info: Option<AddrMut<'_, libc::siginfo_t>>| info.map_or(0, |info| info.as_raw());
5811    match syscall {
5812        syscalls::Syscall::RtSigqueueinfo(call) => {
5813            (ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(address(call.siginfo())), None)
5814        }
5815        syscalls::Syscall::RtTgsigqueueinfo(call) => (
5816            ReservedSendSiginfo::Queued(address(call.siginfo())),
5817            Some(call.tgid()),
5818        ),
5819        syscalls::Syscall::Tgkill(call) => (ReservedSendSiginfo::None, Some(call.tgid())),
5820        other => {
5821            let (sysno, args) = reverie::syscalls::SyscallInfo::into_parts(other);
5822            if sysno == Sysno::pidfd_send_signal {
5823                (
5824                    ReservedSendSiginfo::Pidfd((args.arg2 != 0).then_some(args.arg2)),
5825                    None,
5826                )
5827            } else {
5828                (ReservedSendSiginfo::None, None)
5829            }
5830        }
5831    }
5832}
5833
5834/// The size of Linux's `struct kernel_siginfo` on x86-64, the part of a
5835/// `siginfo_t` every copy from user memory reads, and of the expansion after
5836/// it (`SI_EXPANSION_SIZE`: `SI_MAX_SIZE`, 128, less that).
5837const KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE: usize = 48;
5838const SI_EXPANSION_SIZE: usize = 128 - KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE;
5839
5840/// Whether Linux knows the layout of a siginfo with signal `signo` and code
5841/// `code`, so that it need not check the expansion bytes
5842/// (`known_siginfo_layout`, kernel/signal.c:3320-3337 at v6.12, with
5843/// `sig_sicodes` at :3304-3318 and the limits of
5844/// include/uapi/asm-generic/siginfo.h).
5845fn known_siginfo_layout(signo: i32, code: i32) -> bool {
5846    const SI_KERNEL: i32 = 0x80;
5847    const SI_DETHREAD: i32 = -7;
5848    const SI_ASYNCNL: i32 = -60;
5849    const NSIGPOLL: i32 = 6;
5850    // `sig_specific_sicodes`: a signal below SIGRTMIN with its own codes.
5851    let specific_limit = match signo {
5852        libc::SIGILL => Some(11),
5853        libc::SIGFPE => Some(15),
5854        libc::SIGSEGV => Some(10),
5855        libc::SIGBUS => Some(5),
5856        libc::SIGTRAP => Some(6),
5857        libc::SIGCHLD => Some(6),
5858        libc::SIGPOLL => Some(NSIGPOLL),
5859        libc::SIGSYS => Some(2),
5860        _ => None,
5861    };
5862    if code == SI_KERNEL {
5863        true
5864    } else if code > 0 {
5865        code <= specific_limit.unwrap_or(NSIGPOLL)
5866    } else {
5867        code >= SI_DETHREAD || code == SI_ASYNCNL
5868    }
5869}
5870
5871/// Linux's answer to the copy of a send's `siginfo` from user memory, which
5872/// comes before any permission check (v6.12 kernel/signal.c): `head`, the
5873/// first `KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE` bytes, `None` when the caller cannot read
5874/// them, and `read_expansion`, which reads the rest (`None` when it cannot)
5875/// and is called only when Linux reads it too. The head must be readable
5876/// (EFAULT, `copy_siginfo_from_user`); only for a layout Linux does not know
5877/// is the expansion read at all, and then it must be readable (EFAULT) and
5878/// every byte of it 0 (E2BIG, `post_copy_siginfo_from_user`, :3394-3415), so
5879/// a known layout whose expansion is unmapped copies. `rt_sigqueueinfo` and
5880/// `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` judge the layout by the call's `signal`
5881/// (`__copy_siginfo_from_user`, :3417-3424); `pidfd_send_signal` (`pidfd`)
5882/// by the siginfo's own `si_signo`, which must then equal `signal` (EINVAL,
5883/// :3959-3966).
5884fn siginfo_copy_errno(
5885    signal: i32,
5886    pidfd: bool,
5887    head: Option<[u8; KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE]>,
5888    read_expansion: impl FnOnce() -> Option<[u8; SI_EXPANSION_SIZE]>,
5889) -> Option<Errno> {
5890    let Some(head) = head else {
5891        return Some(Errno::EFAULT);
5892    };
5893    let field = |offset: usize| {
5894        i32::from_ne_bytes(head[offset..offset + 4].try_into().expect("four bytes"))
5895    };
5896    let (si_signo, si_code) = (field(0), field(8));
5897    let layout_signo = if pidfd { si_signo } else { signal };
5898    if !known_siginfo_layout(layout_signo, si_code) {
5899        match read_expansion() {
5900            None => return Some(Errno::EFAULT),
5901            Some(bytes) if bytes.iter().any(|byte| *byte != 0) => return Some(Errno::E2BIG),
5902            Some(_) => {}
5903        }
5904    }
5905    (pidfd && si_signo != signal).then_some(Errno::EINVAL)
5906}
5907
5908/// Whether the thread group a `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` names
5909/// (`group`) is the named thread's own process (`leader`), as Linux requires
5910/// (`task_tgid_vnr(p) == tgid` in `do_send_specific`, kernel/signal.c:3994 at
5911/// v6.12); a tgid naming another thread of that process, or another process,
5912/// is ESRCH there. Always true for a call that names no group.
5913fn reserved_send_names_its_group(group: Option<DetPid>, leader: Option<DetPid>) -> bool {
5914    group.is_none_or(|group| leader == Some(group))
5915}
5916
5917/// Whether a reserved send (`Detcore::refuse_reserved_signal_send`) names the
5918/// caller's own process by its ids alone, before any lookup: `named` is the
5919/// caller's thread or its process's id, or `group`, the thread group a
5920/// `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` names, is the caller's process. Such a send
5921/// keeps its ordinary path; a `group` that does not hold `named` is the
5922/// kernel's ESRCH there.
5923fn reserved_send_names_own(
5924    own: DetPid,
5925    caller: DetTid,
5926    named: DetTid,
5927    group: Option<DetPid>,
5928) -> bool {
5929    named == caller || named == own || group == Some(own)
5930}
5931
5932/// Whether a reserved send is refused, given `targets`, the live threads of
5933/// the process the send names in Detcore's model (`process_signal_targets`),
5934/// and `thread`, the thread a thread-directed send names: only a process
5935/// with a live thread, which is not the caller's own (the caller is a live
5936/// thread of its own process), and for a thread-directed send only when the
5937/// named thread is one of those live threads. Anything else keeps Linux's
5938/// answer on the ordinary path: a zombie or a reaped process has no live
5939/// thread (0 or ESRCH), and an exited thread, or one outside the group a
5940/// `tgkill` names, is ESRCH.
5941fn reserved_send_refuses(caller: DetTid, thread: Option<DetTid>, targets: &[DetTid]) -> bool {
5942    !targets.is_empty()
5943        && !targets.contains(&caller)
5944        && thread.is_none_or(|thread| targets.contains(&thread))
5945}
5946
5947/// How a process-directed signal the calling thread sends goes on
5948/// (`Detcore::own_process_signal`).
5949/// What a positive pid the calling thread names refers to
5950/// (`Detcore::name_guest_pid`).
5951enum NamedGuestPid {
5952    /// This task, by its DetTid. `own_init_caught` is set when it belongs to
5953    /// the caller's own PID namespace's init, to the signals that init catches.
5954    Task {
5955        task: DetTid,
5956        own_init_caught: Option<u64>,
5957    },
5958    /// The call's answer, decided here: ESRCH for a pid no task of the
5959    /// caller's namespace has, or a refusal by name.
5960    Answer(Result<i64, Error>),
5961}
5962
5963/// How a process-directed signal reaches its process
5964/// (`Detcore::process_signal_target`).
5965#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
5966pub(crate) enum ProcessSignalTarget {
5967    /// The process has no live thread: a zombie, which Linux still finds and
5968    /// signals successfully, discarding the signal.
5969    Zombie,
5970    /// The thread that takes the signal: the process's only live thread, or,
5971    /// among several, the receiver Detcore chose by Linux's rule
5972    /// (`Scheduler::process_signal_receiver`). The call is forwarded
5973    /// unchanged, so the copy is the kernel's own; where other threads could
5974    /// take it from the shared queue first, the scheduler resumes this one
5975    /// before them (`Scheduler::first_process_signal_taker`).
5976    Thread(DetTid),
5977    /// Several live threads and none wants the signal: the call is forwarded
5978    /// unchanged too, and the signal waits in the process's shared queue for
5979    /// the first thread that can take it, waking none.
5980    SharedQueue,
5981}
5982
5983enum OwnProcessSend {
5984    /// The ordinary path decides: the call does not name the sender's own
5985    /// multi-threaded process, or the schedule does not force the kernel's
5986    /// choice of receiver.
5987    NotOwn,
5988    /// Admitted: forward the call unchanged, holding the sender's turn.
5989    Admitted,
5990    /// Refused by name, with the policy's answer: the replay of a recorded
5991    /// schedule of a send that would be admitted.
5992    Refused(Result<i64, Error>),
5993}
5994
5995/// What `Detcore::rt_sigqueueinfo_precheck` found.
5996enum RtSigqueueinfoPrecheck {
5997    /// The kernel fails the call without sending (EPERM, or E2BIG, after a
5998    /// complete copy): forward it unchanged.
5999    KernelFails,
6000    /// The kernel's copy certainly faults: Hermit answers EFAULT itself.
6001    Efault,
6002    /// The call goes on to admission.
6003    Proceeds,
6004    /// Hermit cannot read the whole `siginfo_t`, and cannot prove that the
6005    /// kernel's copy faults: `[vvar]`, which neither tracer read reaches; a
6006    /// tail running into a gap; a first 48 bytes in the gap below a grow-down
6007    /// mapping, which the kernel's copy may grow into; or an unreadable
6008    /// `/proc/<tid>/smaps`.
6009    Unreadable,
6010}
6011
6012/// One mapping of a `/proc/<pid>/smaps` listing.
6013#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
6014struct Mapping {
6015    start: usize,
6016    end: usize,
6017    /// Whether user mode can read it: on x86 any of read, write or execute
6018    /// permission allows a read; none (`---`) faults.
6019    user_readable: bool,
6020    /// Whether user mode, and so the kernel's copy to user memory, can
6021    /// write it: write permission.
6022    user_writable: bool,
6023    /// `VmFlags` `gd`: a fault just below it can grow it downwards.
6024    growsdown: bool,
6025}
6026
6027/// The mappings of a `/proc/<pid>/smaps` listing, in address order.
6028fn parse_smaps(smaps: &str) -> Vec<Mapping> {
6029    let mut mappings: Vec<Mapping> = Vec::new();
6030    for line in smaps.lines() {
6031        if let Some(flags) = line.strip_prefix("VmFlags:") {
6032            if let Some(last) = mappings.last_mut() {
6033                last.growsdown = flags.split_whitespace().any(|flag| flag == "gd");
6034            }
6035            continue;
6036        }
6037        let mut fields = line.split_whitespace();
6038        let Some((start, end)) = fields.next().and_then(|range| range.split_once('-')) else {
6039            continue;
6040        };
6041        let (Ok(start), Ok(end), Some(perms)) = (
6042            usize::from_str_radix(start, 16),
6043            usize::from_str_radix(end, 16),
6044            fields.next(),
6045        ) else {
6046            continue;
6047        };
6048        if perms.len() < 4 {
6049            continue;
6050        }
6051        mappings.push(Mapping {
6052            start,
6053            end,
6054            user_readable: perms.chars().take(3).any(|perm| perm != '-'),
6055            user_writable: perms.chars().nth(1) == Some('w'),
6056            growsdown: false,
6057        });
6058    }
6059    mappings.sort_by_key(|mapping| mapping.start);
6060    mappings
6061}
6062
6063/// A5 step 3c.1d (round-1 ruling 4, 3c.1c's rule): whether Linux's copy
6064/// from user memory of `[start, start + len)` by thread `tid` faults, as the
6065/// thread's `/proc/<tid>/smaps` shows in its own turn: `Some(true)` for a byte
6066/// in no mapping and not below a grow-down one, or in a mapping with no read,
6067/// write or execute permission (x86 user mode reads any page with one of
6068/// them; `first_user_unreadable`), or a range that overflows; `Some(false)`
6069/// when every byte is readable by user mode or lies below a grow-down mapping
6070/// the copy may grow; `None` when the mappings cannot be read.
6071pub(crate) fn user_copy_faults(tid: i32, start: usize, len: usize) -> Option<bool> {
6072    if start.checked_add(len).is_none() {
6073        return Some(true);
6074    }
6075    let smaps = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("/proc/{tid}/smaps")).ok()?;
6076    Some(matches!(
6077        first_user_unreadable(&parse_smaps(&smaps), start, len),
6078        Some(Unreadable::NoPermission)
6079            | Some(Unreadable::Hole {
6080                below_growsdown: false
6081            })
6082    ))
6083}
6084
6085/// A5 step 3c.1d (round-3 ruling 3): whether Linux's copy to user memory of
6086/// `[start, start + len)` by thread `tid` faults, as the thread's
6087/// `/proc/<tid>/smaps` shows in its own turn: `Some(true)` for a byte in no
6088/// mapping and not below a grow-down one, or in a mapping without write
6089/// permission, or a range that overflows; `Some(false)` when every byte is
6090/// writable or lies below a grow-down mapping the copy may grow; `None` when
6091/// the mappings cannot be read.
6092///
6093/// smaps' write permission is not always the kernel's whole answer: a write
6094/// can still fault on a page that smaps shows writable, under a protection
6095/// key that denies writes, a userfaultfd write-protected range, or a shared
6096/// file mapping beyond the file's end. There `Some(false)` is wrong, and a
6097/// caller probing in place gets the copy-out's EFAULT where Linux, with a
6098/// signal pending at entry, would make no copy-out (a named residual of A5
6099/// step 3c.1d, review round 4).
6100pub(crate) fn user_copy_out_faults(tid: i32, start: usize, len: usize) -> Option<bool> {
6101    if start.checked_add(len).is_none() {
6102        return Some(true);
6103    }
6104    let smaps = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("/proc/{tid}/smaps")).ok()?;
6105    Some(user_unwritable(&parse_smaps(&smaps), start, len))
6106}
6107
6108/// Whether a byte of `[address, address + len)` is one a copy to user memory
6109/// faults on: in a mapping without write permission, or in a hole not below a
6110/// grow-down mapping (`user_copy_out_faults`).
6111fn user_unwritable(mappings: &[Mapping], address: usize, len: usize) -> bool {
6112    let Some(end) = address.checked_add(len) else {
6113        return true;
6114    };
6115    let mut next = address;
6116    while next < end {
6117        match mappings.iter().find(|mapping| mapping.end > next) {
6118            Some(mapping) if mapping.start <= next => {
6119                if !mapping.user_writable {
6120                    return true;
6121                }
6122                next = mapping.end;
6123            }
6124            Some(above) if above.growsdown => next = above.start,
6125            _ => return true,
6126        }
6127    }
6128    false
6129}
6130
6131/// Why a byte is not readable by user mode (`first_user_unreadable`).
6132#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
6133enum Unreadable {
6134    /// In a mapping without read, write or execute permission.
6135    NoPermission,
6136    /// In no mapping; `below_growsdown` when the next mapping above it can
6137    /// grow down into it.
6138    Hole { below_growsdown: bool },
6139}
6140
6141/// Where the first byte of `[address, address + len)` that user mode cannot
6142/// read lies, or `None` when user mode can read them all. A range that
6143/// overflows the address space counts as a hole.
6144fn first_user_unreadable(mappings: &[Mapping], address: usize, len: usize) -> Option<Unreadable> {
6145    let Some(end) = address.checked_add(len) else {
6146        return Some(Unreadable::Hole {
6147            below_growsdown: false,
6148        });
6149    };
6150    let mut next = address;
6151    while next < end {
6152        match mappings.iter().find(|mapping| mapping.end > next) {
6153            Some(mapping) if mapping.start <= next => {
6154                if !mapping.user_readable {
6155                    return Some(Unreadable::NoPermission);
6156                }
6157                next = mapping.end;
6158            }
6159            above => {
6160                return Some(Unreadable::Hole {
6161                    below_growsdown: above.is_some_and(|mapping| mapping.growsdown),
6162                });
6163            }
6164        }
6165    }
6166    None
6167}
6168
6169/// The signals a send of `sig` lets Linux send later at a moment set by host
6170/// timing, as a kernel sigset; 0 for every signal but the four that stop by
6171/// default. A stopped member makes its process group one that an exit can
6172/// orphan with a stopped job, and Linux then sends that whole group SIGHUP and
6173/// SIGCONT at the exiting process's physical exit (`kill_orphaned_pgrp` in
6174/// kernel/exit.c). Apart from a guest's send, only a terminal stops a guest
6175/// process, and a terminal's signals, these two among them, are recorded with
6176/// `terminal_signals`.
6177fn orphaned_group_signals(sig: libc::c_int) -> u64 {
6178    match sig {
6179        libc::SIGSTOP | libc::SIGTSTP | libc::SIGTTIN | libc::SIGTTOU => {
6180            kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGHUP) | kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCONT)
6181        }
6182        _ => 0,
6183    }
6184}
6185
6186/// Whether a held signal the scheduler woke a sleep for (`held_woken`, a
6187/// child-exit `SIGCHLD`) ends it: as Linux delivery would, only when this
6188/// thread's mask leaves it unblocked and it is caught or acted on by default
6189/// (`KernelSignalState::interrupting`). A blocked one stays pending, and a
6190/// default-ignored one (`SIGCHLD` without a handler) is discarded, and either
6191/// way the sleep goes on (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4126).
6192///
6193/// The caller has already removed a committed `SIGCHLD` that no longer counts:
6194/// the scheduler sends it to the process, so the kernel gives it to whichever
6195/// thread resumes first without blocking it, and a schedule may run a sibling
6196/// first (`--chaos-target-races`). Taken by that sibling, it is gone from the
6197/// child-exit ledger's send window (`ChildExitSigchldControl::PlacedPending`),
6198/// and the sleep goes on to its original deadline, as a futex wait woken for a
6199/// signal waits again (`futex_wait_held_after_wake`). Answering EINTR for it
6200/// anyway was a result Linux cannot give: the call interrupted, the handler run
6201/// in another thread (the Claude re-check of
6202/// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4131). A held signal a host-timed
6203/// source can also post is decided from the mask and dispositions alone.
6204fn held_wake_ends_sleep(
6205    state: &crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState,
6206    held_woken: KernelSigset,
6207) -> bool {
6208    state.interrupting(state.blocked) & held_woken != 0
6209}
6210
6211#[cfg(test)]
6212mod tests {
6213    /// A first probe's not-run report restarts the call; any other result,
6214    /// errno or value, passes through unchanged.
6215    #[test]
6216    fn a_first_probe_not_run_restarts_the_call() {
6217        let restart = |result: Result<u64, Error>| restart_if_first_probe_not_run(result);
6218        assert!(matches!(
6219            restart(Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS.into())),
6220            Err(Error::Errno(Errno::ERESTARTNOINTR))
6221        ));
6222        for errno in [
6223            Errno::EFAULT,
6224            Errno::EINVAL,
6225            Errno::EINTR,
6226            Errno::ERESTARTNOHAND,
6227        ] {
6228            assert!(
6229                matches!(restart(Err(errno.into())), Err(Error::Errno(e)) if e == errno),
6230                "{errno} passes through"
6231            );
6232        }
6233        assert!(matches!(restart(Ok(7)), Ok(7)));
6234    }
6235
6236    /// A reserved send names the caller's own process by its ids when it names
6237    /// the calling thread, the process's id, or the process as the thread
6238    /// group; any other id needs a lookup (A5 step 3c slice 0a).
6239    #[test]
6240    fn a_reserved_send_to_the_callers_own_process_is_decided_by_its_ids() {
6241        let own = DetPid::from_raw(3);
6242        let caller = DetTid::from_raw(4);
6243        let other = DetPid::from_raw(5);
6244        assert!(reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, caller, None));
6245        assert!(reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, own, None));
6246        // tgkill(own, other): the group is the caller's; the kernel answers.
6247        assert!(reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, other, Some(own)));
6248        assert!(!reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, other, None));
6249        assert!(!reserved_send_names_own(own, caller, other, Some(other)));
6250    }
6251
6252    /// A `tgkill` or `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` is refused only when the tgid it
6253    /// names is the thread's own process: a tgid naming another thread of
6254    /// that process, or another process, is Linux's ESRCH
6255    /// (`do_send_specific`), and a call that names no group always matches.
6256    #[test]
6257    fn a_reserved_thread_send_must_name_the_threads_own_group() {
6258        let leader = DetPid::from_raw(5);
6259        let sibling = DetPid::from_raw(6);
6260        assert!(reserved_send_names_its_group(None, Some(leader)));
6261        assert!(reserved_send_names_its_group(Some(leader), Some(leader)));
6262        assert!(!reserved_send_names_its_group(Some(sibling), Some(leader)));
6263        assert!(!reserved_send_names_its_group(Some(leader), None));
6264    }
6265
6266    /// Linux's siginfo copy, which comes before any permission check: an
6267    /// unreadable head is EFAULT; an unknown layout needs a readable, all-zero
6268    /// expansion (EFAULT, E2BIG); `rt_*sigqueueinfo` judge the layout by the
6269    /// call's signal and `pidfd_send_signal` by the siginfo's own, which must
6270    /// equal the call's (EINVAL).
6271    #[test]
6272    fn a_reserved_send_checks_its_siginfo_as_linux_copies_it() {
6273        let head = |signo: i32, code: i32| {
6274            let mut bytes = [0u8; KERNEL_SIGINFO_SIZE];
6275            bytes[0..4].copy_from_slice(&signo.to_ne_bytes());
6276            bytes[8..12].copy_from_slice(&code.to_ne_bytes());
6277            Some(bytes)
6278        };
6279        let zero = || Some([0u8; SI_EXPANSION_SIZE]);
6280        let dirty = || {
6281            let mut bytes = [0u8; SI_EXPANSION_SIZE];
6282            bytes[SI_EXPANSION_SIZE - 1] = 1;
6283            Some(bytes)
6284        };
6285        let unreadable = || None;
6286        // A known layout never reads its expansion, as Linux does not.
6287        let never = || -> Option<[u8; SI_EXPANSION_SIZE]> {
6288            panic!("the expansion of a known layout must not be read")
6289        };
6290        let sys = libc::SIGSYS;
6291        for pidfd in [false, true] {
6292            assert_eq!(
6293                siginfo_copy_errno(sys, pidfd, None, never),
6294                Some(Errno::EFAULT)
6295            );
6296            // SI_QUEUE is a known layout: the expansion is not read, so an
6297            // unreadable one copies.
6298            assert_eq!(siginfo_copy_errno(sys, pidfd, head(sys, -1), never), None);
6299            // -8 is below SI_DETHREAD and not SI_ASYNCNL: unknown.
6300            assert_eq!(siginfo_copy_errno(sys, pidfd, head(sys, -8), zero), None);
6301            assert_eq!(
6302                siginfo_copy_errno(sys, pidfd, head(sys, -8), dirty),
6303                Some(Errno::E2BIG)
6304            );
6305            assert_eq!(
6306                siginfo_copy_errno(sys, pidfd, head(sys, -8), unreadable),
6307                Some(Errno::EFAULT)
6308            );
6309        }
6310        // A mismatched si_signo is EINVAL for pidfd_send_signal only.
6311        let usr1 = head(libc::SIGUSR1, -1);
6312        assert_eq!(
6313            siginfo_copy_errno(sys, true, usr1, never),
6314            Some(Errno::EINVAL)
6315        );
6316        assert_eq!(siginfo_copy_errno(sys, false, usr1, never), None);
6317        // rt_*sigqueueinfo judge the layout by the call's signal: SIGSYS's
6318        // code 2 (SYS_USER_DISPATCH) is known; code 3 is not.
6319        assert_eq!(siginfo_copy_errno(sys, false, head(0, 2), never), None);
6320        assert_eq!(
6321            siginfo_copy_errno(sys, false, head(0, 3), dirty),
6322            Some(Errno::E2BIG)
6323        );
6324    }
6325
6326    /// `known_siginfo_layout`'s table: SI_KERNEL, the codes down to
6327    /// SI_DETHREAD and SI_ASYNCNL are known for every signal; a positive code
6328    /// up to the signal's own limit, or NSIGPOLL for a signal without one.
6329    #[test]
6330    fn known_siginfo_layout_matches_linuxs_table() {
6331        for signo in [
6332            libc::SIGSYS,
6333            libc::SIGSEGV,
6334            libc::SIGTRAP,
6335            libc::SIGUSR1,
6336            40,
6337        ] {
6338            for code in [0x80, 0, -1, -6, -7, -60] {
6339                assert!(known_siginfo_layout(signo, code), "{signo} {code}");
6340            }
6341            for code in [-8, -59, -61, 0x81] {
6342                assert!(!known_siginfo_layout(signo, code), "{signo} {code}");
6343            }
6344        }
6345        for (signo, limit) in [
6346            (libc::SIGILL, 11),
6347            (libc::SIGFPE, 15),
6348            (libc::SIGSEGV, 10),
6349            (libc::SIGBUS, 5),
6350            (libc::SIGTRAP, 6),
6351            (libc::SIGCHLD, 6),
6352            (libc::SIGPOLL, 6),
6353            (libc::SIGSYS, 2),
6354            (libc::SIGUSR1, 6),
6355        ] {
6356            assert!(known_siginfo_layout(signo, limit), "{signo}");
6357            assert!(!known_siginfo_layout(signo, limit + 1), "{signo}");
6358        }
6359    }

6361    /// Only a send to a process with a live thread, other than the caller's
6362    /// own, is refused, and a thread-directed one only when the named thread
6363    /// is live in it: a zombie or reaped process (no live thread), an exited
6364    /// thread and a thread outside the named group keep Linux's answer.
6365    #[test]
6366    fn a_reserved_send_is_refused_only_to_another_live_process() {
6367        let caller = DetTid::from_raw(3);
6368        let other = DetTid::from_raw(5);
6369        let other_sibling = DetTid::from_raw(6);
6370        let live_other = [other, other_sibling];
6371        // kill(other): a live process that is not the caller's.
6372        assert!(reserved_send_refuses(caller, None, &live_other));
6373        // tkill/tgkill of a live thread of it.
6374        assert!(reserved_send_refuses(
6375            caller,
6376            Some(other_sibling),
6377            &live_other
6378        ));
6379        // The caller's own process.
6380        assert!(!reserved_send_refuses(caller, None, &[caller, other]));
6381        // A zombie or a reaped process: no live thread.
6382        assert!(!reserved_send_refuses(caller, None, &[]));
6383        assert!(!reserved_send_refuses(caller, Some(other), &[]));
6384        // An exited thread, or one outside the group tgkill names.
6385        assert!(!reserved_send_refuses(
6386            caller,
6387            Some(DetTid::from_raw(7)),
6388            &live_other
6389        ));
6390    }

6413            ("dbt", BackendCapabilities::DBT, false),
6414            (
6415                "liteinst in-guest",
6416                BackendCapabilities::LITEINST_IN_GUEST,
6417                false,
6418            ),
6419        ] {
6420            assert_eq!(rt_sigsuspend_never_sleeps(&backend), never_sleeps, "{name}");

6424    /// A send of each of the four default-stop signals arms the orphaned
6425    /// process group's SIGHUP and SIGCONT; no other signal arms anything.
6426    #[test]
6427    fn only_a_default_stop_signal_arms_the_orphaned_group_signals() {
6428        let hup_cont = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGHUP) | kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCONT);
6429        for sig in 0..=65 {
6430            let stops = matches!(
6431                sig,
6432                libc::SIGSTOP | libc::SIGTSTP | libc::SIGTTIN | libc::SIGTTOU
6433            );
6434            assert_eq!(
6435                orphaned_group_signals(sig),
6436                if stops { hup_cont } else { 0 },
6437                "signal {sig}"
6438            );
6439        }

6442    /// A child-exit `SIGCHLD` the scheduler woke a sleep for ends it only when
6443    /// it is unblocked and caught: blocked, or with its default (ignore)
6444    /// action, the sleep goes on, as on Linux
6445    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4126). Taken by a sibling
6446    /// first, it is no longer among the held signals the wake counts, and the
6447    /// sleep goes on too (the Claude re-check of
6448    /// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4131). A default-fatal held
6449    /// signal (a host-timed `SIGALRM` without a handler) ends it.
6450    #[test]
6451    fn a_held_wake_ends_a_sleep_only_when_unblocked_and_caught_or_fatal() {
6452        use crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState;
6453        let chld = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD);
6454        let alrm = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGALRM);
6455        let state = |caught: KernelSigset, blocked: KernelSigset| KernelSignalState {
6456            caught,
6457            blocked,
6458            ..Default::default()
6459        };
6460        assert!(held_wake_ends_sleep(&state(chld, 0), chld));
6461        assert!(!held_wake_ends_sleep(&state(chld, chld), chld));
6462        assert!(!held_wake_ends_sleep(&state(0, 0), chld));
6463        assert!(held_wake_ends_sleep(&state(0, 0), alrm));
6464        assert!(!held_wake_ends_sleep(&state(0, alrm), alrm));
6465        assert!(
6466            !held_wake_ends_sleep(&state(chld, 0), 0),
6467            "taken by a sibling"
6468        );
6469    }
6470
6471    /// In a process whose send window is unusable, a sleep woken for the
6472    /// scheduler's `SIGCHLD` ends for it when the copy is still pending, in its
6473    /// process's queue or its own, and unblocked and caught. It parks again
6474    /// when another thread took the copy, its mask blocks it, or it is left at
6475    /// its default action, which ignores it
6476    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4229).
6477    #[test]
6478    fn a_woken_sleep_counts_a_pending_sigchld_in_an_unusable_window() {
6479        use crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState;
6480        let chld = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD);
6481        let state =
6482            |caught: KernelSigset, blocked: KernelSigset, shared: KernelSigset| KernelSignalState {
6483                caught,
6484                blocked,
6485                pending: shared,
6486                shared_pending: shared,
6487                ..Default::default()
6488            };
6489        assert!(woken_sigchld_pending(&state(chld, 0, chld), chld));
6490        assert!(
6491            !woken_sigchld_pending(&state(chld, 0, 0), chld),
6492            "another thread took it"
6493        );
6494        assert!(
6495            !woken_sigchld_pending(&state(chld, chld, chld), chld),
6496            "blocked"
6497        );
6498        assert!(
6499            !woken_sigchld_pending(&state(0, 0, chld), chld),
6500            "default, ignored"
6501        );
6502        assert!(
6503            !woken_sigchld_pending(&state(chld, 0, chld), 0),
6504            "host-timed SIGCHLD keeps it held"
6505        );
6506        let private = KernelSignalState {
6507            caught: chld,
6508            pending: chld,
6509            thread_pending: chld,
6510            ..Default::default()
6511        };
6512        assert!(woken_sigchld_pending(&private, chld));
6513    }
6514
6515    /// A claimed `SIGCHLD` stays claimed at a syscall while it is pending for
6516    /// the thread, in either queue: among them the syscall a held SIGUSR1's
6517    /// handler makes while its mask blocks SIGCHLD. It is forgotten once
6518    /// SIGCHLD is not pending, or when the state cannot be read, and an
6519    /// unclaimed thread reads nothing (the Codex review of the
6520    /// claimed-delivery fix; https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4346).
6521    #[test]
6522    fn a_claimed_sigchld_stays_claimed_while_pending() {
6523        use crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState;
6524        let chld = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD);
6525        let usr1 = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGUSR1);
6526        let state = |blocked: KernelSigset, thread_pending: KernelSigset, shared: KernelSigset| {
6527            Ok::<_, Errno>(KernelSignalState {
6528                caught: chld | usr1,
6529                blocked,
6530                pending: thread_pending | shared,
6531                thread_pending,
6532                shared_pending: shared,
6533                ..Default::default()
6534            })
6535        };
6536        assert!(claim_still_pending(true, || state(0, 0, chld)));
6537        assert!(claim_still_pending(true, || state(0, chld, 0)));
6538        assert!(
6539            claim_still_pending(true, || state(chld | usr1, 0, chld)),
6540            "inside the held SIGUSR1's handler, whose mask holds SIGCHLD"
6541        );
6542        assert!(
6543            !claim_still_pending(true, || state(0, 0, usr1)),
6544            "taken elsewhere"
6545        );
6546        assert!(
6547            !claim_still_pending(true, || Err::<KernelSignalState, _>(Errno::ESRCH)),
6548            "unreadable"
6549        );
6550        assert!(
6551            !claim_still_pending(false, || -> Result<KernelSignalState, Errno> {
6552                panic!("an unclaimed thread reads no state")
6553            }),
6554            "unclaimed"
6555        );
6556    }
6557
6558    /// Before it parks, a sleep whose private queue already holds a guest's
6559    /// thread-directed `SIGCHLD` asks whether the copy is committed, and a
6560    /// committed one that is unblocked and caught ends the sleep as pending
6561    /// for it: the copy was sent before the sleeper filed its request, so the
6562    /// sender's turn had no sleep to wake (the Codex review of
6563    /// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/pull/4177). Without one, or with no
6564    /// wake and nothing private, it asks nothing; blocked, it does not end the
6565    /// sleep.
6566    #[test]
6567    fn a_private_sigchld_sent_before_the_sleep_is_filed_ends_it_before_it_parks() {
6568        use crate::syscalls::threads::KernelSignalState;
6569        let chld = kernel_sigset_bit(libc::SIGCHLD);
6570        let state = |blocked: KernelSigset, thread_pending: KernelSigset| KernelSignalState {
6571            caught: chld,
6572            blocked,
6573            pending: thread_pending,
6574            thread_pending,
6575            ..Default::default()
6576        };
6577        // Before park: no wake named a signal (`held_woken` 0).
6578        assert!(asks_committed_sigchld(0, &state(0, chld), chld));
6579        assert!(!asks_committed_sigchld(0, &state(0, 0), chld));
6580        assert!(
6581            !asks_committed_sigchld(0, &state(0, chld), 0),
6582            "a host-timed SIGCHLD source keeps it held"
6583        );
6584        assert_eq!(
6585            committed_sigchld_verdict(&state(0, chld), 0, chld),
6586            Some(SleepSignal::Pending)
6587        );
6588        assert_eq!(committed_sigchld_verdict(&state(chld, chld), 0, chld), None);
6589        assert_eq!(committed_sigchld_verdict(&state(0, chld), 0, 0), None);
6590        // After a wake for it, the same copy is the held signal it named.
6591        assert!(asks_committed_sigchld(chld, &state(0, 0), chld));
6592        assert_eq!(
6593            committed_sigchld_verdict(&state(0, chld), chld, chld),
6594            Some(SleepSignal::Held)
6595        );
6596    }

6605    /// The mask `rt_sigsuspend` (and the scheduler's record of it) sleeps
6606    /// under can never block SIGKILL or SIGSTOP; every other bit is kept.
6607    #[test]
6608    fn the_installed_mask_cannot_block_kill_or_stop() {
6609        let kill = 1_u64 << (libc::SIGKILL - 1);
6610        let stop = 1_u64 << (libc::SIGSTOP - 1);
6611        assert_eq!(kernel_installed_signal_mask(u64::MAX), !(kill | stop));
6612        assert_eq!(kernel_installed_signal_mask(kill | stop), 0);
6613        let alrm_and_rt64 = (1_u64 << (libc::SIGALRM - 1)) | (1_u64 << 63);
6614        assert_eq!(kernel_installed_signal_mask(alrm_and_rt64), alrm_and_rt64);
6615    }

6707    #[test]
6708    fn kill_targets_only_unambiguous_process_delivery() {
6709        let first = DetTid::from_raw(42);
6710        let second = DetTid::from_raw(43);
6711        assert_eq!(
6712            deterministic_kill_target(&[], None, libc::SIGUSR1),
6713            Err(Errno::ESRCH)
6714        );
6715        assert_eq!(
6716            deterministic_kill_target(&[], Some(true), libc::SIGUSR1),
6717            Ok(None)
6718        );
6719        assert_eq!(
6720            deterministic_kill_target(&[], Some(false), libc::SIGUSR1),
6721            Err(Errno::ESRCH)
6722        );
6723        assert_eq!(
6724            deterministic_kill_target(&[first], None, libc::SIGUSR1),
6725            Ok(Some(first))
6726        );
6727        assert_eq!(
6728            deterministic_kill_target(&[first, second], None, libc::SIGUSR1),
6729            Err(Errno::ENOSYS)
6730        );
6731        assert_eq!(
6732            deterministic_kill_target(&[first, second], None, 0),
6733            Ok(Some(first))
6734        );

6737        assert!(self_sigkill_targets_current_task(
6738            libc::SIGKILL,
6739            Some(process),
6740            None,
6741            process,
6742            first,
6743        ));
6744        assert!(self_sigkill_targets_current_task(
6745            libc::SIGKILL,
6746            None,
6747            Some(first),
6748            process,
6749            first,
6750        ));
6751        assert!(self_sigkill_targets_current_task(
6752            libc::SIGKILL,
6753            Some(process),
6754            Some(first),
6755            process,
6756            first,
6757        ));
6758        for (signal, target_process, target_thread) in [
6759            (libc::SIGTERM, Some(process), Some(first)),
6760            (libc::SIGKILL, Some(DetPid::from_raw(40)), Some(first)),
6761            (libc::SIGKILL, Some(process), Some(second)),
6762            (libc::SIGKILL, None, None),
6763        ] {
6764            assert!(!self_sigkill_targets_current_task(
6765                signal,
6766                target_process,
6767                target_thread,
6768                process,
6769                first,
6770            ));
6771        }
6772    }

6783    #[test]
6784    fn signal_zero_never_notifies_a_target() {
6785        let sender = DetTid::from_raw(42);
6786        let target = DetTid::from_raw(43);
6787        assert!(!should_notify_cross_task_signal(sender, target, 0));
6788        assert!(should_notify_cross_task_signal(
6789            sender,
6790            target,
6791            libc::SIGUSR1
6792        ));
6793        assert!(!should_notify_cross_task_signal(
6794            sender,
6795            sender,
6796            libc::SIGUSR1
6797        ));
6798    }
6799}
